MEETING: Sine Die and First Regular Meeting | DATE: 2013-01-10 | CLIP_ID: 352 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 352

 RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
January 7, 2013
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in “Sine Die” Session on this date at 2:30
p.m. in the Cheyenne Civic Center.  Those present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and
Mr. Valdez.  Also present were Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, and Dan White, City Attorney. 
Following the pledge of allegiance, Mayor Kaysen welcomed those in attendance and stated the
purpose of the session was to ce remonially recognize outgoing Council members for  their years of
service on C ity Council.  He commente d generally on community  leadership and g overning body
achievements; expressed appreciation to outgoing Council members for their time, effort and dedication
to the community, and recognized the supporting roles of families, friends and co-workers.
At Mayor Kaysen’s request, Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, read a ceremonial resolution in its
entirety honoring outgoing Council member Amber Ash, entitled as follows:  “A Ceremonial Resolution
in Recognition of the Honorable Amber Ash for her Dedication and Outstanding Contributions to the
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and its Residents.”  Mayor Kaysen noted Ms. Ash was sworn in as a
County Commissioner earlier in the afternoon and explained, pursuant to state statute, Ms. Ash wa s
ineligible to hold both the position of City Council member and County Commissioner at the same time,
which was why she was not seated at the dais.  He advised Ms. Ash had submitted her resignation as
a member of City Council prior to being sworn in at the County.  Mayor Kaysen presented Ms. Ash with
an engraved plaque noting her 4 years of membership on City Council and thanked her for her service
to the community.  Ms. Ash briefly commented on her years of service, expressed appreciation for being
able to s erve the c ommunity a s a  City  Cou ncil m ember, a nd sta ted s he wa s lo oking fo rward to
continuing her service to the community as a County Commissioner. (#5461)
At Mayor Kaysen’s request, Ms. Intlekofer read a ceremonial resolution in its entirety honoring
outgoing Council member Patrick Collins, entitled as follows:  “A Ceremonial Resolution in
Recognition of the Honorable Patrick Collins for his Dedication and Outstanding Contributions to the
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and its Residents.”  Mayor Kaysen presented Mr. Collins with an engraved
plaque noting his 12 y ears of member ship on Cit y Council and thanked him for  his service  to the
community. M r. Collins briefly  commented on his y ears of servic e as a  City  Council member,
expressing appreciation to the voters of Ward II, co- City Council members who had served with him,
City staff, and members of his family for their support. (#5464)
At Mayor Kaysen’s request, Ms. Intlekofer read a ceremonial resolution in its entirety honoring
outgoing Council member Don Pierson, entitled as follows:  “A Ceremonial Resolution in Recognition
of the Honor able Don Pier son for his Dedic ation and Outstanding  Contributions  to the City  of
Cheyenne, Wyoming, and its Residents.”  Mayor Kaysen presented Mr. Pierson with an engraved plaque
noting his 20 years of membership on City Council and thanked him for his service to the community.
Mr. Pierson briefly commented on his years of service as a City Council member and thanked the voters
of the community for allowing him to serve them. (#5462)
At Mayor Kaysen’s request, Ms.  Intlekofer read a ceremonial resolution in its entirety honoring
outgoing Cou ncil m ember Ja ck R.  Spi ker, e ntitled a s f ollows:  “ A C eremonial Re solution in
Recognition of the Honorable Jack R. Spiker for his Dedication and Outstanding Contributions to the
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and its Residents.”  Mayor Kaysen  presented Mr. Spiker with an engraved
plaque noting his most recent 4 years of membership on City Council and thanked him for his 16 years
of se rvice, b oth a s a  Cou ncil m ember a nd fo rmer Ma yor, to  the  c ommunity.  M r. Spi ker b riefly
commented on his years of service as a City Council member and former Mayor and expressed his
appreciation to City staff, to voters for entrusting their vote in him, and to members of his family for
their support. (#5463)
On behalf of the governing body, Mayor Kaysen expressed appreciation to Mr. Collins for his
leadership as City Council President for 2012 and presented him with an engraved gavel plaque.
Mayor Kaysen announced the First Regular Meeting of the governing body involving newly
elected and continuing members would convene at 3:00 p.m. in the Civic Center.
There being no further business previously noted for t he “Sine Die” Session, Mayor Kaysen
adjourned the meeting at 2:50 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
PUBLISH: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 January 11, 2013
   RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
 
Office of City Clerk
 
January 7, 2013
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne assembled for its First Regular Meeting for 2013
on this date at 3:00 p.m. in the Cheyenne Civic Center.  Purpose of the First Regular Meeting was to
recognize and administer oaths of office to those individuals who were elected, or re-elected, at the most
recent general election to serve as members of the governing body, as well as provide for certain
administrative func tions.  Rod Hott le provided introduc tory comments and ser ved as ma ster of
ceremonies prior to the meeting being officially convened.
Father August Koeune, Pastor, St. Mary’s Cathedral, delivered the invocation.
The Honorable Michael K. Davis, Justice of the Wyoming Supreme Court, administered the oath
of elected office to Richard L. Kaysen as Mayor,  followed by administration of oath of elected office
for the following newly elected and re-elected City Council members:  Jimmy  Valdez and Annette
Williams, Ward I; Bryan M. Cook and Dicky Shanor, Ward II, and Jim Brown and Mike Luna, Ward
III.
Mayor Kaysen officially called the First Regular Meeting to order at 3:10 p.m.  Those present
were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr.
Cook, Mr. L una, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Va ldez and Ms. Wi lliams.  A lso present were Carol
Intlekofer, City Clerk, and Da n White, City Attorney.  Following  the pledg e of allegiance, led by
members of the Mayor’s Youth Council, Mayor Kaysen provided welcoming comments and remarked
on the community role of the governing body and future financial challenges due to the economy.  He
expressed appreciation to those individuals in the community who serve as elected public officials; to
City staff who assisted with the “Sine Die” and current meeting arrangements; to Justice Davis, Father
Koeune, Mr. Hottle, and membe rs of the Ma yor’s Youth Council and Cheyenne Public Employees
Association who participated and assisted with the meeting and reception, to the families of governing
body members for their support, and to Wyoming citizens serving in the military.
Election of City Council President for 2013.  Mayor Kaysen announced, pursuant to City Code,
he would be conducting the election but would not be participating in the vote.  Ms. Broyles moved to
nominate and elect Dr. Rinne as City Council President for 2013, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Mr. Brown
moved that nominations cease, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Motion to cease nominations carried.  Voting
“yes” – all members of the g overning body with the exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not vote.
Motion to nominate and elect Dr. Rinne carried.  Voting “yes”-- all members of the governing body with
the exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not vote.
Election of City Council Vice President for 2013.  Mayor Kaysen announced, pursuant to City
Code, he would be conducting the election but would not be participating in the vote.  Dr. Rinne moved
to nominate and elect Ms. Broyles as City Council Vice President for 2013, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Mr.
Brown moved that nominations cease, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion to cease nominations carried.
Voting “yes”-- all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not
vote.  Motion to nominate and elect Ms. Broyles carried.  Voting “yes”-- all members of the governing
body with the exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not vote.
Approval of Appointment of City  officials (pursuant to Titl e 2 of the  Code of the City  of
Cheyenne) as follows:  Matthew J. A shby, Planning Services Director; James Edward Elias, Public
Works Director; Paul S. Galeotos, Municipal Court Judge; Randy  K. Hays, Assistant City Treasurer
(Board of Public Utilities); Lois A. Huff, City Treasurer; Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk/Assistant City
Treasurer; Ronnald J. Jeffrey, Juvenile Court Judge; Brian N. Kozak, Chief of the Chey enne Police
Department; James A. Martin, Chief of the Cheyenne Fire and Rescue Department; Mark T. Moran,
Municipal Court Judge; Richard L. Parish, Parks and Recreation Director, and Daniel E. White, City
Attorney.  Mr. Valdez moved to approve the appointments “en masse”, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Motion
carried.  Voting “yes”-- all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Kaysen who
did not vote (Mayoral appointments).  Following announcement of the vote, Justice Davis administered
the oath of appointment to the above listed City officials.
Following the benediction by Father Koeune, Mayor Kaysen announced that refreshments would
be served in the Civic Center lobby immediately following the meeting.
There being no further business previously noted for the First Regular Meeting, the meeting was
officially adjourned at 3:33 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 January 11, 2013

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 January 14, 2013
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr.
Valdez and Ms. Williams.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer, and
Dan White, City Attorney. 
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Mr. Brown moved to approve Consent Agenda items, seconded
by Mr. Valdez.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on December 20, 2012.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
[CA] Minutes from Sine Die Session of the Governing Body on January 7, 2013.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
[CA] Minutes from the First Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on January 7, 2013.  Motion
(per Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown and Mr. Valdez each declared a conflict of interest (business interest in vouchers
payable to Ameri-Tech HVAC Services and Radio Station KYOY F M respectively) and vacated
Council Chambers.  Mr. Allen moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by
Ms. Williams.  Mo tion carried.  Vo ting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present in
Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown and Mr. Valdez returned to
Council Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hea ring to determine w hether compliance of anne xation
conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exist for a parcel of land being situated in a portion of
the east half of the east half of Section 20, and a portion of the southwest quarter of the southwest
quarter of the northwest quarter of Section 21, Township 14 North, Range 66 West of the 6th P.M.,
Laramie County, Wyoming (located approximately 1500 feet north of the intersection of Converse
Avenue and Ogden Road).  Jim Flesher, Planning and Development Office, reviewed annexation
requirements and criteria pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-402, stating  that the proposed a rea to be
annexed deviates subs tantially from the City ’s PlanCheyenne, Unified Development Code and
Master Transportation Plan documents.  He reviewed staff findings with regard to compliance, or
non-compliance, with Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-402 (a) (i) through (vi).  Mr. Flesher advised of areas of
non-compliance involving c riteria outlined in W.S. § 15-1-40 2 (a) (i) through (iii), noting the
following topics: Storm water drainage facility involving the proposed annexed area, which would
be located outside of the area to be annexed; inability to utilize the limited “strips” of open public
space area; street(s) connectivity problems relative to existing roadways and the transportation plan,
and “piecemeal” development affecting long range planning, zoning and future property uses.  He
stated findings involving conditions outlined in W.S. § 15-1-402 (iv through vi) had been met with
regard to prope rty contig uity, franchises being  in place to se rve the  proper ty, and anne xation
summary notification requirements.  Frank Cole, United W estern Corporation, petitioner f or
annexation, stated that the first three items (i.e. findings previously noted by Mr. Flesher as not being
in compliance) pertained to platting problems and not annexation.  He requested postponement of
a companion agenda item (#21 - Resolution), which had been referred to Public Services Committee
under the approved Consent Agenda motion earlier in the meeting, as he would not be available to
attend the Committee meeting.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Cole verified the property to be annexed is on the
east side of Converse Ave. a nd was not the Sce nic property development a rea located w est of
Converse Ave.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Flesher confirmed the first three criteria items contained in Wyo.
Stat. § 15-1-402 were those that have not been met; the last three criteria items have been met.  Upon
inquiry, Brandon Cammarata, Planning and Development Director, advised a preliminary plat related
to the proposed development was postponed at the recent Planning Commission meeting for further
review to addre ss concerns, which include  the open spa ce planned for the ar ea.  Ma yor Kaysen
advised of city code language relative to items for Committee referral not being eligible for removal
from th e Consent Ag enda, a nd indicated that a t the next P ublic Service s Commi ttee mee ting
members could consider recommending postponement of the affiliated Resolution agenda item at
that time, per Mr. Cole’s request.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Cole acknowledged that would be acceptable
to him.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met,
Mayor Kaysen closed the public hearing.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive comments and input for a transfer of
ownership for a retail liquor license application filed with the City Clerk's Office for RMH Franchise
Corporation d/b/a Applebe e’s Ne ighborhood Grill & B ar, 1401 D ell Rang e B lvd., Chey enne,
Wyoming ( License cur rently held by  Concord Ne ighborhood Corporation d/b/a Applebe e’s
Neighborhood Grill &  Ba r, 1401 De ll Range  Blvd.,  Cheyenne, Wy oming).  There  being  no
comments or input and purpose of the public hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the
hearing and referred the application to Finance Committee.
 
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, Section 5.1.2 Zoning

ORDINANCE - Granting a Franchise by the City of Cheyenne to Cheyenne Light, Fuel and

(#3966)

Utilities, Federal Highway Administration funds).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount

[CA] Consideration of Bid #R-8-13 for furnishing one new 2012-2013 backhoe loader from Titan
Machinery, Cheyenne, WY, for the Concrete Division (2011-2014 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $99,208.18). 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #M-7-13 for a contract between the C ity o f C heyenne and Inman
Roofing, Inc., Cheyenne, Wyoming, for roof replacement at the Neighborhood Facility Building --
referred to Finance Committee. 
Announcement was made of the Appointment of Jane M. France to the Community Action
of Laramie County, Inc. Board of Directors, to be confirmed January 28, 2013.
Announcement was made of the Re-Appointments of Shane Moore, Charles R. Livermont
and Gale Shenefelt to the Mayor’s Council for People with Disabilities, to be confirmed January 28,
2013.
Announcement was made that due to the Martin Luther King, J r. Holiday on Monday,
January 21, 2013, regularly scheduled City Council Committee meetings will instead be held in the
Municipal Building, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Room 122, Cheyenne, Wyoming, as follows: Finance
Committee will meet Tuesday, January 22, 2013 at 11:00 a.m. and Public Services Committee will
meet Wednesday, January 23, 2013 at Noon. 
M. Lee Hasenauer, Laramie County Commissioner, requested information on the status of
the annual review of the City’s UDC (Unified Development Code).  Matt Ashby, Planning Services
Director, advised comments have been received from the public, staff and City Council members
and staff is in the process of compiling the comments for the Planning Commission meeting to be
held Feb. 19, 2013.  Mayor Kaysen briefly advised of the process involving the governing body after
the review by the Planning Commission.
Welcome was extended to Amber Ash, M. Lee Hasenauer, and Buck Holmes, Laramie
County Commissioners, who were in attendance at the meeting.
There being  no further  business to come befo re the governing body , the meeting  was
officially adjourned at 6:40 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 January 28, 2013
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr.
Valdez and Ms. Williams.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer, and
Dan White, City Attorney. 
Mayor Kaysen noted clerical corrections on the numbering of items (2) contained on the first
page of the meeting agenda.  Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]
are considered to be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There
will be no separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and
support by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be
considered in its normal sequence on the agenda.)  Mr. Brown moved to approve Consent Agenda

Metropolitan Planning Organization (MPO) and Fehr & Peers for 2010 Travel Demand Forecasting
Model Calibration (MPO Planning funds).  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Brown moved
to approve in an amount not to exceed $78,760.00, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Upon inquiry, Tom
Mason, MPO Director, explained the role of the MPO Policy Committee in budgeting, reviewing
and setting priorities of street projects, and explained that if a  project isn’t funded for a specific
budget year, it may be reconsidered for possible inclusion on a futur e project list.  He noted the
Committee meets annually to discuss the MPO budget as well as on a quarterly basis.  In response
to query concerning the status of Parsley Blvd. being included on a project list relative to a past
study, Mr. Mason advised that a 2004 study looked at the street network generally for all of south
Cheyenne, including this area, and stated that Parsley Blvd. will be looked at in the future in more
detail.  He explained the contract will assist with traffic calibration information.  Motion carried.

appointment).

Council for People with Disabilities.  Mr. Br own moved to appr oved, seconded by Mr. Allen.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor
Kaysen who did not vote (Mayoral appointments).

Michael A. Metzler, and  Jason Stephen to the Contractor Licensing Board; and William C. Beaman

Announcement was ma de of the  Appointment of J ohn G. Kneppe r to the B oard of

encroachment on any U.P properties or the Belvoir Ranch.

 Carol Intlekofer, MMC

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2013-02-11 | CLIP_ID: 358 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 358

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 February 11, 2013
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS -
Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and Ms.
Williams.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City
Attorney.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the desig nation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other
members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal
sequence on the agenda.)  Mr. Allen moved to approve Consent Agenda items, seconded by Dr. Rinne.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
[CA] Minutes from Special Me eting of the Gove rning Body on J anuary 28, 2013.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
[CA] Minutes from Reg ular Meeting of the Gover ning Body on J anuary 28, 2013.  Mo tion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Ameri-Tech
HVAC Services) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Valdez moved to approve payment of vouchers
as presented, seconded by Mr. L una.  U pon inquiry regarding a vouche r payable to Child Support
Services, Lois Huff, City Treasurer, clarified these are deductions from employee pay checks pursuant
to court issued garnishments.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Huff and Rick Parish, Parks and Recreation Director,
provided information on hockey referee services and number of games involved relative to vouchers
payable to Michael Cote ($245) and Tim McGuire ($425).  Mr. Parish advised hockey leagues have
different pay schedules between score keepers and referees, and reviewed those leagues whose players
travel for games.  In response to inquiry, Ms. Huff provided information available on a voucher payable
to Cowboy Supply House ($677.30) for trash can liners.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members
of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown
returned to Council Chambers.
ORDINANCE - Pursua nt to Section 2.2.1 Zoning  Map Amendme nt, Section 5.1.2 Z oning
Districts E stablished, and Sec tion 5.1.3 Official Z oning Ma p of the Unifie d Deve lopment Code,
changing the Zoning Classification from CB Community Business District to MUB Mixed-Use with
Business Emphasis District for Lot 4, Block 486, Original City, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the
southeast corner of the intersec tion of Hug ur Avenue and E. 12th S treet, approxim ately 500 fee t
northwest of the  intersec tion of L ogan Ave nue and N ationway).  Reporting  for Public S ervices
Committee, Mr. Allen moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Motion
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#3968)
ORDINANCE - Pursua nt to Section 2.2.1 Zoning  Map Amendme nt, Section 5.1.2  Zoning
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Of ficial Z oning Ma p of the Unifie d Development Code,
changing the zoning classification from HR-2 High Density Residential – Developing District to MR-2
Medium Density Residential – Developing District f or Lots 1-6, Bloc k 1, and L ots 1-6, Bloc k 2,
Diamond Estates, F ourth Filing , Chey enne, Wy oming (located at the southwest cor ner of  the
intersection of Rio Verde Street and Pierce Avenue).  Mr. Allen moved to approve on third and final
reading, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing
body. (#3969)
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2 Zoning Map,
of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code, changing the zoning classification from A-1 Agricultural
and Rural Residential District to HR-2 High Density Residential – Developing District for a tract of
land situated in a portion of the East Half of the East Half (E½E½) of Section 20, T.14N., R.66W., 6th
P.M., Chey enne, L aramie County , Wy oming, to be know n as Scenic  Su bdivision (located
approximately 150 fee t west of the inter section of Conver se Avenue and Ogden Road).  B randon
Cammarata, Planning and Development Director, advised additional information was received on this
date from Scenic Development Group relative to this item as well as agenda item #20(a) (Preliminary
Plat), and stated the Group has requested a letter be read into the record on its behalf as they were not
able to attend the meeting (letter on file with Planning and Development and City Clerk offices).  Upon
inquiry by Mayor Kaysen to members of the governing body and there being no objection raised, Mayor
Kaysen re quested Mr. Cam marata re ad the letter  submitt ed by Scenic De velopment Group.  Mr .
Cammarata read the letter (dated Feb. 11, 2013) which explained Scenic Development’s position that
it may not be able to move forward with its apartment project as they are not the land owners of the
parcel and conditions that are being recommended by the Public Services Committee, associated with
the pr eliminary plat, are no t a greeable to t he la nd ow ner ( i.e. Co le fa mily).  Ca sey Pa lma, St eil
Surveying Services, agent for the applicant, Scenic Development, encouraged Council members to
review the conditions being recommended for appropriateness, and suggested that perhaps there might
be some wa y to find a flexible compromi se between the par ties.  Ed Murra y commented on the
preliminary plat with Mr. Allen raising a point of order that discussion should remain pertinent to the
zoning classific ation item. May or Ka ysen re quested Mr. Murra y provide his comments on the
preliminary plat when that item would be on the floor later in the meeting.  Mr. Allen reported there was
no recommendation on the item from the Public Services Committee.  Mr. Allen moved to approve on
second reading, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Comment followed regarding discussion at the Public Services
Committee meeting regarding wording in S ection 7. of the ordinance which reads: “ ... That this
ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and publication and upon the official filing
of the affiliated Scenic Subdivision final plat map with the County Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of
Deeds for Laramie County, Wyoming.”  Mr. Allen moved to amend Section 7. to read as follows,
seconded by Mr. Luna:  “ ... That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and
publication and  upon the approval and publication of an ordinance annexing said property.”  Discussion
followed and included the following topics: Customary agenda procedure involving annexation, zoning
and platting items that are affiliated, and concerns that the usual procedure was not being followed for
the Scenic Development items; support for the apartment project; timing of actions to be taken on the
zoning classification relative to a nnexation and preliminary /final plat; support for a nd against the
proposed amendment, and possible postponement of the item.  During discussion and upon inquiry, Mr.
Cammarata provided information pertaining to a previously adopted resolution determining conditions
for annexation had been met (re: Scenic Development project property) relative to delayed introduction
of the annexation ordinance.  Motion to amend failed (due to a tie vote).  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Mr.
Cook, Mr. Luna, Mr. Shanor and Ms. Williams.  Voting “no” --  Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Dr. Rinne,
Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Comments followed in non-support of the zone cha nge due to the
failed motion to amend and changing the zone classification prior to the property being annexed, and
timing of actions to enable annexation and the zone change to be completed together.  Upon inquiry,
Mr. Palma advised general discussions have been held on whether a property to be annexed should have
the zoning determined prior to annexation, and expressed his opinion that it would be appropriate to
either postpone the item or deny it and resubmit it with the final plat.  Main motion failed.  Voting “no”
-- all members of the governing body.

Laramie County, Wyoming, to be known as the Bluffs, 9  Filing (located approximately 1,500 feetth

to March 11, 2013, seconded by Mr. Brown.  It was noted postponement is at the request of the

according to the provisions of W.S. §16-4-112 and 16-4-113 -- referred to Finance Committee (sponsor
-- Mr. Brown).

Liquor License No. 39 issued by the City of Cheyenne to Stillwater, Inc., dba ‘Mingles Bar’ -- referred

[CA] RESOLUTION - Approving the West Lincolnway Streetscape plans (landscape and fence) --
referred to  Pub lic Ser vices Co mmittee (s ponsors - - M r. Al len, Ms.  Broyles, Mr . V aldez an d M s.
Williams).
Preliminary Plat for Scenic Subdivision, a tract of land situated in a portion of the East Half of
the East Half (E½E½) of Section 20, T.14N., R.66W., 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (located
north of and adjacent to the existing WalMart store) (Annexation in process).  Ed Murray, vicinity
landowner, spoke in opposition to approval of the preliminary plat.  He expressed his concerns with the
proposed Scenic development pa rcel as submitted by  the prope rty owner in conjunction with the
annexation application; convoluted procedure involving the parcel’s affiliated annexation, zoning and
platting processes, and lack of communication by the property owner (Cole family) and failure to look
at comprehensive and “big picture” planning for the area.  He reviewed his further concerns relative to
Exhibit D, network transportation plan involving the area, that was submitted with the initial annexation
application, stating the transpor tation plan ref lected in th e exhibit is  inconsistent with the Master
Transportation Plan incorporated into the Cit y’s PlanCheyenne.  During his comments, Mr. Murra y
displayed and referred to an enlar ged copy of Exhibi t D (map) a nd a diagram depicting the Master
Transportation Plan map portion from PlanCheyenne for the area, and discussed north-south and east-
west collector roads in Section 20.  He noted that the north-south collector road reflects the extension
of Rue Terre north to Storey Blvd., and that specific land uses (zoning classifications) affecting the
Murray property, as currently depicted in PlanCheyenne, show an emphasis on mixed use commercial
and mixed use residential.  Describing the Murray property as “the donut hole” situated at the cross-
roads of the collector roads, Mr. Murray stated that changing the City’s transportation network plan, as
proposed by the property  owner pursuant to Exhi bit D, will have a de trimental effect on Murr ay
property development plans.  He explained his failed attempts to discuss the issues with the property
owner; spoke in support of proper community growth and good community stewardship, and noted the
City’s vested financial interests in Storey Ave. and Converse Ave. roadway extensions and associated
infrastructure.  He indicated that a recent Exhibit C, submitted by the applicant for the preliminary plat,
has resulted in three staff conditions being proposed in conjunction with the preliminary plat.  In
concluding his comments, Mr. Murray provided copies of Section 4.3.2, Transportation Network Plan,
contained in the City’s Unified Development Code (UDC), reciting the wording outlined in Section a.,
General Requirements, and provisions # 1. 3. and 4.; stated his opinion that compliance with the rules
of the UDC have not been met, and reiterated his opposition to approval of the preliminary plat.  Ed
Cole, Cole family, landowner, advised that what Scenic Development is proposing makes no difference
on how the final transportation plan is adopted for Section 20.  He briefly reviewed history of the City’s
acquisition of property from the Cole family along Storey and Converse, noting the City allowed four
entrances into the land, one of which involves the Scenic Development area, and that planning staff now
want to allow two other entrances, which was not part of the original plan.  He advised the Cole family
and City staff do not agree on the way the streets should be aligned; commented on the slow ra te of
commercial development in the area, and spoke in f avor of the quality project proposed by Scenic
Development and that it is a g ood project for the City , noting the time a nd money that Scenic ha s
invested.  He stated his belief that the street alignment issue does not make any difference in whether
this is approved.  Casey Palma, Steil Surveying Services, clarified there are four proposed conditions
attached with the recommendation on the preliminary plat and that one of the conditions should address
some concerns as it is a revision to the traffic impact study and analysis.  He advised that Scenic is
favorable to re vising the tra nsportation impact study and networ k plan prior to the f inal plat being
submitted.  Mr. Murray clarified his previous comments were made to specifically address concerns
involving the project area as well as for betterment of the community through comprehensive area
planning that includes the transportation network, instead of piecemeal incremental developments.
James Perrot Macneil spoke in opposition to t he preliminary plat as the prope rty has not y et been
annexed, and against all community development generally.  Mr. Allen moved to acknowledge receipt
of the preliminary plat with staff conditions #1 through 3 as listed in the 02-01-2013 memo from the
City Planning and Development Department, and requiring a revised Traffic Impact Study to provide
consistency with the Transportation Network as required under Section 4.3.2 (re: UDC), seconded by
Mr. Brown.  Comments followed regarding the convoluted nature of issues and procedures involving
development of the property; landowners’ and developers’ reliance on PlanCheyenne and the UDC
(ordinance) tra nsportation require ments; sugg estion that customary  vs. non-customary  proce dures
involving the process affiliated with preliminary plats, annexations and zone chang es be reviewed;

liquor licenses (retail, restaurant, limited retail, resort, bar and grill licenses, and microbrewery permits)
filed with the City Clerk’s Office.
Under other business:
Tom Lindsey commented on lack of development of the “downtown hole” off of Lincolnway
and suggested a multi-purpose building be built on the sit e, including an underground parking area,
which could house a health food restaurant, office space, short and long term housing, and outdoor area
on the top of the building for activities and possibly a garden area.
Dennis Pa checo spo ke in s upport of  de veloping the  “ downtown h ole”, a nd dis cussed h is
concerns with poor road conditions within the City.  He specifically referenced the vicinity of Pershing
Blvd. and Logan Ave. as an example.  Mayor Kaysen provided information on street improvements at
19th/20th and Converse t hat will begin this sum mer and extend west to the ceme tery complex on
Pershing, and noted ther e are also plans for improveme nts on Logan as funding becomes available.
Comment was made thanking Mr. Pacheco for expressing his concerns.
County Commission Chairman Troy Thompson was recognized as being in attendance at the
meeting.
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was officially
adjourned at 7:28  p.m.
 Submitted by,
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2013-02-25 | CLIP_ID: 361 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 361

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 February 25, 2013
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS -
Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and Ms.
Williams.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City
Attorney.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the desig nation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other
members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal
sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Brown, and pursuant to support received by Dr. Rinne
and Ms. Broyles, agenda item #11 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  U pon request by Ms.
Broyles and pursua nt to  support received by Mr. B rown and Dr. Rinne, ag enda item #15( c) was
removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon request by Mr. Brown and pursuant to support received by
Mr. Allen and Ms. Broyles, agenda item #15(a) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Allen
moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on February 11, 2013.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Shanor moved to c hange the a genda order of the day  to bri ng forward Item #14(a), a
proposed preliminary plat, immediately prior to Item #8 to allow for consecutive discussion and action
affiliated with an ordinance on second reading for re-zoning the property (Item #8) pertaining to The
Bluffs, 9  Filing, located a pproximately 1,500 fee t north of t he intersection of Conver se Ave. andth
Ogden Road, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Mayor Kaysen clarified debate on the motion was limited to
topic of the motion only.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Ameri-Tech
HVAC Services) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Valdez moved to approve payment of vouchers
as presented, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Upon inquiry regarding a voucher payable to Chase Park Grants,
LLC, Matt Ashby, Planning Services Director, advised Chase Park services were obtained to write grant
applications and submit them on behalf  of the City  and that funding  awarded is assisting  with the
Capitol Basin West Edge Project.  It was noted that seeking additional funding opportunities leverages
6  penny sales tax dollars for the project.  Mr. Ashby provided information on the dollar amount ofth
grants that have been awarded to date through Chase Park’s efforts.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” --
all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote,
Mr. Brown returned to Council Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive comments and input for renewal of existing
City liquor licenses (retail, restaurant, limited retail, resort, bar and grill licenses, and microbrewery
permits) filed with the City Clerk’s Office.  William Overling expressed concerns regarding renewal
of the retail liquor license held by Cadillac Ranch Bar, Inc. and cited Wyo. Stat. §12-4-104(b)(i), which
states that if evidence is presented at the hearing that the welfare of the people residing in the vicinity
of the proposed license premises shall be adversely affected then the license should not be renewed.
He advised he and other residents in the neighborhood adjacent to the bar have issues with noise levels,
citing EPA levels; parking congestion, including vehicle lights; lack of signage directing bar patrons
to an off-site nearby parking area; debris found in their yards and on the streets; fighting, vandalism,
and undesirable activities by bar customers affecting the area.  He reviewed incidences where police
officers have had to respond, and submitted a petition signe d by residents in the area requesting the
liquor license for Cadillac Ranch either be revoked or not be renewed.  Kevin Beers, President, Cadillac
Ranch Bar, reviewed past communications he has had with Mr. Overling and residents in the vicinity
and stated his openness to further communications; advised he has voluntarily met with police officers
regularly to ensure  c ompliance with laws and stated he is trying to be a good neighbor.  He further
reviewed noise level testing conducted by police officers, noting it has never exceeded the allowable
decibel levels, and explained that during activities surrounding last year’s Cheyenne Frontier Days time
frame he ha d cancelled plans for a band to per form outside of the building  due to neig hborhood
concerns.  In response to Mr. Over ling’s comments, he stated he  is looking into installing a sound
barrier to help with vehicle lights and noise issues, noting cost expense and referencing a raised fence
he had previously built to help address complaints, and that corrections to signage informing patrons
of the off-site parking area will be made.  He stated he is sensitive to neighbors’ concerns and is trying
to rectify them, however, he has no control over patrons and their activities after the business closes and
they are off the premises.  Rebecca Schwab and Stephanie Forney, advising they live in close proximity
to the bar, each discussed their concerns the bar’s effect has had on the neighborhood.  Topics discussed
included: Fighting, parking and noise issues; undesirable activities and concerns affecting their family
and personal property, and the notification process when the bar was first approved for its license.  Mike
Dieters, r epresentative of  Pr evention Ma nagement o f L aramie Cou nty a nd local su bstance a buse
professional, discussed social alcohol influence factors affecting underage drinking, and family and
individual effects of alcohol abuse.  Upon inquiry regarding violations within the past year at D.T.’s
lounge on Lincolnway, Officer Steve Neisen, Cheyenne Police Department, advised compliance issues,
including better signage involving designated smoking areas (per city code), have been corrected by the
owner.  Co mment followed on underage sales compliance checks and inquiry was made whether
licensee representatives were present at the meeting for the following establishments:  Albany, Old
Chicago, Keg ‘n Kork, A nderson’s Other Corne r, Redwood, Scooter s, D.T.’s Liquormart, Re-o-na
Sushi Restaurant, and Olive Garden.  Bryan Grzegorczyk, Redwood Lounge owner, acknowledged
failure of an underage service compliance check at the establishment, and explained th e i ncident
involved mistaken identity of a customer by staff and that the employee has been re-trained.  Upon
inquiry regarding compliance check failures at Re-o-na Sushi Restaurant, Officer Niesen advised he met
with the manag er, noting a lang uage barrier affected previous communications, and stated a lcohol
server training requirements had been further explained and employees are now TIPS trained.  Upon
inquiry regarding the building occupancy capacity within the Cadillac Ranch Bar, Mr. Beers advised
Fire Dept. staff have checked the capacity level and he explained how the occupancy level is monitored.
There being no further comments or discussion, Mayor Kaysen stated the purpose of the public hearing
had been met and referred the renewals to Finance Committee for review.  He noted the Committee will
meet on Monday, March 4 at 11:00 a.m. in Room 122, Municipal Building.
Preliminary Plat for The Bluffs, 9  Filing, a tract of land situated in a portion the East Half ofth
the East Half (E½E½) of Section 20 and a portion of the Southwest Quarter of the Southwest Quarter
of the Northwest Quarter (SW¼ SW¼ NW¼) of Section 21, T.14N., R.66W., 6th P.M ., Cheyenne,
Laramie County, Wyoming (Annexation in process) ( located approximately 1,500 feet north of the
intersection of Converse Avenue and Ogden Road).  Due to a family relationship, Dan White, City
Attorney, declared a conflict of legal interest and excused himself from the dais, noting that any legal
advice requested for the preliminary plat (Agenda Item #14(a)) as well as two subsequent ordinances
on the agenda (Agenda Items #8 and #9) would be provided by John Ridge, Deputy City Attorney.  Mr.
White then vacated Council Ch ambers wi th M r. Rid ge joi ning the  g overning bo dy on  the  da is.
Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Allen stated there was no recommendation on the item
from the Committee.  Mr. Allen moved to deny acknowledgment of the preliminary plat, seconded by
Mr. Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning  Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, Zoning
Districts established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified Development
Code, changing the zoning classification from A-1 Agricultural and Rural Residential District to MR-2
Medium Density Residential – Developing District for a tract of land situated in a portion the East Half
of the East Half (E½E½) of Section 20 and a portion of the Southwest Quarter of the Southwest Quarter
of the Northwest Quarter (SW¼ SW¼ NW¼) of Section 21, T.1 4N., R.66W., 6th P.M., C heyenne,
Laramie County, Wyoming, to be know n as the B luffs, 9  Filing (located approximately 1,500 feetth
north of the intersection of Converse Avenue and Ogden Road).  Mr. Allen moved to approve on second
reading, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Comments followed on procedural concerns with approving the
zone change prior to anne xation of the prope rty being completed.  Mr. A llen moved to amend by
changing the wording contained in Section 7. to read as follows, seconded by Mr. Shanor: “ ... That this
ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and publication and upon successful filing
of a n a nnexation or dinance wi th t he Cou nty Cle rk a nd Ex- officio R egister o f D eeds fo r Laramie
County, Wyoming.”  Discussion followed and inc luded the following: Procedur al order of a ctions
involving affiliated agenda items pertaining to annexations, re-zoning, preliminary plats, and final plats;
amendment allowing the re-zoning ordinance to move forward and be “in the wings” when annexation
becomes effective; comments in support as well as non-support of the amendment; concerns with
approving a zone change prior to annexation and affiliated plat actions and taking re-zoning action when
future annexation and development may not occur, and linking annexation and zoning actions together
time-wise.  Upon inquiry, Matt Ashby, Planning Services Director, advised at this time staff has not
weighed-in on a definite position on having the zone change effective with a pending/future annexation,
and raised a concern that, potentially, a zone change could be “hanging out there” for a long period of
time without annexation moving forward in a timely manner.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” --
all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Brown, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen
voting “no”.  Comment was made that, hopefully, affiliated platting issues could be worked out.  Main
motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception of
Mr. Brown and Mr. Valdez voting “no”.
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Z oning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, Z oning
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified Development
Code, changing the zoning classification from P Public to NB Neighborhood Business for Lot 1, Block
4, Storey Boulevard Extension, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located east of and adjacent to Converse Avenue,
approximately one-quarter mile south of Storey Boulevard).  Mike Langhoff commented that when the
current owners acquired the property they were aware of the public zoning classification, and stated he
was opposed to the zone change.  Mr. Allen moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Ms.
Williams.  Comments followed regarding the need to change the zoning as the property is no longer
owned by a public entity.  Upon inquiry, John Ridge, Deputy City Attorney, stated his belief that the
zoning classification should be changed, noting that without making a change results in no property
value being realized by the developer/owner.  Mayor Kaysen clarified the purpose of a “P” public zone
is for properties owned by the City or public entity .  Upon inq uiry, Matt Ashby, Planning Services
Director, verified within the code ( i.e. Section 5.6.1(c), Unified Development Code), the context of
Public District Standards states “If property or a portion of property with this classification is sold or
transferred to a nongovernmental entity, the classification shall be changed before the property can be
utilized.”  It was noted the property, previously owned by the City, is now owned by a consortium of
private investors.  Reference was made to a past attempt to cha nge the property’s zone type by the
investors and that the proposed change to neighborhood business may be more appealing to the vicinity.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Luna
voting “no”.  Mr. Ridge vacated the dais and Mayor Kaysen noted for the record that Mr. White had
resumed his seat on the dais.
RESOLUTION - Re- appropriating the  Fisca l Year  2013 budg et of the City  of Chey enne
according to the provisions of W.S. §16-4-112 and 16-4-113.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
(#5477)
RESOLUTION - D irecting the  City  At torney to p etition th e First Judicial D istrict Co urt,
Laramie County, Wyoming, for an order suspending, for a period not to exceed thirty (30) days, Retail
Liquor License No. 39 issued by the City of Cheyenne to Stillwater, Inc., dba ‘Mingles Bar’.  Anthony
Reyes, attorney with the law firm of Riske and Salisbury, representing Stillwater, Inc., advised since
the Finance Committee meeting negotiations through City staff and the City Attorney’s office have been
held and a settlement agreement has been reached.  He stated by reaching the agreement, the licensee
certainly does not condone underage drinking and they are not trying to skirt their responsibilities in any
way, and that by entering into the agreement it is more economical to Mingles and the City than to
proceed with a District Court hearing.  Mr. Reyes noted that under the provisions of the agreement,
Mingles Bar will close completely for 2 consecutive weeks, which is a substantial financial hardship,
and would then be on probation for 6 months.  He further advised that Mingles voluntarily initiated a
partial (bar area) business closure on Feb. 1 and 2 to provide alcohol server re-training to its staff; that
a new person has been hired to review surveillance tapes relative to legal age sales compliance, and that
all employees associated with the violations have been terminated.  Mr. Reyes requested approval of
a substitute resolution, which addr esses the set tlement agreement, and expressed his belief tha t the
compromise was fair to both parties, stating that rather than being an adversary in court the licensee
wants to partner with the City to curb underage drinking.  Upon inquiry, he reviewed a provision of the
agreement addressing any future c itations for violations of Wy oming Statutes or municipal c odes
regarding underage sales, possession or consumption in or upon the premises of Mingles Bar, which
would make the settlement agreement null and void, and advised all employees were sent to TIPS re-
training on Feb. 5 .  He clarified that the former manager is still part owner of the business but is notth
involved in day-to-day operations.  Upon inquiry, he provided information on the number of employees
the business has and t heir duties; type of se curity and over sight in plac e (i.e. cameras, doormen,
managers); identification scanners that are being researched for acquisition to assist, in addition to
visual inspection of proof of identification, in detecting false I.D.s, and time frame when the owners
became aware that their liquor license ma y be in troubl e due to t he number of violations that had
occurred.  Upon inquiry, Terry Wilson, one of the owners of Mingles, advised the building occupancy
capacity of the bar is over 100 persons; that karaoke music is sometimes held on Fri. and Sat. nights;
confirmed that during the 2 week closure the entire establishment would be closed; reviewed elements
of identification scanners that are being researched for compatibility with such issues as military and
out-of-state I.D.’s; noted his potential openness to the possibility of hiring off-duty police officers on
the weekends; provided information on individuals comprising corporate ownership of the business,
and explained what entailed a draft beer special as advertised on the business’ website.  Reporting for
Finance Committee, Mr. Brown moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Mr. Brown moved to amend
by substitute resolution which includes a propose d settlement ag reement referenced as Exhibit A,
seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Upon inquiry, Mr. White provided a summarization of settlement agreement
provisions in which Mingles would voluntarily close all operations for 14 consecutive days, and which
addresses the scenario of any future violations regarding sales to or possession by an underage person
within the designated time frames set out in the agreement that could result in the City moving forward
with a District Court filing to request suspension of the license for a period of not more than 30 days.
Mr. White confirmed that pr ovisions of the ag reement perta in to both the bar a nd packa ge store
operations.  Chief Brian Kozak, Cheyenne Police Dept., commented that the violation restrictions in
the proposed a greement perta in to underag e alc ohol violat ions and not all laws involving liquor.
Discussion followed and inc luded the following topics: Zero tolerance involving underage sales by
licensees; h ow t he t ime frame for b usiness cl osure wa s d etermined; co ncern wi th n egotiating a
settlement involving violations of law; support for as well as against the proposed amendment involving
a settlement agreement; agreement providing for an immediate penalty vs. time frame involving a court
process and unknown ce rtainty of any  penalty that may  be i mposed through a cour t decision, and
procedural scenario if the r esolution as orig inally submitt ed, and not  the substitute, was adopted.
During discussion and upon inquiry, Mr. White confirmed that if the resolution as originally presented
was passed and a petition seeking suspension was fil ed with District Court, and assuming the court
agreed and an order was issued suspending the liquor license for a specified time frame, Mingles could
continue to remain open during the suspension time frame and conduct non-liquor related business
operations.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Reyes advised Mingles would not be willing to agree to change wording
in the agreement to indicate that violations include any violation of City ordinance, explaining that since
underage alcohol sales infractions is the issue that has put Mingles in the current situation to begin with,
the proposed violation provisions in the agreement is what has been agreed upon.  Discussion continued
on the 2 consecutive week closure; effects of any violation under the terms of the agreement; whether
approving of an agreement would be setting a precedent; identifying an effective deterrent as a penalty
and handling any future similar activity by other licensees on an individual basis; whether a different
closure time period would be considered by the licensee; unknown consequences involving pursuit of
a court filing, and the agreement negotiation process and involved parties.  During discussion and upon
inquiry, Mr. Reyes stated that during negotiation discussions the dates for the 2 week closure time frame
(March 18 thru March 31, 2013) we re requested by Mingles and they are not willing to chang e the
decided upon time frame at this time.  Upon inquiry whether any communication had been made with
District Court, Mr. W hite verified there has bee n no communication as it is inappropriate  for a ny
discussion on the matter to be held with a district court judge due to the potential filing that might be
before them in court.  Mayor Kaysen provided information that negotiations were conducted between
respective legal counsel for each party, and Mr. White reviewed the history of the negotiations held with
Mingles and how the proposed settlement was reached.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body with the exception of Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook and Ms. Williams voting
“no”.  Main motion to adopt as a mended by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body. (#5478)
Following a brief recess, Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at 8:20 p.m.
[CA] RESOLUTION - Approving the Cheyenne Area Street Enhancement Toolbox.  Mot ion (per
Consent Agenda) carried. (#5479)
RESOLUTION - A pproving the West L incolnway Streetscape plans (la ndscape and fe nce).
Darren Rudloff, Visit Cheyenne, spoke in support of the planned enhancements, noting the location as
a major gateway entrance into the community and major business improvements that have occurred over
the past few years, and advised the project has the support of many entities and organizations, such as
U.P.R.R., WYDOT, Visit Cheyenne, Cheyenne LEADS, Cheyenne Chamber of Commerce, Boys and
Girls Club and Ty rrell-Doyle.  He a dvised of outside funding and assistance pa rticipation and
commented that the City’s portion for annual maintenance expenses is estimated to be $11,000.  Dale
Steenbergen, President/CEO, Greater Cheyenne Chamber of Commerce, stated that many individuals
have been working on this project for several years and expressed concern with a proposed amendment
to the resolution that would reduce the amount of landscaping.  He spoke in support of maintaining the
original amount of landsca ping, noting the C ity should meet the same r equirements as it places on
others when improving or developing property, and commented on West Lincolnway being “the front
door” to the community.  Gene Burchett, chair, Kiwanis Landscape Committee, provided a brief history
of the Committee and s poke in support of the proje ct and the ne ed to complete Phase  I  of the
enhancements.  He noted there is a prospect of more money for the project through grant opportunities.
Mr. Allen moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Mr. Allen moved to amend to include the condition
that the proposed living landscape area to be maintained by the City be reduced by 75%, seconded by
Mr. Shanor.  It was noted funding for the plan is related to another agenda item (re: Agreement with
WYDOT for West Lincolnway Project) that will be discussed at the next Finance Committee meeting.
Rick Parish, Parks and Recreation Director, spoke in support of community beautification projects but
advised the department needs funding dollars in order to maintain the project, citing other projects
involving right-of-way beautification that have resulted in personnel and maintenance costs and involve
such areas as fencing, trash pickup, traffic poles, signs, and curbs and gutters.  Upon inquiry pertaining
to estimated annual City maintenance costs, contained in a February 15, 2013 letter from Tom Mason,
Director, Metrop olitan Transportation Org anization, to C ouncil members, Mr. Par ish advised the
department’s budget has remained stagnant for many years and provided information on the different
areas and volume of acres the department maintains that involve personnel and equipment.  He noted
the department has adapted and adjusted procedures in many ways to meet needs and that several
maintenance areas are the result of other department projects involving beautification of rights-of-way.
Mr. Burchett submitted letters [on file with City Clerk’s office] from the Boy Scout Council and Boys
and Girls Club offering their assistance to the City with picking up trash and weeding within the project
area.  Mr. Steenber gen re iterated his support on maintaining the initial level of la ndscaping and
encouraged maintenance funding be committed for the g ood of the c ommunity.  Upon in quiry, Mr.
Parish provided information on Parks and Recreation department staffing levels.  Discussion included
support for the project overall and beautification of the City’s entryways; appreciation for community
involvement; prioritization of maintenance funding, and concern with on-going funding.  Motion to
amend failed.  Voting “no” -- all members of th e governing body.  Discussion on the main motion
followed a nd, upon inquiry, included informa tion by  May or Ka ysen that the a rea proposed f or
beautification is part of WYDOT’s right-of-way, who has jurisdictional authority, and that WYDOT,
the City and many community members have taken the initiative to work on planning the beautification
efforts for many years.  Motion to approve carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.
(#5480)
Use Agreement to permit Level 3 Communications, LLC, limited use of the public right-of-way
for the installation of fiber optic communications systems and setting a fee.  M r. Brown moved to
approve, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Mr. Brown moved to amend by substitute, seconded by Mr. Valdez.
Upon inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney, advised that due to an oversight the original agreement did
not include insurance provisions and that the substitute contains that language which will protect the
City.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.  Main motion as
amended carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#5963)

Associates A rchitects for the provision of sc hematic de sign, de sign de velopment, cons truction
documents, bidding documents, bidding assistance and construction management for the Municipal
Pool Renovation and Expansion Project (1% Specific Purpose Sales & Use Tax).  Motion (per Consent
Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $639,550.00). (#5964)
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and DHM Design Corporation
for the provision of schematic design, design development, construction documents, bidding documents
and bidding assistance for the Converse Softball Complex Renovation project (Menard property sale
proceeds).  F rank Cole c ommented on the nee d for sidew alks, curbs a nd gutters along portions of
Converse a nd Airport Parkw ay to be included in the plan a nd that fenc e adjustments and a rea
landscaping a lso be incorpor ated a s oth er private de velopments requir e.  Rick Parish, Parks a nd
Recreation Director, advised the City  currently does not have  a plan f or the e ntire area, noting the
agreement is for design for the complex, and that a past master plan that was prepared is the property
of the Cheyenne Extreme Softball group.  Mr. Brown raised a point of order questioning whether a
motion was on the floor with May or Kaysen stating no motion had y et been made and asking for a
motion before disc ussion continued .  Mr. B rown moved to a pprove in a n amount not to ex ceed
$119,843.00, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Comments followed in support of the project.  Motion carried.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#5965)
[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Cheyenne Umpires Association to provide umpire
services for the 2013, 2014 and 2015 Softball Seasons (softball registration revenue) -- referred to
Finance Committee. 
[CA] Agreement for Juvenile Compliance and Diversion Officer Services between Laramie County,
the City of Cheyenne, and the Laramie County Community Juvenile Services Joint Powers Board for
reimbursement of dual-commissioned Juvenile Diversion Officer’s salary (Federal Office of Juvenile

416 West 17  Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming (1% Specific Purpose Sales & U se Tax) -- referred toth
Finance Committee.
[CA] Amendment No. 1 to Contract No. 5956, Contingency and Development Agreement,  between
the City of Cheyenne and parties involved in the Microsoft Dataplant Demonstration Project to effect
a date change -- referred to Finance Committee. 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #R-17-13 for furnishing two new medium/heavy duty transit buses with
wheelchair lifts for the City of Cheyenne, Public Works Department, Transit Division (partial State
Loan and Investment Board and Wyoming Department of Transportation funds).  Motion (per Consent
Agenda) carried (accept bid submitted by Creative Bus Sales, Phoenix, AZ in an amount not to exceed
$193,786.00).
[CA] Consideration of Bid #R-19-13 for f urnishing ballistic vests for the  Police Department from
Skaggs Uniforms, Salt Lake City, Utah (Miscellaneous Federal Grants).  Motion (per Consent Agenda)
carried (amount not to exceed $35,596.50).
Appointments of Earl B. Kincheloe to the Cheyenne Housing Authority Board; Beth Pratt to the
Mayor’s Council for People with Disabilities, and Nicholas Pickering to the Building Code Board of
Appeals.  Mr. Brown moved to approve, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the g overning b ody with the exception of May or Kaysen who did not vote (Ma yoral
appointments).
[CA] Request to approve , pursuan t to Wyo. Stat. §12-5-402 (b), a  sponsor rela tionship between
Cheyenne Frontier Days, Inc. and Miller/Coors, a malt beverage industry representative, in conjunction
with the 2013, 2014 and 2015 annual Cheyenne Frontier Days community event.  Motion (per Consent
Agenda) carried.
Under other business:
Pete Laybourn expressed his concerns regarding a neighborhood issue along Pershing Blvd. from
Converse to Seymour, particularly the intersection area of Duff and Pershing.  He briefly reviewed past
history of the intersection relative to a traffic light; commented on increased vehicular and pedestrian
traffic and safety issues, and noted private business investments and improvements and economic
vitality within the area.  He requested the governing body consider a professional evaluation involving
traffic volumes, pedestrian access and traffic practices relative to whether traffic lights and pedestrian
crosswalks would be appropriate.  Mr. Laybourn stated with the pending reconstruction project work
planned for Pershing Blvd. that this would be a good opportunity to also address this topic.  Michelle
McMahon, President/CEO, Frontier Access & Mobility, also discussed vehicle and pedestrian safety
concerns involving the Duff and Pershing intersection, and spoke in support of implementation of a
traffic light a nd crosswalks.  Connie Cook, busi ness employee in the ne ar vicinity, also conve yed
pedestrian safety concerns, relating a recent accident that had occurred at the intersection, and stated that
people can not safely cross the street.  Support for a traffic study for the intersection area was voiced by
Mr. Allen and Ms. Williams.
Brad Harrington commented on a recent public hearing before the Planning Commission with
regard to input on proposed changes to the Unified Development Code, and expressed concerns on the
hearing format not being conducive to receiving comments from those who w ere in attendance.  He
stated his opposition to the UDC from a personal property rights standpoint and encouraged Council
members to repeal it.  M. Lee Hasenauer, Laramie County Commissioner, inquired about pending dates
relative to future meetings concerning proposed UDC changes with Mayor Kaysen advising that meeting
dates have not yet been set as input that has been received is being reviewed.  He added that there will
be a City Council work session scheduled prior to changes coming before the City Council and that
public notices of the meetings will be provided.  B arbie Harrington expressed her vie wpoint of the
public hearing held on the UDC, conveying her belief that not all of the public’s input (i.e.: wr itten
materials) was shared with Commission members.  Comment was made  that meetings involving the
UDC changes should be better publicized.
Buck Holmes, L aramie County  Commi ssioner, advised he  is the Commi ssion’s deleg ated
representative to attend City Council meetings and he was available for any communication purposes.
He stated Commissioners Amber Ash a nd M. L ee Hasenauer were also in the audienc e to furthe r
communication between the commissioners and council members.
Congratulation was extended to the Tyrrell-Doyle Chevrolet family for celebrating their 75th
anniversary of doing business in Cheyenne.
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was officially
adjourned at 9:22 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 March 8, 2013

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2013-03-11 | CLIP_ID: 363 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 363

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 March 11, 2013
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS -
Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and Ms.
Williams.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City
Attorney.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the desig nation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other
members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal
sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Allen, and pursuant to support received by Ms. Broyles
and Mr. Shanor, agenda item #13(d) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Allen moved to
approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on February 25, 2013.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Ameri-Tech
HVAC Services) and vacated Council Chambers.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve payment of vouchers
as presented, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Upon inquiry regarding a voucher payable to Appraisals, Inc.
($975.00), Lois Huff, City Treasurer, and Lt. Mark Munari, Police Department, provided information
regarding appraisal of two properties located at 400-408 W. 17  Street, which are adjacent to the siteth
proposed for the new public safety building (Dinneen property), noting the property owner shared the
cost of the appraisal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present in
Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown returned to Council Chambers.
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning  Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, Zoning
Districts established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified Development
Code, changing the zoning classification from A-1 Agricultural and Rural Residential District to MR-2
Medium Density Residential – Developing District for a tract of land situated in a portion the East Half
of the East Half (E½E½) of Section 20 and a portion of the Southwest Quarter of the Southwest Quarter
of the Northwest Quarter (SW¼ SW¼ NW¼) of Section 21,  T.14N., R.66W., 6th P.M., Cheyenne,
Laramie County, Wyoming, to be know n as the B luffs, 9  Filing (located approximately 1,500 feetth
north of the intersection of Converse Avenue and Ogden Road).  Mayor Kaysen advised a letter (on file
with City Clerk’s office) had been received from the applicant requesting the item, as well as an
affiliated a genda ite m pe rtaining to a nnexation, b e withdrawn.  U pon in quiry, M r. Al len, Pub lic
Services Committee Chair, confirmed that Committee members ha d no objection to the item being
withdrawn.  Upon query and with no objection being raised from members of the governing body,
Mayor Kaysen stated the item was being withdrawn from the agenda.
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zon ing Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, Z oning
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified Development
Code, changing the zoning classification from P Public to NB Neighborhood Business for Lot 1, Block
4, Storey Boulevard Extension, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located east of and adjacent to Converse Avenue,
approximately one-quarter mile south of Storey Boulevard).  Reporting for Public Services Committee,
Mr. Allen moved to appro ve on third and final rea ding, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Motion carr ied.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#3970)
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Z oning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, Z oning

Code, changing the zoning classification from A-2, County Agriculture, to AG, Agriculture, for Lot 1,
Block 1; L ot 1, Block 2; a nd L ot 2, Block 2, Plains I ndustrial Park, and c hanging the zoning
classification from A-2, County  Ag riculture, to HI , Hea vy I ndustrial, for L ot 3, Block 2, Plains
Industrial Park, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located south of I-80, west of Campstool Road) -- referred to

[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Cheyenne Umpires Association to provide umpire

[CA] Agreement for Juvenile Compliance and Diversion Officer Services between Laramie County,
