reimbursement of dual-commissioned Juvenile Diversion Officer’s salary (Federal Office of Juvenile
Justice and Delinquency Prevention Grant).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) approved (revenue to City
$37,578.00). (#5969) 
[CA] Agreement between the Wyoming Department of Transportation and the City of Cheyenne for
the West Lincolnway Project (Wyoming Department of Transportation funding).  Motion (per Consent
Agenda) approved. (#5970)
Real Estate Purchase Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and W. E. Dinneen, Inc., for the
purchase of real estate  located at 405 West 18  Street, 1704 Pioneer Avenue, 410 West 17  Street, andth th
416 West 17  Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming (1% Specific Purpose Sales & Use Tax).  William Overlingth
posed numerous questions involving the proposed acquisition of the property as the site of a new public
safety building.  His queries included the property appraisal amount and what the City is proposing to
pay; renovation costs, and thoroughness of researching other potential sites.  I n response, Lt. Mark
Munari, Cheyenne Police Department, provided information on the property involving existing building
and lot infrastructure and renovation benefits; renovation costs; property appraisal ($1.655 million) vs.
owner’s requested purchase price ($1.986 million); existing underground as well as upper level parking
availability; project funding in the amount of $25 million approved by voters for use of 1% Specific
Sales and Use  Tax, and other sites that had be en analyzed.  Bo b Morris, Downtown De velopment
Authority Board member, spoke in s upport of the City acquiring the property for the public safety
building, noting the importance of maintaining a downtown police presence which a lso p romotes
utilization of a vacant building site.  Joh n Dinneen, representative of W.E. Dinneen, Inc., property
owner, provided a  brief history of the prope rty’s use by  the owner , noting the f ormer Dinneen car
dealership business that had occupied a majority of the site has been closed for six years.  He reviewed
the owner’s efforts in putting together a team of professionals to ascertain the property’s attributes and
marketing options to utilize the site to its highest and best use for economic value to the community.
He discussed the property appraisal amount vs. the requested purchase price, stating an appraisal is an
estimated value only and that commercial property appraisals are more difficult to do as it is hard to find
comparable prope rties, and pr ovided re asons why  the owner  believes some c omponents of the
building’s features should have been assigned more value in the appraisal report.  During his comments,
Mr. Dinneen referred to visual slides that were displayed and submitted a handout (on file with City
Clerk’s office) from the appraisal report relative to the square foot market valuation.  Upon inquiry, Mr.
Dinneen advised as part of the City’s acquisition process an environmental assessment of the property
will be conducted.  Mr. Overling commented in opposition to purchase of the property at this time and
suggested other sites be checked into relative to comparison of costs of rehabilitation vs. new building
construction.  James Perrott Macneil stated his belief that there are not many large properties available
and spoke in support of the site as the location for the public safety building.  Lt. Munari advised that
pursuant to research in preparing information for the tax ballot, the Dinneen property provided the best
option for use of ta xpayers dol lars and that the estimated construction cost includes r enovation.
Reporting for F inance Commi ttee, Mr. B rown moved to a pprove in a n amount not to ex ceed
$1,986,000.00, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Discussion topics included: Wording of ballot proposition
approved by voters in the funding amount of $25 million dollars for the project; concern with possible
additional c osts; f uture p otential t o a cquire a dditional p roperties w ithin th e b lock f or e xpansion;
property appraisal valuation vs. owner’s requested purchase price; support for the public safety building
to be located within the downtown area; suggestion that further research be done on optional sites;
estimated fair market value; City not legally restricted to purchasing property at more than the appraised
amount; long-range plans for other service areas to be included at the building site; review of other sites
that ha d b een a nalyzed; c omposition of  the  sit e re view c ommittee, w hich in cluded City -County
Emergency Manag ement Ag ency and Combined Communications  dispatch re presentatives; due
diligence efforts, and conc erns with re novation and maintena nce issues a s well as future  6  pennyth
project funding.  Comments in support as well as non-support of purchasing the property were made.
During discussion and upon inquiry, Lt. Munari and Chief Brian Kozak, Police Department, provided
information in response to questions, including proposed project costs; criteria used by the committee
in reviewing sites, which included a preferred downtown location to be in close vicinity to municipal
court and jail facilities for reduced costs and efficiencies, and positive elements of the Dinneen property.
Upon inquiry, Mayor Kaysen provided information on future expansion (long-range plan) at the site that
might include re-location of Municipal Court offices.  Upon inquiry to Council members whether there
would be any objection to him asking questions for clarification relating to topics that were being raised
during discussion, and no objection being  voiced, Mayor Kaysen requested information from Chief
Kozak and L t. Munari re garding costs for a downtown location vs. outside of the dow ntown area
associated with such areas as fiber optics; proposed use of the building’s underground parking area;
environmental assessment information (to be received prior to closing date if purchased); information
provided to the public on the proposed building site and structure renovation plans prior to the ballot
proposition being vote d upon; funding  amount ($25 m illion) voters approve d, and cha llenges
discovered with other potential locations reviewed by the site committee.  Motion to approve failed (due
to a tie vote).  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Valdez, Ms. Williams and Mayor Kaysen.
Voting “no” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Shanor.
Following a 10 minute recess, Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at 8:08 p.m.

microbrewery, 1509 Pioneer Ave.; OG of Cheyenne, Inc. d/b/a The Olive Garden Italian Rest., bar &

E. Lincolnway; O’Jackson’s, Inc. d/b/a  Jackson’s All American Sports Bar & Grill, bar & grill, 1121
Old Town Lane; Blazin Wings, Inc. d/b/a Buffalo Wild Wings Grill & Bar, bar & grill, 1740 Prairie
Ave.; The Suite, LLC d/b/a The Suite, bar &  grill, 1901 Central Ave.  Car ol Intlekofer, City Clerk,
offered a suggestion that in lieu of rea ding the list of renewal applicants for the 2013-2014 term, as
shown on the meeting agenda, the listing itself be recorded in its entirety in the official minutes of the
meeting together with any motions and votes.  She noted, pursuant to state statute req uirements,
publication of notice of renewal applications and posting of notices on respective premises have been
achieved, and the  holding of  a public hea ring before the g overning body has bee n conducted.  She
further stated that the City Attorney has advised that this statement on the record provides sufficient
documentation to identify those individual renewal applications that may be acted upon at the meeting.
Upon query by Mayor Kaysen to members of the governing body, there was no objection voiced to
recognize the renewal applicants as listed on the agenda in lieu of the Clerk reading the individual
applicants, and Mayor Kaysen announced the r eading of re newal applicants’ infor mation would be
waived.  Mr. Brown moved to approve the renewal applications “en masse”, seconded by Mr. Valdez.
Mr. Brown moved to divide the question to allow for a separate vote on Stillwater, Inc. d/b/a Mingles,
seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.  Mr.
Brown moved to approve the liquor license application for Stillwater, Inc. d/b/a Mingles subject to the
terms and conditions of the Resolution and Settlement Agreement approved by the governing body on
Feb. 25, 2013, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Upon inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney, advised wording of
the motion preserves the City’s rights under the Resolution and Settlement Agreement and makes clear
that renewal of the liquor license for Mingles is subject to the terms and conditions of the Resolution
and Settlement Agreement.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.  Upon
inquiry regarding whether a representative for Olive G arden was pre sent at the mee ting to provide
information concerning failure of a police compliance check during 2012, it was noted that no one was
present on behalf of the establishment.  Police Chief Brian Kozak provided information available on the
topic, stating the individual involved in the failed compliance check was no longer employed by the
licensee.  Motion to approve all remaining renewal applicants “en masse” carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body.
[CA] Authorization to purchase self-contained breathing apparatus equipment from Norco, Inc., single
source vendor, throug h the Fir efighter Ac countability prog ram (F ederal Emerg ency Manag ement
Agency (F EMA) Ac countability Gra nt and 2011 - 2014 1% Sale s Tax ) -- referred to Finance
Committee. 
Upon inquiry, Mayor Kaysen provided a brief update on negotiation discussion status involving
the City-owned portion of property commonly referred to as the “downtown hole”, advising that interest
by adjacent property owners is still ongoing.
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was officially
adjourned at 8:18 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 March 21, 2013

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY

The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen;  COUNCIL MEMBERS
- Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and Ms. Williams.
Absent: Ms. Broyles.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer, and Dan
White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Mayor Kaysen noted a clerical correction on the numbering of items contained on the first page
of the meeting agenda.  Consent Ag enda. (All agenda items listed with the desig nation of [CA] are
considered to be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will
be no separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in
its normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Brown, and pursuant to support received by
Mr. Allen and Dr. Rinne, agenda item #8 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Allen moved
to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” --
all members of the governing body present.

HVAC Services) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Valdez moved to approve payment of vouchers
as presented, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing
body present in Council Chambers.  F ollowing announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown returned to
Council Chambers.

Use Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Parks and Recreation Department, and Cheyenne
Warriors Professional Indoor Football Team, LLC, for the purpose of holding arena football games and

Department, and Chey enne Junior L eague Baseball for the provision of c oncession services at the
Cheyenne Junior League Baseball Complex Concession and Restroom Facility -- referred to Finance
Committee.
[CA] Use Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Warren Federal Credit Union to p ermit

[CA] Contract between the City of Cheyenne, Youth Alternatives, and Laramie County Community
Partnership, Inc., for academic and enrichment activities for at-risk school-age youth at Carey Junior

Associates for the addition of strategic grant writing services and associated preliminary engineering
design required by grants to enable securing of additional grant funding for the Cheyenne West Edge
(Lower Capitol Basin) Project (1% Specific Purpose Sales & Use Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda)
carried (amount not to exceed $263,620.00). (#5781)  
Contract Modification No. 4 to Contract No. 5496, between the City of Cheyenne and Ayres
Associates for additional testing services for the East Pershing Boulevard Reconstruction Project (Phase
IV) (2007-2010 1% Sales Tax, Board of Public Utilities, Federal Highway Administration funds).  Mr.
Brown reported there was no recommendation on the item from Finance Committee.  Mr. Brown moved
to approve in an amount not to exceed $19,360.00, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Brown
advised of the results of the votes taken for recommendation at Finance Committee.  Upon inquiry,
Nathan Beauheim, Acting City Engineer, advised the modification specifically relates to the west
corridor portion of the project that Reiman is constructing.  He provided information regarding the need
for the additional testing services for quality control relative to certain omissions discovered in the
initial bid/contract provisions, noting the use  of a sub- consultant should result in a net sa vings of
approximately $6,000.00 to the City.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing
body present. (#5496)
[CA] Contract Modification No. 1 to Contract No. 5922, between the City of Cheyenne, Parks and
Recreation Department, and Simon Contractors, Inc., Cheyenne, Wyoming, to increase the quantity of
structural concrete retaining and wingwalls for the Cheyenne Greenway Walterscheid Underpass Project
(1% Spec ific Purpose S ales and Use  Ta x, Miscellaneous F ederal g rants) - - re ferred to F inance
Committee.
[CA] Consideration of Bid #R-20-13 for furnishing three new pickup trucks for the Public Works
Department, Street & Alley Division (2007 - 2010, 2011- 2014 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent
Agenda) carried (accept bid from Spradley Barr Motors, Inc., Cheyenne, WY, in an amount not to
exceed $104,690.00).
Consideration o f B id # M-9-13 f or a  Co ntract f or th e E ast P ershing B oulevard/19th
Street/Converse Avenue Intersection Improvements Project between the City of Cheyenne, Engineering
Department, and Simon Contractors, C heyenne, Wyoming ( 2007 - 2010 1% Sales Ta x).  Rich
Bolkovatz, Reiman Corp., expressed concerns with awarding the bid to Simon Contractors, commenting
on bid elements involving disclosure of sub-contractors; type of contractor trade license involved and
work that c an be done  under the  license ty pe; landscaping and tree removal as well electrical and
plumbing work involved a nd re quirements of  tho se wh o c an p rovide the  se rvices; b id q uantity
discrepancies, and state pre ference law complianc e.  John Arambul, Sim on Contractors, provided
information on services that will be provided in volving landscaping and tree removal, relative to a
licensed arborist, and bidding of the project based upon wording in the bid specifications, and advised
the company  does have  the nec essary require d licenses.  Mr. B rown reported there  was no
recommendation on the item from F inance Committee.  Mr. Brown moved to acc ept the bid in an
amount not to exceed $4,503,141.55, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Arambul reviewed
work the company had recently provided involving a portion of we st Pershing, which required tree
removal and landscape work, and advised they sub it out if it is considered high risk, otherwise they do
the wo rk themselves.  He stated that he did not feel he will need to hire a subcontractor for the
landscaping and tree removal portion of this project.  Upon inquiry , he reviewed how the company
acquires information from proposed subcontractors or specialized service providers to a ssist with
preparation of e stimated costs to submit  in a bid proposal.  Discussion followed and included the
following topics: Requirements for providing landscaping/tree removal, plumbing and electrical work;
subcontractor requirements and lists and timing of submittal of same; work that can be provided under
a Class A contractor license; type of work that will be done regarding landscaping/tree removal and
electrical and plumbing areas; concern with potential change orders and wording of bid specification
document; responsibility for materials testing; wording of the bid specification issued by the City and
suggestions for changes/clarifications to future bid documents involving work of this type relative to
subcontractor list requirements; proof  of contra ctor qualifications; enforcement of contractor self-
performance ar eas, a nd liquidat ed damages.  During  discussion and upon inquiry , Mr. Ara mbul
provided information in response to questions and Nathan Beauheim, Acting City Engineer, provided
information on contractor materials testing requirements.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members
of the governing body present with the ex ception of Mr. Cook, Dr. Rinne and Ms. Willi ams voting
“no”. (#5975)
[CA] Consideration of Bid #M-11-13 for annual purchase of furnishing aggregate for the Street and
Alley Division (2011 - 2014 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accept bid from
Knife River, Cheyenne, WY in the amount of $4.70/ton).
[CA] Consideration of Bid #M-12-13 for annual purchase of  hot mix, tack oil and cold mix for the
Street and Alley Division (2011 - 2014 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accept
bid from Knife River, Cheyenne, WY in the amount of $51.95/ton-hot mix; $1.15/gal-tack o il;
$77.50/ton-cold mix.).
    
[CA] Consideration of Bid #R-24-13 for furnishing and installing playground systems for JL Ranch
Park fr om Great Western Park a nd Play ground, Wellsvill e, Utah, f or the Parks a nd Recre ation
Department (Community Facility fees) -- referred to Finance Committee.
[CA] Authorization to purchase self-contained breathing apparatus equipment from Norco, Inc., single
source vendor, throug h th e Firefighter Ac countability prog ram (F ederal Emerg ency Manag ement
Agency (FEMA) Accountability Grant and 2011 - 2014 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda)
carried.
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held Monday, April 8, 2013 at 6:00 p.m.
in City Council Chambers, 2 101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for Fiscal Year 2013-2014
Annual Action Plan, City of Cheyenne Community Development Block Grant (CDBG) entitlement.
Under other business:
Jan Nelson Schroll provided handouts (on file with City Clerk’s Office) regarding the City’s
old pump house building located within the west edge storm drainage project.  She expressed her belief
that it is a dangerous building and a potential liability to the City, citing safety concerns due to the
unstabilized condition of the building, broken glass, discarded tires and storage of certain items in an
open environment.  She spoke in favor of stabilizing and renovating the building in the future.
Tom Lindsey commented on lack of development of the “downtown hole” off of Lincolnway
and stated action should be initiated to fill it in to help beautify the area.  He suggested a multi-purpose
building be built on the site.  Mr. L indsey als o expressed his opinion t hat the rounda bout (i.e.
Pershing/19th/Converse project) was not necessary.  Later, under other business, and upon request,
Mayor Kaysen advised a resolution was adopted authorizing the mayor to negotiate with the adjacent
property owner  rela tive t o the City-owned portion of the prope rty commonly  ref erred to as the
“downtown hole” and that negotiations are on-going, and that fencing of the site has been stabilized.
Duane Wall read a  statement of history  he had c ompiled pertaining to damag e he a lleged
occurred to his prope rty in early 2000 due to dra inage issues resulting from a housing subdivision
development.  He stated problems he  has endured due to the dr ainage design and valley pan/gutter
installed adjacent to his property, and noted his attendance at previous City Council meetings to address
the matter.  He reviewed his concerns with a local building contractor and City staff who were involved
in the development project at the time; enforcement of City laws and codes; construction specifications,
subdivision and zoning regulations in place at the time of construction vs. current Unified Development
Code provisions; his prior requests for monetary relief, correction and remedy to the drainage problems
he had raised, and submitted a packet of information (on file with City Clerk’s office) to members of
the governing body.  He requested reimbursement for expenses he has incurred regarding the issues he
presented.
Margaret Kelly raised concerns regarding parking issues in front of homes located along E. 25th
Street between Central and Warren Ave. and asked whether special parking areas for residents only
could be assigned.  She advised there is a 2 hour limit on parking in the area since it is located within
a historical district, but stated this is often abused by employees who work in the vicinity, those
attending s tate l egislative m eetings d uring t he s ession, a nd t ourists/visitors t o t he c ommunity.
Comments followed reg arding communications with City  staff on the  matter a nd Mayor Kaysen
advised he would follow up with staff.
Comment was made regarding concerns on receiving information from City departments.
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was officially
adjourned at 7:25 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 April 4, 2013

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 April 8, 2013
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS -
Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and Ms. Williams.
Absent: Ms. Broyles.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer, and Dan
White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of contained  [CA]  are considered
to be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other
members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal
sequence on the agenda.)  Mr. Brown moved to approve Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Luna.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.  Mayor Kaysen noted
Agenda Item #22(g) is being referred to Committee of the Whole, which will meet to review the item
on April 17 at 6:00 p.m. in City Council Chambers.
[CA] Minutes from Reg ular Meeting of the Gover ning Body on March 25, 2013.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Ameri-Tech
HVAC Services) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Valdez moved to approve payment of vouchers
as presented, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing
body present in Council Chambers.  F ollowing announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown returned to
Council Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive comments and input for the Fiscal Year 2013-
2014 A nnual A ction Pl an, Ci ty o f Ch eyenne Co mmunity D evelopment B lock G rant ( CDBG)
entitlement.  Deanne Widauf, Housing and Community Development Office (H&CD), provided a brief
overview of the history and purpose of the CDBG program, noting the City has received entitlement
grant funds since 1975.  She advised the proposed plan is consistent with the 5 year Comprehensive
Consolidated Plan, and stated the anticipated allocation funding amount is $344,408.00, which is 8%
less than last year, and that an additional $298,086.00 in recaptured and un-appropriated funds will also
be utilized (total amount of $642,494.00).  Ms Widauf explained the grant application process; public
notifications and opportunities for input, and r ole of the A dvisory Council in reviewing  and
recommending projects to be funded.  She stated 10 projects are proposed for funding for public service
projects, general service projects and program administration, and reviewed the projects and funding
amounts as recommended by the Advisory Council.  Upon inquiry, Rick Parish, Director of Parks and
Recreation, provided i nformation on the de partment’s funding request for the Holliday  Park ADA
accessible restroom project (facility located at 19  and Alexander) which was submitted through theth
application process with the assistance of the City’s Grants Manager.  There being no further comments
and input, and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the public hearing.
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Z oning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, Z oning
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified Development
Code, changing the zoning classification from A-2, County Agriculture, to AG, Agriculture, for Lot 1,
Block 1; L ot 1, Block 2; a nd L ot 2, Block 2, Plains Industrial Park, and c hanging the  zoning
classification from A-2, County  Ag riculture, to HI , Hea vy I ndustrial, for L ot 3, Block 2, Plains
Industrial Park, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located south of I-80, west of Campstool Road).  Reporting for
Public Services Committee, Mr. Allen moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Ms.
Williams.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present. (#3971)
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 , Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, Z oning
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified Development
Code, changing the zoning classification from MR-1, Medium Density Residential-Established to NR-3,
Neighborhood Residential-High Density for t he West 44-feet of Lot 4, Block 259, Original City,
Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the southeast corner of 21  Street and Maxwell Avenue) – referred tost
Public Services Committee (sponsor - Mr. Allen).
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Z oning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, Z oning
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified Development
Code, changing the zoning classification from P, Public, to CBD, Central Business District for the south
½ of Lots 1 and 2, and 16-foot vacated alley, Block 229, Original City, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located

ORDINANCE - Vacating portions of Duff Avenue and E. 13th Street adjacent to Blocks 443,
444, 4 53 a nd 4 54, O riginal C ity, Ch eyenne, Wy oming ( located s outh o f E . L incolnway, w est of

RESOLUTION - A uthorizing the May or and the Ci ty Clerk to sign a  Final Plat for Plains
Industrial Park, a r eplat of a portion of HR Ranch Road r ight-of-way within GDC Subdi vision and
portions of Section 6, T.13N., R.65W.; Section 1, T.13N., R.66W.; Section 31, T.14N., R.65W.; and
Section 36, T.14N., R.66W., 6th P.M., Cheyenne, Wyoming (located south of I-80, west of Campstool
Road).  Mr. Allen moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Mr. Allen moved to amend by substitute
to correct a typographical error in the 4th paragraph to change the “dedication of Lot 3, Block 2" to
“dedication of Lot 1, Block 2”, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body present.  Main motion as amended by substitute carried.  Voting “yes”
-- all members of the governing body present. (#5483)
RESOLUTION - A uthorizing an independent professional tra ffic safety planning study be
conducted for the inter section of Per shing Boulevard and Duf f Avenue in the City of Cheyenne,
Wyoming.  Tom Mason, Director, Metropolitan Planning Organization (MPO), recommended the study
be facilitated through the MPO, expl aining that assig nment to the MPO would provide for a  more
detailed a nd ind epth stu dy inv olving oth er n eighborhood a nd c ommunity ele ments, a nd pu blic
involvement.  He pr ovided information on the MPO’s fede ral funding cycle and the MPO Poli cy
Committee’s inv olvement i n a pproving pr ojects; e xpressed h is beli ef that ti ming of  the  stu dy is
important relative to current area construction that could affect accuracy of results, and recommended
developing the scope  of work to be included in the study  be done in the summer  with hiring of  a
consultant to conduct the study later to coincide with the MPO budget cycle, contingent upon the Policy
Committee’s approval, and when c onstruction has bee n completed in the a rea.  Upon inq uiry, Mr.
Mason provided information on a grant the MPO received last summer to develop a complete streets
policy, which could be included in PlanCheyenne, and noted the policy would address transportation
routes for cars, pedestrians and bicyclists.  Anne  Picot, member, Mayor’s Council for People with
Disabilities, commented on pedestrian safety concerns at the Pershing/Duff intersection and expressed
concerns with postponing the study, stating timely resolution to the issue should be achieved.  Lee Filer,
state representative, spoke in favor of addressing the matter immediately due to safety concerns.  Gregg
Crisp, chairman, Mayor’s Council for People with Disabilities, advised the Council does not support
the resolution in its current state but would support a study by MPO with a complete street policy that
would encompass traffic, pedestrians, and businesses located near the intersection.  Debbie Massengill,
employee of Hoy’s Drug, also cited pedestrian safety concerns she has observed at the interse ction
crossing. Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Brown stated there was no recommendation on the
item from the Committee.  Mr. Allen moved to adopt, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Discussion included the
following topics: Support for the MPO to conduct the study , which will provide for  a more
encompassing study of areas of concern, after construction is completed i n the area; addressing the
matter properly and efficiently through MPO assistance, and the need to acquire accurate data.  Motion
to adopt failed.  Voting “no” -- all members of the governing body present.

Announcement was made of the a ppointment of Robert G. Ge ringer as City Engineer, to be
confirmed April 22, 2013.
Announcement was made of the appointment of Michael J. Sorenson to the Laramie County
Community Juvenile Services Joint Powers Board, to be confirmed April 22, 2013.
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held April 22, 2013 at 6:00 p.m. in City
Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, to determine whether compliance of
annexation c onditions r equired b y Wyo. Sta t. § 15-1-402 e xist f or a  pa rcel la nd be ing sit uated in
portions of Sections 14, 15, 22, 23, 27, Township 13 North, Range 67 West of the 6th p.m., Laramie
County, Wyoming, to be known as the Swan Ranch II Annexation (located northwest of the intersection
of Clear Creek Parkway and Tundra Drive).
Under other business:
Pete Laybourn thanked Council members for supporting the traffic study to be conducted at the
intersection of Pershing Blvd. and Duff Avenue.  He expressed concerns with the “failure” of concrete
at the intersection as well as other locations within the City, and requested Council members be diligent
in having good quality concrete for street, curb and gutter projects.
Lee Filer, state legislative representative, expressed concerns on the following issues: Repair
costs of a sewer l ine i ncurred b y a c onstituent a nd C ity vs. h omeowner’s a rea o f r esponsibility;
sanitation fee  assessme nts involving 40 tena nts in the Majestic apa rtment building; manda tory
residential recycling bin fee; deterioration of curbs, gutters and sidewalks in certain neighborhoods,
specifically citing an area near Walterscheid and Fox Farm; small business owner concerns relating to
communication o f i nformation b y Bu ilding, P lanning and Fi re De partments i nvolving z oning,
development, licensing and inspections, and need for flashing lights and crosswalks, in addition to
current signage, near certain elementary schools for student crossing safety.  During queries raised by
Representative Filer throughout his statements, Mayor Kaysen offered to meet with the constituent and
City staff relative to the sewer line topic; and Jim Elias, Public Works Director, and Nathan Beauheim,
Acting City Engineer, provided information available involving the topics of sanitation and recyling
fees, and schedule of certain street repairs/renovation projects.  Com ments made by some Council
members regarded a suggested area that should be revie wed for street repairs or reconstruction and
flashing lights/crosswalk designations at certain schools; residential responsibility involving sewer lines,
and level of state funding assistance to municipalities.
Upon inquiry regarding street sweeping operations sometimes occurring the same day as trash
receptacles are placed in the street for sanitation pick-up, Mr. Elias advised at times scheduling is
difficult to re-arrange for coordination of spring clean-up and that routes and times change, but they do
try to stagger the schedule to avoid overlapping of operations.
There being no other business raised by the public and City Council members, Dan White, City
Attorney, requested the governing body meet in executive session, pursuant to provisions of Wyo. Stat.
16-4-404 (a) (iii) and (ix), to receive legal advice on two different matters -- potential settlement of a
claim asserted against the City which, in the absence of a settlement, could result in litigation against
the City, and poten tial need for the City  to initi ate litigation processes in another matter  involving
applicable provisions of state law.  D r. Rinne moved to mee t in executive session, seconded by Mr.
Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.  Mayor Kaysen
announced members would meet in executive session in Council Chambers, not open to the public;
requested the City Attorney, City Clerk, and others as requested by the City Attorney, be present at the
executive session, and stated there would be no action taken by the governing body as a result of the
executive session, and that the regular City Council meeting would officially conclude at the close of
the executive session.  (City Clerk Note: Following a brief recess, executive session convened at 7:28
p.m.)
The meeting was officially adjourned at 8:11 p.m. following conclusion of the executive session.
 Submitted by,
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 April 18, 2013

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2013-04-22 | CLIP_ID: 370 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 370

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 April 22, 2013
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS -
Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and Ms.
Williams.   Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City
Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of contained  [CA]  are considered
to be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other
members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal
sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Dr. Rinne, and pursuant to support received by Ms. Broyles
and Mr. Brown, agenda item #22(a) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  M r. Allen moved to
approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr.  Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” --
all members of the governing body.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on April 8, 2013.  Motion (per Consent
Agenda) carried.
Mr. Valdez moved to approve pa yment of vouche rs as pre sented, seconded by Mr. Allen.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive comments and input to determine whether
compliance of annexation conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exist for a parcel of land being
situated in  portions of Sections 14, 15, 22, 23, 27, Township 13 North, Range 67 West of the 6th p.m.,
Laramie County, Wyoming, to be known as the Swan Ranch II Annexation (located northwest of the
intersection o f Cl ear Cr eek Pa rkway a nd T undra D rive).  Jim  F lesher, Pl anning Se rvices D ept.,
reviewed requirements and compliance of conditions contained in Wy o. Stat. §15-1-402, including
specific information indicating compliance with Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402(a)(i) through (vi).  Upon inquiry,
Mr. Flesher verified the area involved as depicted on the map he displayed during his comments and
advised a zone chang e for either c ommercial or industrial use will be forthcoming.  He state d the
annexation resolution and subsequent ordinance will run concurrently with the site plan, and advised
additional future annexation of property in the area is planned.  There being no further comments or
input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing.

Real Estate Purchase Agreement between W. E. Dinneen, Inc., and the City of Cheyenne, for
the City’s purchase of various parcels of real property generally located within Block 353, Original City,
between P ioneer a nd T homes Av enues an d b etween 1 7  an d 1 8  S treets, Cheyenne, W yoming,th th
commonly known as  405 West 18  Street, 1704 Pioneer Avenue, 410 West 17  Street, and 416 Westth th
17  Street, which are owned by W.E. Dinneen, Inc. (1% Specific Purpose Sales & Use Tax).  Chiefth
Brian Kozak, Cheyenne Police Department, provided a slide presentation entitled “Cheyenne Public
Safety Center Land Acquisition Report”, and discussed the background and general overview of the new
public safety building project which is initially planned to house the Police Department, Cheyenne Fire
and Rescue  Depa rtment Administration, L aramie County  Combined Comm unications Center,
Emergency Operations Center, and Cheyenne Victim Services.  He  advised the infor mation he was
presenting was basically the same as what was provided at the recent Committee of the Whole meeting.
Chief Kozak’s presentation topics included the following: Space needs assessment conducted in 2009;
professional consultants utilized to conduct various analysis involving sites,  engineering, concrete and
structural reviews and studies, energy efficiency, architectural concepts  and construction cost modeling;
background of property sites that were researched and factors evaluated in reviewing potential sites;
operational and security benefits of the proposed Dinneen property; conceptual building renovation and
floor p lan a nd s ite f ootprint; estimated p roject c osts; f uture b uilding s ite e xpansion o pportunity;
elements of the property appraisal and negotiated purchase price of $1.886 million dollars; maintaining
a downtown public safety presence; 6  penny sales tax approved by voters to fund the project and publicth
education efforts, including project information posted on the D epartment’s website, and numerous
planning and evaluation aspects involving the project.  During Chief Kozak’s comments, additional
information on other sites that were reviewed was provided by Lt. Mark Munari, Police Department.
Chief Kozak noted the following consultants in attendance at the meeting:  Ted Halsey, DLR Group;
Bryan Hemeyer, FCI Constructors; Stephan Pappas, Pappas and Pappas Architects; Ralph Rempel,
Martin/Martin Engineering; Bob Clary, structural engineer; and Clay Muirhead, Terracon Consultants.
The consultants provided information on the proposed site and existing building on the site, and other
sites reviewed, and responded to questions posed by the governing body.  Also offering comments in
support of acquisition of the Dinneen property were: Melissa Leger, Downtown Development Authority
(DDA) board member, noting the Dinneen site was presented to the voters as the proposed location;
John Sundahl, President, DDA, c ommenting in fa vor of the building  project being located in the
downtown a rea a nd a dding su pport to  do wntown r evitalization, a nd Mr . Pa ppas, sp eaking a s a
voter/taxpayer, who expressed his belief that acquiring the property would be a good fudiciary decision
and that appraisals are only an indication of what other properties are selling for at the time.  As a
follow-up to a slide shown by Chief Kozak during his presentation, Rick Fortney, Laramie County
Director of I nformation Technology, dis tributed copies of a n updated diag ram of c omputer inter-
connectivity between City and County offices, and discussed f iber optic c able connections and the
“ring” of infrastructure between the governmental agencies in the downtown area.  Subsequent to a
point of order raised by Mr. Allen that a speaker state their name for the record and with Mayor Kaysen
concurring, Glen Crumpton, Combined Communications Center, identified himself and advised he had
also been involved with the project since the beginning stages.  During comments, questions were posed
by members of  the g overning body to consultants and spea kers regarding review of other  potential
downtown building sites; elements considered during site evaluations; Phase I environmental study, and
groundwater concerns in the downtown area.  M r. Muirhead provided information on components
involved in a Phase I environmental study and what elements may be recognizable as a “risk” that could
involve a decision to conduct a Phase II study.  Upon inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney, verified that
the purchase agreement contemplates the City conducting and reviewing results of the Phase I study
prior to closing on the purchase of the property, and, based upon the results, the City could decide to
not move forward with the purchase or to proceed with a Phase II study.  In response to comments
involving the environmental study, Chief Kozak noted that one of the benefits of the site is that there
will be no need to disturb the ground.  Reporting for Committee of the Whole, Dr. Rinne stated there
was no recommendation from the Committee.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Allen.
Discussion included the following topics: Comments in support as well as non-support of purchasing
the prope rty; information contained in the L and Ac quisition Report; benef its o f a downtown
enforcement presence; due diligence on evaluation of potential building sites; property appraisal and
additional information being received (since the last (failed) action on proposed property acquisition
was taken by the governing body at its March 11, 2013 meeting) resulting in a negotiated purchase price
offered by the property owner; pros and cons of renovation of the existing building vs. constructing a
new building; site amenities, including covered and secured parking; concern with negotiating future
property acquisitions involving property appraisal vs. purchasing amount and maintaining taxpayers’
credibility; new construction offering an opportunity for a “state of the art” building and efficiencies,
and downtown a rea involving the Capit ol Basin and West Edge  drainage projects and c onceptual
revitalization.  Motion to approve carried.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Dr.
Rinne, Mr.  Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  V oting “no” -- Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Mr. Shanor and Ms.
Williams. (#5989)
Following a brief recess, Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at 8:14 p.m.

Consideration of Bid #M-10-13 for a contract for the Snyder Avenue Reconstruction project
between the City of Cheyenne, Engineering Department, and Simon Contractors, Cheyenne, Wyoming
(2003-2006, 2007-2010, 2011- 2014 1% Sales Tax).  Sam Berta, Engineering Dept., advised Simon
Contractors submitted the lowest bid which includes Alternate A.  Mr. Brown moved to accept the bid
in an amount not to exceed  $5,669,859.20, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Comments followed regarding the
governing body being informed of any long term impact of construction projects to businesses or
homeowners in the are a.  Upon inquiry , Mr. B erta provided infor mation pertaining to an on- street
parking issue involving a business that was impacted due to a prior W. Pershing/Snyder project, and
clarified that no private parking area was affected.  Nathan Beauheim, Acting City Engineer, advised
a convenience store business located at the corner of Randall and Snyder had implemented an off-street
parking area on the west side of the building, noting this is in violation of city ordinance, and pursuant
to the rec onstruction project the parking  area will be re moved.  He advised the situation has be en
discussed with the owner.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Berta explained that Alternate A involves using a square
box instead of a round outfall pipe, which will provide savings in the amount of $200,000, and reviewed
the physical location of the project area.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing
body. (#5994)
Appointment of Robert G. Geringer as City Engineer.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve, seconded
by Mr. Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception
of Mayor Kaysen who did not vote (Mayoral appointment).
Appointment of Michael J. Sorenson to the Laramie County Community Juvenile Services Joint
Powers Board.  Mr. Brown moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” --
all members of the governing body  with the exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not vote (Mayoral
appointment).
 
[CA] Designation of Official Voting Delegate and Alternate Delegate for the City of Cheyenne at the
2013 Wyoming Association of Municipalities Annual Convention -- referred to Finance Committee.
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was officially
adjourned at 8:20 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 May 3, 2013

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2013-05-28 | CLIP_ID: 375 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 375

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 May 28, 2013
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen (arriving at 6:01 p.m.), Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Mr.
Shanor, Mr. Valdez  and Ms. Williams.   Absent: Dr. Rinne.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City
Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney. 
Expression of Appreciation to Mayor’s Youth Council and State of the Youth report.  Jan
Spires, Mayor’s Youth Council Coordinator, welcomed the Mayor’s Youth Council members and
their families and introduced Kadi Davis, Youth Council Chair.  Ms. Davis provided a State of the
Youth report, which focused on the topic of teamwork, and described various activities the group
participated in during the past year. She noted one issue they concentrated on, which was to help
raise funds to bring Miss Cheyenne Blokker of the Netherlands to visit Cheyenne.  Mayor Kaysen
and Ms. Spires  commended members of the 2012-2013 Youth Council for their accomplishments
and presented them with a certificate and gift of appreciation.  Th ose recognized were Juliette
Givhan - Vice-Chair, Tyler Bedwell, Savannah Brown, Jon Chenchar, Alex Cole, Ian Dome, Alex
Farkas, John Matsen, Rachel Mosele y and Jacob Peterson.  Ms. Da vis was also presented with a
plaque and gift of appreciation recognizing her service during the past school year as chair of the
Youth Council.  May or Ka ysen fur ther e xpressed appre ciation to members f or their time,
commitment and dedication, and thanked the members’ families for their support.
Consent Ag enda. ( All ag enda items listed with the desig nation of conta ined  [CA]  are
considered to be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will
be no separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and
support by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be
considered in its normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Cook, and pursuant to
support received by Mr. Allen and Ms. Broyles, agenda item #24(h) was removed from the Consent
Agenda. Upon request by Mr. Brown, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Allen and Ms.
Broyles, agenda item #25(a) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Allen  moved to approve
remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body present. 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on May 13, 2013.  Motion (per Consent
Agenda) carried.
Mr. Valdez moved to approve pa yment of vouche rs as pre sented, seconded by Mr. Allen.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
ORDINANCE - Approving and establishing revised water and sewer rates, tap and system
development fees, administrative fees, service charges and penalties and septic and commercial waste
dump rates, as recommended by the Board of Public Utilities.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr.
Brown moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Upon inquiry pertaining to a
letter received by governing body members from Edward Strader, questioning the assessment of water
rates f or mul ti-family un its vs . s ingle family residences, Ti m Wils on, D irector, Board of  Pub lic
Utilities (BOPU), explained the water rate structure.  He advised the current structure was established
and adopted by the g overning body in 2004, noting it was de veloped through use of a n outside
consulting firm in conjunction with the BOPU Master Plan, and provides for tiered water rates.  He
explained the different categories of water users and how each rate is designed to meet costs associated
with providing se rvices to the differ ent types of c ustomers and not on the number of  people that
occupy a building, lot size or number of families in a building.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, and Ms. Williams
voting “no.”
 
ORDINANCE - A mending the City  of Chey enne’s Sanitation Fee  Schedule.  Mr. B rown
reported there was no recommendation on the item from Finance Committee.  Mr. Brown moved to
approve on second reading, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Comments followed regarding expenses the
fees help cover for numerous City services and operations; awareness that some departmental services
do not generate revenue, and concern with non-competitive wages and employee turn-over.  Ms.
Broyles moved to amend to provide for a 3.4% increase in the sanitation fee schedule with a directive
that all full-time City employees, with the exception of uniformed Fire and Board of Public Utilities
employees, current and future will be compensated at a minimum of  not less than $12.00 per hour
effective July 1, 2013, seconded by Mr. Allen.  It was advised that currently there are approximately
36 employees who would be affected by the amendment.  Joe Dougherty, Cheyenne Transit Director,
spoke in support of the amendment and requested that permanent part-time Transit employees also be
considered relative to the intent of the amendment.  Rich Wiederspahn, Human Resources Director,
and Jim Elias, Public Works Director, spoke in favor of the amendment, commenting on the difficulty
of attracting employees at the City’s current starting salary, which is $10.50-$11.00 per hour. Upon
inquiry, Lois Huff, City Treasurer, provided information on estimated cost to implement the change
if the amendment was approved.  Upon inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney, clarified the amendment
being proposed is considered an oral amendment to the motion to approve on 2  reading, essentiallynd
earmarking a certain amount of fee revenue to be used to set a minimum wage for City employees at
$12.00 per hour, and if approved, language will be added to the ordinance that will be included on 3rd
reading of the ordinance.  M s. Broyles clarified her motion did not include permanent part-time
employees.  U pon inquiry  reg arding the def inition of a permane nt part-time employ ee, Rich
Wiederspahn explained there are part-time employees whose positions are considered to be permanent
positions and have an e xpectation of employ ment over time ve rsus seasonal employees who are
considered temporary employees.  Discussion topics included: Comments in support as well as non-
support of the a mendment; computing c osts to include permane nt part-time employ ees; diverting
income streams for the purpose of the amendment; transparency in accounting relative to sanitation
fees; suggestion of forming a task force to study and evaluate the sanitation revenue structure, and the
different ty pes of sanitation fee s.  Upon inquiry , Mr. Elias provided infor mation available on
automated vs. prior  manual sanitation pick-ups r elative to chang es in manpowe r, and c urrent
constraints involving labor and equipment to expand recycling program services.  Upon further inquiry
regarding use of temporary labor services, such as LaborReady contractual services, Mr. Elias advised
of the per hour cost to the City to hire temporary personnel through an employment agency and types
of work areas assigned to temporary personnel.  Mr. Brown raised a point of order that a discussion
topic involving sanitation tipping fees was not germane to t he motion to a mend on the floor with
Mayor Kaysen advising discussion should pertain to the motion to amend topic.  Following  further
comments on effect of the amendment on employees generally, motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes”
-- Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Mr.
Luna, Mr. Shanor and Ms. Williams.  Upon further inquiry, Mr. Elias explained that temporary labor
individuals (i.e. utilized through an employment agency) are not City employees; receive no City
benefits; that background checks and liability fall under the agency’s responsibility and policies; that
those individuals can not operate City vehicles; that duration of services is based on labor needs, and
that in addition to utilization of temporary labor for Sanitation, other City departments also utilize this
option for labor assistance on a temporary basis.  In response to Ms. Williams comments involving
various sanitation service areas, Mayor Kaysen requested she submit her questions in writing to Mr.
Elias to provide for complete and accurate answers.  Comments of conce rn followed on sanitation
operational costs, evaluation and transparency of same, and sanitation fee revenue being used to defray
general fund expenses for sanitation as well as other departmental expenses.  Main motion as amended
carried.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting
“no” -- Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Mr. Shanor and Ms. Williams.
ORDINANCE - Appropriating moneys for the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming for the various
purposes in conducting the municipal government of said City and fixing the amount of general and
special taxes as part of the r evenue required to meet the said appropriation, all for the F iscal Year
beginning July 1, 2013, and ending June 30, 2014.  A summary of proposed funds and budget amounts
are as follows:  General Fund $54,661,323; Weed and Pest $516,000; Youth Alternatives Grants
$391,602; Co mmunity F acility F ees $ 151,000; R ecreation Pr ograms $ 1,111,000; C ommunity
Development Block Grant $651,581; 1% Sales Tax Fund $12,607,309; Solid Waste Management Fund
$7,560,110; Law Enforcement Grants $179,609; Transportation Planning $828,209; FTA Transit
Grant $1,982,001; Juvenile Justice $311,757; Special Friends $134,173; Special Purpose Option Tax
$167,446; Youth Activities $13,100;  Golf Facilities $200,000; Civic Center $993,393; Ice and Events
Center $510,242, and Cemetery Perpetual Fund $20,000 -- Total City proposed budget $82,989,855.
Water Department $27,942,800, and Sewer Department $13,133,400 -- Total Board of Public Utilities
proposed budget $41,076,200, for a total proposed budget appropriation of $124,066,055.  Following
a five minute recess, Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at 7:30 p.m.  Lois Huff, City Treasurer,
advised she had issued a memo on this date to governing body members addressing concerns raised
at the Committee of the Whole meeting regarding recurring revenues.  She reviewed certain pages of
the budg et book that have  been r evised to  move $263,802 of e xpenditures f rom the Specia l
Distribution line item (state f unding) to the I mpact Assistance Distribution line item, which is not
restricted in use.  Rick Parish, Director of Parks and Recreation, and Rich Wiederspahn, Director of
Human Resources, commented in support of the proposed cost of living salary adjustment (COLA)
for City employees, noting municipal services provided and dedication by employees.  Included in Mr.
Wiederspahn’s comments was a request for separate consideration of the issue of a COLA apart from
the Employ ee I nvestment S tudy (EI S), which has not y et bee n rec eived in fina l form from the
consultant and involves new proposed salary ranges for job positions.  Also speaking in favor of the
COLA was Joe Dougherty, Director of Transit, who spoke in favor of providing the COLA to full-time
and permanent part-time employees, and Sara V asquez, Cheyenne Public Employ ees Association
(CPEA) President and Assistant Director of Pu rchasing.  M s. Vasquez encouraged City Council
members to not consider the topics of a COLA and the EIS in the same category, and to wait until the
EIS recommendations have been received to address any salary adjustment implementation plans.  She
spoke in favor of the proposed 2.25% COLA as initially presented in the budget, a nd ex pressed
concerns with potential unequitable employee salary adjustments due to the amendment made at the
Committee of the Whole meeting; pending firefighter labor union arbitration involving salaries; Board
of Public Utilities (BOPU) employee salary increases, and effect of the amendment passed earlier in
the meeting with regard to t he Sanitation fee ordinance.  Re porting for Committee of the Whole
(COW), Ms. Broyles moved to approve on 2  reading, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Pursuant to COWnd
recommendation, Ms. Broyles moved to amend as follows: “ ...That to the extent the various line items
in Section 1. of the budg et ordinance include the  appropriation of funds n ecessary to provide the
proposed 2.25% pay increase for City employees, said funds be held in re serve until the Employee
Investment Study is received and that said funds be re-appropriated from reserves at such times and
subject to such terms and conditions as the governing body shall determine, provided, however, that
this amendment shall not apply to those line items in Section 1. of the budget ordinance which include
provisions for the compensation of firefighters who are members of I.A.F.F. Local #279 or to those
line items in Section 1. which include provisions for the compensation of employees of the Board of
Public Utilities.  The 2.25% inc rease for the firefighters would not bec ome effective until after a n
arbitration award is entered.”  Mr. Shanor seconded the motion.  Following comments, Mr. Cook
moved to amend the amendment to incorporate a .50 cent per hour increase across the board for all
full-time employees effective July 1, 2013.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Cook confirmed Board of Public
Utilities and firefighters (union) employees would not be addressed as part of the motion.  Pursuant
to a point of clarification raised by Mr. Allen regarding wording of the motion, and upon request by
Mayor Ka ysen, Carol I ntlekofer, City  Clerk, re cited the wording of the motion to amend the
amendment.  Mr. Brown raised a point of order questioning whether the intent of the motion was to
replace the previous pending (COW) amen dment or if it was intended to somehow run concurrent
with the previous motion to amend.  Mr. Cook verified the intent of the motion is to propose a .50 cent
salary increase in lieu of the proposed 2.25% COLA and replace the previous amendment on the floor.
Mr. Brown seconded Mr. Cook’s motion to amend the amendment.  D an W hite, City Attorney,
cautioned that if the second amendment is intended to replace the first amendment, it may have an
effect on firefighters and BOPU employees, stating he was not certain that City Council has the ability
to micro-manage BOPU with regard to its employees’ salaries and he also was not certain what effect
the second amendment would have on firefighters’ salaries.  Mr. Dougherty commented in support of
the second amendment a lso including pe rmanent part-time employ ees.  D iscussion followed and
included the following topics: Proposed .50 cent per hour increase vs. 2.25% COLA; amendment(s)
not including firefighters at this time due to current ongoing arbitration; past BOPU employee study
that had been conducted; 3.4% consumer price index rate relative to the proposed 2.25% COLA; value
and priority rec ognition of employ ees; systemic problem re lative to an on- going plan for sa lary
compensation adjustments; history leading to the EIS being conducted and implementation procedure
envisioned, and status of anticipated receipt of the EIS.  During discussion Ms. Huff advised the cost
was approximately the same between a .50 cent or 2.25% COLA, as computed on the number of full-
time employees only, and verified all employees are paid on an hourly basis with some being classified
as salaried positions.  Tim Wilson, Director, Board of Public Utilities, advised the Board has approved
a 2.25% increase for its employees, consistent with what was being proposed in the City’s budget, to
be effective July 1, 2013.  He provided information pertaining to the BOPU comprehensive analysis
on employee positions and salaries conducted by an outside firm a few years ago, noting annual review
of compensation levels is conducted on a regional market basis.  Pursuant to comments on the EIS,
Mayor Kaysen provided an update on the study, advising the consultant planned to be in Cheyenne
next month and is working on completion of the three step study plan, and a work session with City
Council would be scheduled.  He further explained the time frame to receive the study was longer than
anticipated as the consultant had to re-build the nearly 20-year old current compensation plan.  Point
of clarification on the motion to amend the amendment, relative to the first motion to amend (i.e. COW
recommended amendment), was raised by Ms. Broyles, who noted her concern that each motion to
amend should be voted on separately, particularly due to pending firefighter arbitration and for fairness
reasons.  M ayor Kaysen advised if the second amendment is a pproved, it w ould replace the first
motion to amend, and that procedure-wise following the vote on the motion to amend the amendment,
then a vote w ould then be ca lled for on the initial motion to amend, potentially  as ame nded.
Continuing discussion included comments on: Focusing on dollars and certain levels of compensation
and market value pr ior to deciding on any per hour  raise a mount; support and non-support of the
motion to amend the amendment, and concern that the pending motion should exclude BOPU and fire
union employees.  Mr. Cook requested his motion to amend the amendment be withdrawn, advising
a different amendment may then be proposed to exclude BOPU and firefighters.  Mr. White stated
Robert’s Rules of Order is clear that once a motion has been made and seconded, the motion is the
property of the City  Council as a whol e and can not be withdraw n in the absenc e of una nimous
consent.  Mr. White reviewed the options available to address Mr. Cook’s request, which were to
either unanimously agree to Mr. Cook’s request to withdraw his motion, or to vote on his motion to
amend the amendment.  Following a point of order raised by Mr. Allen questioning whether discussion
had been closed, Mayor Kaysen called for further discussion by Council members.  Pursuant to inquiry
involving the leng th of time since the last COL A for e mployees w as appr oved, Mayor Ka ysen
provided information involving the history of COLA adjustments and one-time salary increases since
2009; budget constraints and revenue shortfalls in the subsequent fiscal budget year since that COLA
which affected the ability to provide increases, noting employee staffing reductions also occurred at
that time, and an across the board adjustment of $1,000 effective July 1, 2011.  He advised employees
have not received any type of compensatory relief since then as funds have not been available, citing
restrictions placed on recent state funds allocated to municipalities and noting that through the FY2014
budgeting process, revenue projections vs. expenses determined the  budget could support a 2.25%
COLA.  Upon further inquiry, Mayor Kaysen reviewed the history and initiation of the EIS, which was
funded by employee health insurance savings and was pursuant to a request to conduct the study from
employee groups.  He further informed that an employee committee, excluding himself, was formed
and the group interviewed responders to the EIS request for proposals; that the effort is employee-
driven and the committee is still i nvolved, and tha t it is envisioned that some employ ees will be
involved as part of the EIS implementation team once the study is received. Upon query, Ms. Vasquez
stated that CPEA had initially requested an across the board increase for the upcoming fiscal year and
that CPEA can support either the proposed across the board increase or COLA.  Motion to amend the
amendment failed.  Voting “no” -- all members of the governing body present.  Mr. Cook moved to
amend the amendment to approve a .50 cent an hour raise across the board for full-time employees.
Mr. White stated it would be helpful if the wording of the proposed amendment were clearly defined
relative to how the proposed amendment would change existing wording to the initial motion to amend
(i.e. COW recommended amendment).  M r. Brown raised a procedural point of order stating his
preference to separately address actions on the amendments.  Mr. Cook indicated he would not move
forward with completion of the wording of his motion.  Mr. Shanor moved to a mend the amended
budget (re: COW recommended amendment), explaining the proposed amendment is silent as to the
current amendment so that anytime it’s silent the remaining portions in the current amendment would
remain in effe ct, as follows: “ ...The  funds re garding employee raises placed into reserves by the
current amended budget be instead placed as a line item in the budget to be spent on employee raises
in FY2014 upon receipt of information regarding where each employee salary is compared to market
value from the consultant currently working on our employee investment study.  Under the current
employee classifications, any employee whose salary is determined by the consultant to be 2.25% or
more under maximum market value will be given a 2.25% raise immediately, retroactive to July 1,
2013.  Any employee whose salary is de termined by the consultant to be  2.24% t o . 1% u nder
maximum market value will be given a raise in a percentage that will bring that employee equal to
maximum market value but not over.  If any of these designated funds remain after raises are given,
such remainder shall be placed in reserves and utilized to implement an employee investment plan in
the FY2015 budget.”  Mr. Allen seconded the motion.  Ms. Vasquez commented on a past BOPU
position and compensation study  and spoke in support of the  2.25% COLA.  Mr. W iederspahn
explained the market survey portion of the EIS study was used to determine the pay plan and that the
value of a par ticular position, specific to the City  of Chey enne, was then de termined through the
consultant’s process which included employees’ questionnaires; discussions with supervisors and 10
factors utilized by the consultant to establish a particular range for a position and determine the value
of a position.  He discusse d elements involved in the differ ent factors; how changes in the marke t
(range) can occur, and cautioned the Council against being in the position of giving tacit approval of
the final study data which has not yet been seen or accepted.  Pursuant to comments concerning request
for clarity of the proposed motion, Mr. Shanor verified his proposed motion to amend the amendment
does not alter the first motion to amend (as recommended by COW), and would be an addition to the
wording contained in the motion to amend.  Mr. Brown raised a point of order requesting verification
that no amendments have  y et passed a nd questioned the motions on the floor.  May or Ka ysen
confirmed that no motions i nvolving amendments ha ve passe d.  Mr. White ex plained that Mr.
Shanor’s proposed amendment is intended to a dd to, and not chang e, the e xisting wording of the
recommended amendment from COW.  Mayor Kaysen advised of the motions before the body (i.e.
motion to amend (per COW recommendation) and subsequent motion to amend the amend ment;
explained procedurally the order in which the amendments would be acted upon, and confirmed there
were only two pending  amendments.  Upon r equest, Mr. Shanor  recited his moti on to amend the
amendment.  Continuing discussion included the following topics: Support and non-support of the
motion to amend the amendment; consultant being tasked with analysis of each position/salary area
in more finite detail than initially envisioned; comments that BOPU and firefighter positions should
be excluded fr om the amendments; support for employ ees a nd rec ognition of addressing  salary
inequities; amount of funds ava ilable to implement the proposed a mendment(s) and prioritizing of
same; long-range plan addressing compensation; compensating those employees under market value;
time frame that will be needed to completely put into effect all of the changes affiliated with the EIS;
timely adjustment for employees; working toward having a compensation plan in place for FY2015
following receipt of the study; effect of the amendment(s) on employee salaries relative to unknown
study data yet to be re ceived and appr oved; EIS providing a  minimum/maximum salary range for
positions; review and approval of the EIS recommendations by the governing body, and unknown
result of possible firefighter salary adjustments due to pending arbitration.  Pursuant to request during
discussion, Ms. Intlekofer read the initial moti on to amend (i.e. COW recommended amendment).
Upon inquiry, Ms. Huff advised that calculating salaries per the proposed motion on the floor will be
labor and time intensive to complete; Mayor Kaysen provided information on the intent of the EIS,
verifying that no current sala ries would be a djusted “down”  based on the  new pa y rang es, and
components of the study involving market analysis and development of a compensation plan based on
that data, a nd employ ee involveme nt during  the EI S process.  May or Ka ysen also provide d
information, per r equest, concerning the consultant’s prof essional services agreement, which wa s
approved through the City Council agenda process.  Ms. Broyles moved to strike the last sentence in
the motion to amend (i.e. COW recommended amendment) referencing that the 2.25% increase for
firefighters wouldn’t become effective until after an arbitration award.  Mr. White clarified the intent
of the effect of the pr oposed amendment as recommended by COW wit h regard to firefighter and
BOPU employees, and stated it is legally important to keep the 2.25% for firefighters in the budget by
retaining the last sentence in the COW recommended amendment.  He further stated his opinion that
based on specific state statutes relative to the Board of Public Utilities and its employees, the City
Council d oes n ot h ave the ri ght to  mic ro-manage B OPU wi th r egard to s alary a mounts fo r i ts
employees.  No second to the motion was received, and it was noted the motion on the floor was to
amend the amendment, not the initial motion to amend.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Wiederspahn stated the
consultant has indicated that market values can fluctuate based upon adjustments to the economy, and
provided information on the minimum, mean and maximum levels of pay ranges, noting specific
market data is not available for all positions; factors involving determination of the worth of a position,
and evaluation factors that will involve the City’s decision on how to implement the plan with regard
to placement of positions (salaries) within the ranges.  M otion to amend the amendment carried.
Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Mr. Shanor and Ms. Williams.  Voting “no” -- Mr.
Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Motion to amend as amended carried.  Voting
“yes” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Mr. Shanor and Ms. Williams.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Brown,
Ms. Broyles, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Main motion as amended failed.  Voting “yes” -- Mr.
Allen, Mr. Luna, Mr. Shanor and Ms. Williams.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook,
Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.

be sponsoring a Calcutta to be held July 18, 2013 beginning at 7:00 p.m. at The Redwood Lounge,
2105 East Lincolnway, Cheyenne, Wyoming, in conjunction with the Cheyenne Frontier Days Wild
Horse Races (15% of the proceeds to benefit the CFD Scholarship Program).

Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for a restaurant liquor lic ense
application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Anong Larsen and Brett Larsen d/b/a Anong’s Thai
Cuisine, 620 Central Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming.  
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held June 10, 2013 at 6:00 p.m. in City
Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, to receive public comments regarding
the intent of the City  of Chey enne to submit an application to the W yoming Business Council’s
Business Ready Community, Downtown Development Grant Program,  for funding for the Capital
Basin Flood Control Project (Trunkline Interceptor). 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held June 10, 2013 at 6:00 p.m. in City
Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for proposed budgets for the City of
Cheyenne and Board of Public Utilities for Fiscal Year 2014. 
Under other business, Mayor Kaysen recognized the presence of County Commissioner Buck
Holmes at the meeting, and Mr. Shanor expressed appreciation to members of the public and City
Council for their input and  professional debate during the meeting.
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was officially
adjourned at 10:03 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
