MEETING: City Council Meeting Special Session | DATE: 2013-06-04 | CLIP_ID: 377 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 377

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
June 4, 2013
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne convened in a special meeting on this date
beginning at 5:30 p.m. in Council Chambers for the purpose of reconsidering the FY2014 budget
ordinance on 2  Reading.  Present were:  MAYOR: Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS -nd
Mr. B rown, Ms. B royles, Mr . Co ok, M r. L una, D r. Rin ne, M r. Sha nor, Mr . V aldez an d M s.
Williams.  Ab sent: Mr. Allen.  Also present were: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City
Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Reconsider Ordinance - 2  Reading - Approp riating moneys for the City  of Chey enne,ND
Wyoming, for the various purposes in conducting the municipal government of said City and fixing
the a mount o f g eneral and special ta xes a s p art of  the  re venue re quired to  me et th e sa id
appropriation, all for the Fiscal Year beginning July 1, 2013, and ending June 30, 2014.  Ms. Broyles
indicated that a t the May 28  City Council meeting  the motion to approve on 2  reading theth nd
ordinance involving the FY2014 budget failed and, as she voted on the prevailing side and pursuant
to Chapter 2.04 of City Code, moved to reconsider the vote taken on the ordinance, seconded by Mr.
Brown.  Upon re quest, Carol I ntlekofer, City Clerk, re ad the e ntitlement of the ordina nce to be
reconsidered as follows: Ordinance on 2  Reading Appropriating moneys for the City of Cheyenne,nd
Wyoming, for the various purposes in conducting the municipal government of said City and fixing
the a mount of general and special taxes as part of the revenue required to meet th e sa id
appropriation, all for the Fiscal Year beginning July 1, 2013, and ending June 30, 2014.  Ms. Broyles
stated that subsequent to the ordinance failing on 2  reading, and after reviewing the governingnd
body’s statutory responsibility under Wyo. Stat. §16-4-111(a), she was requesting support for the
motion to reconsider to mee t compliance with state law that requires the City to make necessary
appropriations and adopt the budget within 24 hours of the conclusion of the budget public hearing,
which is scheduled for 6:00 o’clock p.m. on June 10 .  Motion to reconsider carried.  Voting “yes” --th
all m embers of  the  g overning bo dy pr esent.  Ma yor Ka ysen advi sed that since the  mot ion to
reconsider carried, the effect of the motion is to immediately place before the governing body the
main motion (as amended) to approve the budget ordinance on 2  reading as it was prior to the votend
taken at the May 28  meeting.  Sara Vasquez, President, Cheyenne Public Employees Associationth
(CPEA), advised that the Association “stands firm” on its support for a 2.25% COLA (cost of living
adjustment) effective July 1, 2013 as propose d in the in itial budget.  Maurine B ader, Wyoming
Liberty Group, e xpressed concern that taxpayers would be  burdened with paying for the cost of
salary increase, and referenced the stagnant economy and recent Bureau of Labor statistics indicating
Wyoming employment is shrinking.  She urged Council members to consider where the funds would
come from for the increases, and suggested the City eliminate services that compete with or could
be pr ovided mo re e fficiently by the  pr ivate se ctor.  D iscussion in cluded th e fo llowing top ics:
Information contained in a letter received on this date by governing body members from The Mercer
Group (consultant providing services for the Employee Investment Study (EIS)); data affiliated with
proposed COLA for e mployees; services to be pr ovided by and pay ments made to date to The
Mercer Group pursuant to contract and time frame in which to receive study results, and reference
to the Merc er Group letter and attorney-client information from the City  Attorney received by
governing body members associated with the previously approved (i.e. at the May 28  City Councilth
meeting) amendment to the main motion.  Upon inquiry during discussion, Mayor Kaysen provided
information pertaining to the proposed COLA relative to the annual Southeast Wyoming Consumer
Price Index; COLA’s for employees not provided on an annual basis due to past budgets not being
able to support an increase; last COLA provided in 2009 with a flat amount increase of $1,000 in
2011; additional scope of work requested of the consultant which has affected the time frame in
which to re ceive the study  (re: reconstructing the City ’s 20 y ear old pay  plan and e valuation of
certain job titles and positions that currently share common job titles but consist of different types
of duties per the departments involved; elements of the EIS involving consultant services providing
for review of job titles, positions and responsibilities; employees’ questionnaires, review and input
by managers, creation of a ne w pay plan (mar ket based), and e valuation of re quisite number of
employees needed in the va rious City divisions.)  He furthe r advised the  consultant anticipates
completing and submitting the EI S later this month.  Upon inquir y, Lois Huff, City  Treasurer,
advised that to date The Mercer Group has been paid approximately $38,000.  Comments followed
on the previous amendment (per the May 28  meeting) relative to information containe d in Theth
Mercer Group letter and the attorney-client communication from the City Attorney.  Ms. Broyles
raised a procedural question with regard to her intent to amend the main motion to strike the previous
amendment and whether the entire wording of the amendment was required to be stated as part of
the motion.  For informational purposes, Mayor Kaysen requested the City Clerk read the previously
approved amendment in its entirety, which was then read as follows by Ms. Intlekofer:  “That to the
extent the various line items in Section 1 of the budget ordinance include the appropriation of funds
necessary to provide the proposed 2.25% pay increase for City employees, said funds be held in
reserve until the Employee Investment Study is received and that said funds be re-appropriated from
reserves a t su ch ti mes a nd su bject to  su ch te rms a nd c onditions as the Governing B ody sh all
determine, provided, however, that this amendment shall not apply to those line items in Section 1
of the budg et ordinance whic h include provisions for the  compensation of Fire fighters who a re
members of I.A.F.F. Local #279 or to those line items in Section 1 which include provisions for the
compensation of employees of the Board of Public Utilities.  The 2.25% increase for the Firefighters
would not become e ffective until after  an ar bitration awa rd is enter ed.  The  funds re garding
employee raises placed into reserves by the current amended budget be instead placed as a line item
in the budget to be spent on employee raises in FY2014 upon receipt of information regarding where
each employee salary is compared to market value from the consultant currently working on our
Employee Investment Study.  Under the current employee classifications, any employee whose salary
is determined by the consultant to be 2.25% or more under maximum market value will be given a
2.25% raise immediately, retroactive to July 1, 2013.  Any employee whose salary is determined by
the consultant to be 2.24% to .1% under maximum market value will be given a raise in a percentage
that w ill br ing that employee e qual to  ma ximum ma rket va lue bu t no t ov er.  I f a ny of  the se
designated funds remain a fter raises are given, such re mainder shall be placed in re serves and
utilized to implement an employee investment plan in the FY2015 budget.”  Ms. Broyles moved to
amend by striking the previously approved amendment (as read by the City Clerk), seconded by Mr.
Brown.  Discussion fol lowed on acknowledging receipt of the letter f rom The Me rcer Group,
whether the preliminary recommended pay plan referenced in the letter might be available to City
Council members, and support for the motion to amend to remove the previous amendment from the
main m otion du e to t he inf ormation t hat h ad b een r eceived f rom th e c onsultant a nd the  City
Attorney.  Pursuant to query  to and comments by  City Council members as to their pr eference
regarding The Mercer Group letter being publicly acknowledged, Mayor Kaysen requested the City
Clerk read the letter into the rec ord.  Ms. I ntlekofer noted the date  of the letter  as June 4, 2013,
addressed to Dear Distinguished Members of Council, and read the content of the letter in its entirety
as follows: “I hope this finds all of you well.  Several members of Council have requested that I
provide data for use in the determination and identification of employees who would be eligible for
a Cost Of Living Adjustment (COLA).  First and foremost, I must tell you that, in my professional
opinion, the City of Cheyenne currently has no official data, information or policy framework upon
which to deny any employee a Cost Of Living Adjustment.  My reasoning follows.  A Cost of Living
Adjustment (COLA) is used in an effort to “keep a dollar a dollar” from one year to the next.  In
instances where a pay plan is in use by an organization, the COLA increases that pay plan by a
specified percentage while also increasing each eligible employee’s pay by that same percentage.
To be eligible for the entire COLA amount an employee must simply be at the maximum of the pay
plan or less.  Employees who are at the maximum will remain at the maximum as the pay plan is
increased by the same percentage as the employees’ salaries are increased.  Employees who are
initially above the maximum of the pay plan, but upon the adjustment of the plan by the COLA
percentage would be lowe r than the  new maximum, would be ra ised to the new ma ximum but
receive less than the full COLA percentage.  Employees whose pay exceeds the current maximum
of their range by more than the  COLA percentage would not receive an adjustment in pay.  The
above scenario is for organizations that have and use a pay plan.  In my opinion, this is not the case
with the City; Cheyenne’s current pay plan is more than seventeen years old and has little, if any,
reflection of the functional hierarchy of classified positions or the parameters of the labor market in
which Cheyenne must complete for employees.  As such, the City has no regulatory or procedural
basis upon which to deny any employee a Cost Of Living Adjustment based on their relative position
within the pay plan.  In the Compensation and Classification Study that I have been working on I
have collected and analyzed data and information pertaining to appropriate pay ranges, pay grades,
and position classifications for the City’s employees.  I have developed a preliminary recommended
pay plan.  None of this data has been reviewed, discussed, or approved by Council.  As such, it
currently has no standing in the decision regard COLA awards or other salary increases.  Further, the
current discu ssion seems to relate to identify ing e mployees w ithin 2.25% of the pre liminary
recommended minimums and maximums of their preliminary recommended pay grade in an effort
to identify those who would and would not receive a COLA.  In so doing, by identifying employees
through the use of unofficial data who would receive a pay increase, you are no longer discussing
a COLA.  Such an award of salary increases in this manner becomes a free-standing policy driven
salary increase for a subset of employees based on preliminary, unofficial data.  This decision is
much more open to interpretation at numerous regulatory levels and, in my opinion, exposes the City
to far reaching liability based upon the appearance of possible discriminatory practices.  As an added
consideration, were you to use this unofficial data in the award of salary increases to a subset of your
employees this same data would not be the data brought forth in the litigation or regulatory appeal
of your decision; your “current” pay plan would be viewed as the only official pay plan in use for
the employees of Cheyenne and the presumptive document that informed and drove your decisions.
One further consideration: using unofficial data while having been apprised beforehand in writing
regarding the possible discriminatory appearance and ramifications in u sing that unofficial data
would further weaken the City’s case.  It continues to be a pleasure working with the City.  Best
regards, Phillip G. Robertson, Sr. Vice President, The Mercer Group, Inc.”  Comments followed in
support of the amendment removing the previous amendment to the main motion.  Mayor Kaysen
advised he will make a request to Mr. Robertson with regard to the inquiry concerning whether the
preliminary recommended pay plan could be pr ovided, noting that Mr. Robertson anticipates the
final recommended pay plan to be submitted later this month.  Motion t o amend carried.  Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body present.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes”
-- Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” --
Mr. Luna, Mr. Shanor and Ms. Williams.  Mayor Kaysen advised a special meeting of City Council’s
Committee of the Whole would be held on June 6, 2013 at 5:30 p.m. to review the budget ordinance
on 3  reading; that the budget public hearing would be held at 6:00 p.m. during the regular June 10rd th
City Council meeting, and that the budget ordinance would be addressed on 3  and final reading atrd
the June 10  meeting, noting all meetings are open to the public.th
There being no further business previously noted for the special meeting, Mayor Kaysen
adjourned the special meeting at 6:06 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 June 10, 2013

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2013-06-10 | CLIP_ID: 379 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 379

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 June 10, 2013
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr.
Valdez and Ms. Williams.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer, and
Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Mr. Allen  moved to approve Consent Agenda items, seconded by
Ms. Broyles.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on May 28, 2013.  Motion (per Consent
Agenda) carried.
[CA] Minutes from Special Meeting of the Governing Body on June 4, 2013.  Motion (per Consent
Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Ameri-Tech
HVAC Services) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Valdez  moved to approve payment of vouchers
as presented, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Upon inquiry, Mayor Kaysen advised a voucher payable to AM
Signal Inc. ($7,665.80 for a solar powered school zone flashing beacon) is a cooperative effort and
shared expense with the school district.  Upon inquiry regarding a voucher payable to Labor Ready
Center, Inc. ($7,292.13) relative to amounts paid for temporary laborers for the Transfer Station, Jim
Elias, Public W orks Director, provided information on the number  of individuals involved in the
billing; hours worked; tasks assigned, and reasons for and benefits of utili zing temporary laborers
through the ag ency.  Upon f urther inquiry, Mr. Elias stated the individuals ar e not per manent or
seasonal City employees and receive no benefits.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body present in Council C hambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. B rown
returned to Council Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive comments and input for a restaurant liquor
license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Anong Larsen and Brett Larsen d/b/a Anong’s
Thai Cuisine, 620 Central Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Upon inquiry, Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk,
confirmed the application is for a new restaurant liquor license at the site.  There being no further
comments and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing and  referred
the application to Finance Committee.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive comments and input regarding the intent of
the City of Cheyenne to submit an application to the Wyoming Business Council’s Business Ready
Community, Downtown Development Grant Program,  for funding for the Capital Basin Flood Control
Project (Trunkline Interceptor).  Cindy Schneider, Grants Manager, advised the grant is to help with
funding to divert flood waters away from the downtown and west edge areas and will set the stage for
further economic development.  She stated the total amount of the project is $4,200,000.00 and a grant
application for mineral royalty funds has also been submitted, noting that grant, if approved, as well
as 6  Penny funds earmarked for the project can go toward the City’s match and other grants may alsoth
be available.  There being no further comments and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor
Kaysen closed the hearing.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing for comments and input on the proposed budgets for
the City of Cheyenne and Board of Public Utilities for Fiscal Year 2014.  There being no comments
and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing.
ORDINANCE - Approving and establishing revised water and sewer rates, tap and system
development fees, administrative fees, service charges and penalties and septic and commercial waste
dump rates, as recommended by the Board of Public Utilities.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr.
Brown moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Motion carried.  Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Cook voting “no”. (#3975)
 
ORDINANCE - Amending the City of Cheyenne’s Sanitation Fee Schedule. Maurine Bader,
Wyoming Liberty Group, spoke in opposition to the  proposed fee increases, commenting on
accountability to taxpayers, costs to citizens and the need to research operational efficiencies.  Bill
Henderson suggested designating a fund to rese rve revenues for future alloca tion due to unknown
factors th at c ould a ffect t he b udget, s uch a s f irefighter a rbitration a nd r esults o f th e E mployee
Investment Study (EIS), and requested consideration of keeping the current landfill dumping quantity
of up to 2,000 lbs. that Cit y residents can  submit in the fe e schedule.  Victor Cicc one expressed
concerns with increasing rates and the effect on those with fixed incomes, and questioned the need for
installing GPS (global positioning system) in sanitation trucks.  Jim Elias, Director of Public Works,
explained components of the new waste management software, which includes use of the GPS, and
advised the system will provide for pick-up route efficiencies and that research reflects users of these
types of systems have experienced cost savings.  In response to comments, Mayor Kaysen clarified that
use of sanitation revenues to cover other City operations has been a practice for decades.  Mr. Brown
moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Ms. Broyles raised a point of
clarification questioning if members did not wa nt to support any increases but did want to support
other portions of the schedule, could the question be divided.  Mayor Kaysen reviewed the procedure
to amend the ordinance.  Broyles moved to amend by striking any sanitation fee increase but keeping
the seven paragraphs noted at the end of the or dinance in place, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Mr. Elias
clarified the seven paragraphs were contained in a staf f cover memorandum and not as part of the
ordinance, which includes the attached fee schedule, and encouraged consideration of keeping the 12
notes outlined in the fee schedule.  Ms. Broyles revised her motion to change the reference to the seven
paragraphs to  ins tead in clude No tes 1  thr ough12 a s c ontained in  the  pr oposed s chedule, w ith
concurrence by Mr. Allen as second to the motion.  Mr. Hende rson commented on GPS costs over
time, referencing the general finance Rule of 72, and suggested review of full life cycle costs.  Point
of clarification was raised by Mr. Shanor pertaining to wording of the motion to amend and, upon
inquiry, Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, stated her interpretation of the revised motion on the floor was
to strike any fee increases but keep Notes 1 through 12.  Discussion included the following topics:
Notes contained at the end of the proposed fee schedule, specifically Note 3.; continuing to allow City
residents to dump up to 2,000 lbs. at the landfill without a fee; customer service concerns; sanitation
pickup routes and procedures; verification that an a mendment to the ordinance, pursuant to action
taken at the May 28, 2013 Council meeting to include a $12.00 per hour minimum wage for certain
full-time City  employ ees, w as still part of the pend ing ordinance, and c onfirmation that if the
amendment on t he floor w as approved it would in essence  remove the intent of Section 1. of the
ordinance.  U pon inquiry during discussion, Vicki Nemecek, Assistant Director of Public Works,
explained the paragraphs outlined in the staff memo for areas proposed to be included in the notes
section of the fee schedule, and reviewed proposed new areas contained in the fee schedule and notes.
Mr. Elias provided information on Note 3.; sanitation routes; customer services that are provided and
costs/personnel time involved in certain services, and, upon inquiry, noted benefits of implementing
the new G PS in vehicles.  During  comments on the GPS, Dr. Rinne raise d a point of orde r that
discussion was not germane to the motion on the floor as f unding for the GPS is included with the
budget ordinance, not the sanitation fee ordinance.  Following verification of the motion on the floor,
motion to amend failed.  Voting “yes” -- Ms. Broyles, Mr. Luna and Mr. Shanor.  Voting “no” -- Mr.
Allen, Mr. Brown, Mr. Cook, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Valdez, Ms. Williams and Mayor Kaysen.  Ms. Broyles
moved to amend by striking Section 3. of the ordinance, which was intended, by  previous amendment
made at the May  28, 2013 Counci l meeting, to br ing certain employees up to a  minimum salary
amount of $12.00/hour, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Comment followed that it may be proposed that the
topic might be a ddressed differently than including it in the pending or dinance.  Motion to amend
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Cook voting
“no”.  Discussion followed on the Landfill Dumping Fees portion of the fee schedule relative to the
per ton fee with Mr. Elias providing information on the types of waste being submitted and fees based
on weight.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend by striking the proposed increase to the cost per ton from the
Landfill Dumping Fee section, which would keep the  current provision in place, se conded by Mr.
Shanor.  Dr. Rinne revised (clarified) his motion to amend to reflect, in the fee schedule, that City
residents can dump up to 2,000 lbs. per four month period for free.  Mr. Shanor, as second to the initial
motion, concurred with the revision.  Mr. Ciccone requested clarification of the proposed amendment
with regard to the procedure utilized to ascertain the 2,000 lb. limit and questioned how much money
the compost fac ility raises r evenue-wise for the City .  Mr. Elias advised loa ds are weighed at the
landfill and records are kept and when the same customer comes back with another load, the weights
are added to previous weight amounts to track the 2,000 lb. limit, and that compost facility revenue
is approximately $115,000 per year.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body.  Upon inquiry involving roll-off containers (pursuant to Note 9.) and various bin fees,
Mr. Elias reviewed current fees; explained problems involving overweight roll-off containers and the
reasons for assessing charges for overweight containers; confirmed that private haulers are allowed
to place their containers in the City pursuant to a past change in city code, and provided information
on curbside bins.  Mr. Br own raised a point of order  pertaining to wha t the question on the floor
involved with Mayor Kaysen clarifying the main motion on the floor is to approve the ordinance as
amended.  Discussion followed on use of sanitation revenue to off-set other City services; other City
services that don’t involve reve nue generation; diversifying the City’s funding stream to help f und
operations; transparency involving operational costs; limited revenue sources available to the City and
effect on the general fund, including staffing and current level of services, if sanitation fees are not
used to cover costs for other operations and services.  Main motion as twice amended failed.  Voting
“yes” -- Mr. Brown, Dr. Rinne, M r. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Vo ting “no” -- Mr. Allen, Ms.
Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Mr. Shanor and Ms. Williams.
ORDINANCE - Appropriating moneys for the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming for the various
purposes in conducting the municipal government of said City and fixing the amount of general and
special taxes as part of the r evenue required to meet the said appropriation, all for the Fiscal Year
beginning July 1, 2013, and ending  June 30, 2014.  B ill Henderson spoke in support of the budg et
process, noting it can be amended later if needed, and commented that there were potential areas where
rates could be incr eased.  Reporting  for Committee of the Whole, Dr. R inne stated there was no
recommendation from t he C ommittee.  Dr . R inne m oved t o ap prove o n t hird an d fi nal reading,
seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Mr . Shanor moved to amend the budget by directing that $615,000 in
recurring expenditures be cut from the FY2014 proposed budget under the following conditions: 1.
That $615,000 currently proposed in appropriated money for a 2.25% raise to City employees remain
intact in its entirety and no other cuts will be made to any part of currently proposed City employee
compensation and benefit packages; 2. That the currently proposed recurring revenue estimates and
recurring expenditure estimates remain as is and not be altered to accommodate this $615,000 cut; 3.
That nonrecurring funds, specifically the $1,890,785 million in state special distribution funds and the
roughly $1,748,220 in impact assistance capital investment funds, will not be used to cover this cut
in recurring expenditures; and 4. As part of this amendment the Council grants the administration the
authority to determine how this $615,000 cut will be made subject to these conditions.  Mr. Henderson
spoke in support of the amendment and to keep the amount in reserves.  Upon inquiry, Lois Huff, City
Treasurer, confirmed the City’s portion of the increase to employee retirement pension cost is included
in the $615,000.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend the amendment by striking “and recurring expenditure
estimates” contained in Condition No. 2., seconded by  Mr. Allen.  During comment on addressing
salary adjustments for employees, Mr. Brown raised a point of order that comments should pertain to
the pending motion to amend the amendment.  Motion to amend the amendment carried.  Voting “yes”
-- all members of the governing body.  Discussion followed on proposed employee salary increases;
revenue constraints, and conc ern w ith Condi tion No. 4. relative to a dministrative vs. Council’s
responsibility in determining where reductions will be made to meet the $615,000 cut and effect on
staffing levels and reduction of services.  Upon inquiry regarding potential legal concerns involving
Condition No. 4., Dan White, City Attorney, expressed his belief tha t the authority granted to the
administration in this condition could represent an unconstitutional or unlawful delegation of power
by City Council and could be inc onsistent with state statutes.  Mr. Shanor  moved to amend the
amendment, seconded by Mr. Allen, as follows: In lieu of paragraph (i.e. condition) 4. of the pending
amendment, which is in relation to the administration’s discretion to administer the $615,000 cut as
it deems appropriate, the following will be inserted: To effectuate this $615,000 cut, the City budget
will be cut by 1.125% across the board by each department’s individual budget being decreased by
1.125% in the numbers below: City Council, $272,592; Mayor, $2,133,960; City Clerk, $2,771,029;
Public Works, $11,087,848; Police, $11,730,919; Fire, $9,092,141; Parks and Recreation, $5,630,325;
Treasurer, $5 94,426; E ngineering, $ 878,179; P lanning Se rvices, $1 ,867,005; G eneral A ccounts,
$6,385,404, and Support Servic es $1,602,551, for a  total amended budg et of $54,046,383.  Mr.
Henderson commented on use of City reserves as an option and proposed salary increases vs. a COLA
(Cost of Living Adjustment).  Nick Winslow questioned how much money the City will receive due
to the increase in state fuel tax effective July 1, 2013 with Ms. Huff advising the projected revenue has
been calculated and is included in the proposed budget, noting the revenue will be used for roads and
street construction.  Discussion followed on concern with legal issues, unknown fire union arbitration
results and use of recurring revenue for proposed salary increases.  Upon inquiry, Carol Intlekofer, City
Clerk, advised the  amendment c annot be f urther amended at this time as there  were already two
pending motions to amend on the floor.  Motion to amend the amendment failed.  Voting “no” -- all
members of the governing body.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend the amendment, seconded by Mr. Luna,
as follows: I n lieu of par agraph 4 . of the pending amendment whic h is in relation to the
administration’s discretion to administer the $615,000 cut as it deems appropriate, the following will
be inserted: To effectuate this $615,000 cut, the City budget will be cut by 1.125% across the board
by each department’s individual budget being decreased by 1.125% as reflected in the numbers below
(re: same listing with dollar amounts as included in the previous motion to amend the amendment)
except under Fire strike the amount of $9,092,141, and insert the Fire Department budget will be cut
by 1.125% o r only to the extent we don’t violate the collective bargaining agreement, for a total
amended budget of $54,046,383, minus the Fire Department amount.  Pursuant to inquiry regarding
wording of the motion, Mr. Shanor revised his motion to add at the end of the wording “... for a total
amended budget of $54,046,383, minus the fire department amount and in addition to whatever the
Fire Department budget ends up being under these conditions.  Mr. Luna, as second to the motion to
amend the a mendment, concurred with the revised w ording of the motion.  Ms. Huff que stioned
verification of the dollar amount that would be listed in the total amount of the budget ordinance if the
amendment we re a pproved ( i.e. orig inal budget amount of $9,195,592 or a  revised a mount of
$9,092,141 reflecting a 1.125% cut, which represents $103,451).  Mr. Shanor stated preferably the
amount of $9,195,592 would be cut by 1.125% but that may not be achievable if it violates the fire
collective bargaining agreement, and commented on either budgeting for that amount and having the
provision subject to an analysis of the collective bargaining agreement or leaving it open ended that
the Fire Department budget will be cut by  1.125% or to the e xtent that the collec tive bargaining
agreement will allow.  Ms. Huff expressed he r belief that she nee ded to inclu de a definite dollar
number for the total budget and couldn’t do a narrative budget.  Upon inquiry, Mr. White advised of
his concerns with the proposed amendment stating the Fire Department currently includes 3 firefighters
employed pursuant to federal grant funds under the SAFER program and that, before approving the
amendment, it would be helpful to analyze grant requirements to ensure the amendment would not
affect the City’s obligations under the grant agreement.  He further stated that the topic of who decides
whether we a re in violation of the c ollective labor agreement is a conc ern as the fire  union may
disagree with a decision and may file a request for arbitration, which would then involve an arbitrator
possibly making a budget decision, which could be an unconstitutional delegation of authority.  Upon
inquiry to Mr. White regarding whether potential liability could exist if one budget is not cut but cuts
to other budgets are effected, Mr. White stated the basic idea being presented for an across the board
cuts to all depar tments by 1.125% would proba bly be leg al but there  is a conc ern when the Fire
Department is included as it raises other issues that have to be carefully analyzed.  Mr. Shanor moved
the previous question.  Joe Dougherty, Transit Director, stated that from his experience with the City’s
budgetary process, the budget has to have a fixed number.  Mr. Allen seconded the motion to move
the previous question (call for the question to l imit debate).  Mo tion carried.  Vo ting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body.  Pursuant to a point of order  raised by Dr. Rinne, Mayor Kaysen
clarified the motion just approved was to close debate on the motion to amend the amendment and,
for informational purposes, inquired of Ms. Huff whether a COPS federal grant involving funding for
the Police Department officers might also affect the proposed cuts.  Ms. Huff advised the COPS grant
has expired but the City is required to retain 3 police officers funded through the grant through Dec.
31, 2013 and that amount needs to remain in the budget and if the 1.125% cut involving the motion
to amend the amendment is approved, the Police Department can not cut funding for those positions.
Motion to amend the amendme nt failed.  Voting  “no” -- all members of the governing body.  Mr.
Shanor moved to amend the amendment as follows: In lieu of paragraph 4. of the pending amendment
which is in relation to the administration’s discretion to administer the $615,000 cut as it  deems
appropriate, the following will be inserted: To effectuate this $615,000 cut, the City budget will be cut
by 1.125% ac ross the boar d by each department’s individual budge t being decreased by 1.125%,
excluding the Fire Department, as reflected in the numbers below (re: same listing with dollar amounts
as included in the previous motion to amend the amendment with the subtraction of the  amount listed
for Fire) for a total amended budget amount.  Pursuant to a request for clarification by Ms. Huff, Mr.
Shanor clarified his motion is to leave the Fire Department’s initial budget amount of $9,195,592 as
listed in the ordinance and that the total amended budget amount, if the motion is approved, would be
$54,149,835.  Mr. Luna seconded the motion to amend the amendment.  Comment followed that by
amending the budget amount, the effect is that the “numbers” are no longer reconciled.  Following a
10 minute recess, Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at 8:34 p.m.  Verification was made of the
wording of the motion on the floor and comment was offered that support for the amendment not be
provided to allow for reconciliation of the amended budget total.  Motion to amend the amendment
failed.  V oting “ no” --  all members of the governing body .  Mr. Shanor moved to a mend the
amendment as follows:  In lieu of paragraph 4. of the pending amendment which is in relation to the
administration’s discretion to administer the $511,549 cut as it deems appropriate, the following will
be inserted: To effectuate this $511,549 cut, the City budget will be cut by 1.125% across the board
by each department’s individual budget being decreased by 1.125%, excluding the Fire Department,
as reflected in the numbers below (re: same listing with dollar amounts as included in the pre vious
motion to  amend the amendment with the amount now listed for  Fire as $9,195,592) f or a total
amended budget amount of $54,149,835.  Mr. Allen seconded the motion.  Sara Vasquez, president,
Cheyenne Public Employees Association, expressed concerns that the proposed 1.125% budget cuts
could result in staff reductions in force (RIF) measures as well as reduced services.  Claus Johnson,
City employee, reiterated similar concerns expressed by Ms. Vasquez and further commented on past
budget constraints resulting in RIFs and early retirement offerings and effect on remaining employees
to “do more with less”.  He stated his opinion that COLAs (Cost of Living Adjustments) and raises
are  differ ent issues and c ommented further that the employ ee-driven EIS (Employee Investment
Study) wa s to assist with hi ring and kee ping qua lified, trained sta ff to provide service s.  Mr.
Henderson aske d whether  exis ting c ontract service s offe red any  opportunity  for a mounts to be
amended, and if that might be a funding source for the proposed 2.25% COLA increase for employees,
and posed possible contractual areas that may be increased or decreased to cover the COLA cost.
Mayor Kaysen provided information on the different types of contracts the City has, noting that some
may have provisions to renegotiate terms while others do not; advised some contracts involve sales
tax funds and grants, and provided information on employee health coverage.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Huff
advised health care costs to the City is approximately $6 million a year.  During comments offered by
James Perrot Howard McNeil Pershing regarding Swan Ranch developers, Mr. Brown raised a point
of order that comments were not germane to the motion on the floor with Mayor Kaysen concurring.
Ms. Huff commented on discussions she was involved in with department/division heads during the
budget preparation process and complimented them on efficiently managing their budgets, and effect
of additional budget reductions.  She advised the $615,000 (for employee increases) is already included
in the budget, and commented generally on estimated revenue projections contained in the budget,
impact assistance funds and dollar amount involved in the previous agenda item that failed relative to
sanitation fee increases.  Rich Wiederspahn, Human Resources Director, referenced the difficulties
employees experienced during and after the 2010 RIFs; requested Council not lose sight of the human
side of the issue and look at alternatives, and do whatever can be done to keep employees.  Discussion
included the f ollowing topics: Proposed motion to amend the a mendment; unknown eff ect of fire
arbitration; proposed budget revenue and expenditure estimates; fiscal responsibility; 1% Sales Tax
expenditures, and request t o receive information from department heads advising of the effect a
1.125% cut would ha ve on their b udgets.  During  comments on 1% Sales Ta x expenditures, Mr.
Brown raised a point of order  that comments wer e not g ermane to the motion on the floor as 1%
monies aren’t in the general fund involving the motion.  Mayor Kaysen requested comments on 1%
Sales Tax expenditures and grants be held for discussion after the amendments had been dealt with.
Pursuant to the request made during discussion, the following Department/Division heads provided
information on the effect of a 1.125% cut to their respective budgets: Mr. Dougherty, Transit Director;
Chief Brian Kozak, Police Department; Chief Jim Martin, Fire and Rescue Department; Rick Parish,
Director of Parks and Recreation; Matt Ashby, Director of Planning Services; Jim Elias, Director of
Public Works; Rob Geringer, City Engineer; Ms. Intlekofer, City Clerk; Ms.Huff, City Treasurer; Mr.
Wiederspahn, Director of Human Resources, and Mr. White, City Attorney.  Also offering comments
was Gus L opez, Director, City -County Hea lth Depar tment.  Area s noted that could be a ffected
included: Employee staffing levels, equipment and supplies, reduced services; training; meeting the
public’s expectations for services, maintenance of grounds, facilities, computer hardware and software,
and meeting certain legal requirements.  Discussion and comments continued on statutory time frame
requirement in which to approve the budget; effect of a 1.125% cut to department budgets; Council’s
budgetary responsibility; whether the budget was “padded”; staff positions in some departments;
proposed income for the Ice and Events Center; concern with ongoing recurring expenses; effect of
affordable health ca re a ct a nd fede ral seque stration; budge t re-a ppropriation ava ilability; fire
arbitration, and looking  at the big  financial picture, long term, fr om a policy perspective.  Dur ing
discussion, va rious b udget in formation w as p rovided b y Ms.  Hu ff, a nd Mr . Pa rish responded to
inquiries conce rning depar tment part-time staff ing le vels, reve nue g enerated by  the g ymnastics
program, and Ice and Events Center revenue due to the recent loss of the Cheyenne Warriors contract.
Mayor Kaysen advised the budget that was presented is a conservative, lean budget.  Motion to amend
the amendment failed.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Luna, Mr. Shanor and Ms. Williams.  Voting
“no” -- Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Upon request,
Ms. Intlekofer read the pending motion to amend, as amended (i.e. Condition 2.), involving the cutting
of $615,000 in recurring expenditures and the four conditions contained in the motion.  Discussion
included the numerous amendments presented during the meeting; Council’s involvement in providing
viable solutions regarding areas to cut, and authority provided in Condition 4. which the City Attorney
previously stated could be unconstitutional.  Motion to amend failed.  Voting “no” -- all members of
the governing body.  Ms. Broyles moved to amend by raising every full-time employees salary working
for the taxpayers in the City of Cheyenne to a minimum salary of at least $11.53 per hour for FY2014;
this raise would bring  36 individuals to a basic minimum for a total compensation of $56,119.63,
which includes workers compensation, social security and retirement; funding for this amendment will
be supported from recurring revenues, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Upon inquiry by Mr. Dougherty on
whether the amendme nt would apply to permane nt part-time employees, Ms. B royles verified her
motion does not include permane nt part-time employees but that would be looked at in the future.
Discussion followed on the per hour amount proposed in the amendment relative to a 2.25% COLA;
bringing employees up to market value; funding source to pay for costs involved with the amendment;
waiting for EIS results that may affect salary levels, and written attorney-client advice previously
received by Council m embers from the City  Attorney.  On beha lf of the Council as a n entity, Mr.
White expressed his concern to the Council wit h regard to discussion involving his attorney -client
privileged communication, stating no individual member of Council has the right to waive the
privilege, and requested that any further discussion on this, or any attorney-client advice he provides,
cease immediately.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Huff confirmed that with a 2.25% COLA the $11.53/hour rate
would result in $11.79/hour an d provided information regarding some positions i nvolved in the
proposed amendment that involve grant funding.  She stated that the actual effect of the amendment
on the general fund would be about $48,000.  Motion to amend failed.  Voting “yes” -- Ms. Broyles
and Mr. Luna.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Mr. Cook, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez,
Ms. Williams and Mayor Kaysen.  Following comments, main motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- Mr.
Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Allen,
Mr. Luna, Mr. Shanor and Ms. Williams. (#3976)
Following a brief recess, Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at 10:53 p.m.
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, Zoning
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified Development
Code, changing the zoning classific ation from MR-2 Medium Density  Residential - De veloping
District to NR-3 Neighborhood Residential - High Density District for Lot 14, Block 1, Saddle Ridge,
Fifth Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the southwest corner of the intersection of Countryside
Avenue and Medicine Man Trail).  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Allen moved to
approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body with the exception of Ms. Williams voting “no”. (#3977) 
 
ORDINANCE  - Amending various Sections of Title 8, Health and Safety, Chapter 8.08, Alarm
Systems, of the Cheyenne City Code pertaining to Alarm Devices and Systems, and False Alarms.
Charlie Giroux stated his concerns that there was no separation difference between a residential alarm
and a business/commercial alarm, and the fee rate associated with a change in alarm systems.  Upon
inquiry, Chief Brian Kozak, Police Department, advised the ordinance will not go into effect until
September 1, 2013 and reviewed plans to educate the community and businesses through distribution
of information prior to that time.  He stated that officer response to false alarms is the greatest concern
which the ordinance will help address, noting that the same resources are expended whether response
is to a residence or business.  Mr. Brown moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by
Mr. Valdez.  Mr. Brown moved to amend Section 8.08.020 A. by reducing the permit amount from
$200.00 to $100.00, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  It was clarified this would be a one time permit fee for
new permittees, not an annual fee as is currently place, as long as the system is the same, and that
current permittees will pay $25.00 to effect the change.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body with the exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no”.  Mr. Shanor moved to
amend Section 8.08.055 B. by inserting the language “and permit fee amount” between the words
“City” and “prior” so the section would read “ ... Each alarm monitoring company and installer shall
notify its customers of the requirement to obtain an alarm permit from the City and permit fee amount
prior to finalizing any sales or service agreement for an alarm system”.  Ms. Broyles seconded the
motion.  Chief Kozak expressed support for the amendment.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes”
– all members of the governing body.  Upon inquiry concerning Section 8.08.030 C. and Council
members’ involvement in comprising the 3 member  panel relative to the a ppeal process involving
possible revocation or denial of a permit, Dan White, City Attorney, explained that when drafting the
ordinance he reviewed Chapter 5.04 of City Code which relates to the revocation of business licenses
and involves a contested case hearing before the full Council.  He stated he felt alarm permits do not
implicate constitutional gua rantees, such a s a business license doe s, and consider ing a  hear ing
procedure for citizens would be helpful, he determined a “sub-set” of Council members to serve on
the panel would be ade quate in lieu of the  entire governing body.   Dr. Rinne moved to amen d by
striking the second sentence in its entirety in Section 8.08.020 C. 1. b. which reads as follows: “ ... A
permittee may be subject to a fee of one hundred dollars ($100.00) if any responding police officer is
unable to establish contact with the permittee or with emergency contacts listed in the application for
the purpose of granting access to the alarm system location or deactivating the alarm.”  Motion was
seconded by Mr. Allen.  Mr. Giroux spoke in fa vor of the a mendment.  Chief Kozak advise d the
purpose of the section is due to the scenario of officers being on the scene, sometimes for hours, trying
to contact the responsible person and this is to encourage the permit holder to update their information.
 He noted that assessment of the fee is at the discretion of the police chief.  Comments followed on
communication unavailability at times, particularly involving cellphone contact attempts where lack
of cell coverage areas may exist; current wording of the sentence relative to “may be subject to a fee”
vs. lack of the  word “ shall”; responsibility of perm ittees to provide  current contact information;
suggestion that a reduced fee of $50.00 be considered, and effect of remaining wording in the section
if the amendment was approved.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Cook, Mr.
Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and Ms. Williams.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles
and Mayor Kaysen.  Main motion as amended three times carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body. (#3978)
ORDINANCE - Granting a Franchise by the City of Cheyenne to Cheyenne Light, Fuel and
Power Company, its successors and assigns, the right to furnish, sell and distribute natural gas to the
City and to all persons, businesses, and industry within a portion of the City in Cheyenne Light, Fuel
and Power  Company ’s Certifica ted Ter ritory and the r ight to ac quire, c onstruct, install, locate,
maintain, operate and extend into, within and through said City all facilities reasonably necessary to
furnish, sell and distribute natural gas within the City and the right to make reasonable use of all streets
and other public places and public easements as herein defined as may be necessary; and fixing the
terms and conditions thereof.  Mr. Brown moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by
Mr. Valdez.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#3979)
ORDINANCE - A mending Chapter 5.12,  Alcoholic B everages, Section 5.12.030(A) ,
Application–Fees–Procedures, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Cheyenne City
Code to authorize one (1) additional dispensing room for restaurant liquor licensees upon application
to and appr oval by  the Governing B ody of the City  of Chey enne pur suant to the provisions of
Wyoming Statute §12-4-410(b), as amended, effective July 1, 2013.  Mr. Brown reported there was
no recommendation from Finance Committee on the item.  Mr. Brown moved to approve on second
reading, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Upon inquiry, Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, provided information
on the purpose of  the ordinanc e, which is to ame nd City C ode to “mirror ” state statute c hanges
approved by the state legislative body at its most recent session, and explained the statutory change
provides the opportunity for current restaurant liquor licensees to request one additional dispensing
room in the same buildi ng and under  the same r equirements and re strictions as the initial liquor
license.  She noted a 2/3rds fee of the initial license is required and, upon further inquiry, stated to date
she had not received any inquiries or requests pursuant to the statute change.  Comments followed on
municipalities’ ability to be more restrictive than state statutes, and concern with employees ages 18
to 21 serving alcoholic beverages.  Motion failed (due to a tie vote).  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Brown, Mr.
Luna, Mr. Shanor, Ms. Williams and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Allen, Ms. B royles, Mr.
Cook, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Valdez. 
ORDINANCE - A mending Ordinance No. 3951 def ining the te rms and conditions of
employment for non-uniformed employees of the City of Cheyenne.  Mr. Brown moved to approve
on second reading, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Mr. Brown moved to postpone for two weeks (to June
24, 2013), sec onded by  Ms. Broy les.  I t was noted the r eason f or the F inance Commi ttee’s
recommendation to postpone was due to correlation of the proposed ordinance with the budget and
since the budget ordinance passed earlier in the mee ting, the pur pose for postponement no long er
existed.  Motion to postpone failed.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Luna.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown,
Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez, Ms. Williams and Mayor Kaysen.  Main
motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
ORDINANCE - A mending Ordinanc e No. 3952 de fining the terms and c onditions of
employment for sworn officers of the City of Cheyenne Police Department.  Mr. Brown moved to
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Mr. Brown moved to postpone for two weeks
(to June 24, 2013), seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Similar to the previous agenda item, it was noted that
the purpose for postponement no longer existed due to passage of the budget ordinance.  Motion to
postpone failed.  Voting “no” -- all members of the governing body.  Main motion carried.  Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body.
ORDINANCE - Amending various sections within Chapter 5.68, Body Art Establishments and

ORDINANCE - Amending Chapter 5.08, Business License Fees, Section 5.08.250, Body Art

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2013-06-24 | CLIP_ID: 381 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 381

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 June 24, 2013
The Go verning B ody of the C ity of  Che yenne me t in  re gular s ession on  thi s d ate
beginning at 6:00 p.m. in C ouncil Chambers.  Pre sent were: MAYOR - Richa rd L. Kaysen;
COUNCIL MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne,
Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and Ms. Williams.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff,
City Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There  will be no
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support
by two other membe rs is rec eived.  Any  item removed f rom the Consent Ag enda w ill be
considered in its normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Ms. Williams, and pursuant
to support received by Ms. Broyles and Mr. Allen, agenda item #22(a) was removed from the
Consent Agenda.  Mr. Allen moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by
Mr. Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. 
[CA] Minutes from Reg ular Meeting of the Gover ning Body on J une 10 , 2013.   Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
 
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Ameri-Tech
HVAC Services) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Allen moved to approve payment of vouchers
as presented, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Upon inquiry, various City department heads provided
information for their respective areas involving vouchers payable to the following:  Wyoming State
Bank (a dditional pr incipal pa yment f rom e xcess es crow f unds fo r s anitation le ase/purchase
equipment); Work Area Protections Corp. (traffic cones utilized for various Parks and Recreation
events and road closures); Welding Shop (solid waste dumpster container repairs; stenciling, pickup
and delivery); Waste Management (solid waste disposal fee for use of Ault, CO landfill); North Star
Pharmacy (pharmaceutical drugs for Fire and Rescue Dept. advanced life services); Laramie County
Clerk (re solution fili ng fee ); Color Graphics  (uniforms for  rec reational gir ls softball prog ram
included in registration fee) and AmeriMulch Dispersions LLC (dye to color wood chips (mulch)
sold at Compost  facility).  Motion car ried.  Voting  “yes” -- all member s of the g overning body
present in Council C hambers with the exception of Ms. Wi lliams voti ng “no”.  F ollowing
announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown returned to Council Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive comments and input pursuant to W.S. § 15-
1-112(b), regarding a proposed trade of certain City-owned land generally described as Lots 30 and
31, Block 1, Eastridge Addition, to 19  & Converse, LLC, a Colorado limited liability company, inth
exchange for 9,504 square feet of land, being a portion of Lots 1 and 2, Block 1, Ruth Addition, to
be  ac quired by the City  for r ight-of-way purposes in conne ction with the rec onstruction of the
Pershing Boulevard/Converse Avenue/19  St reet intersections.  Dan White, City Attorney, explainedth
the purpose of trading property involves a proposed settlement of the eminent domain case filed by
the City to acquire right-of-way at the former Hollywood Video business site for the round-about at
the intersection.  He provided information on the proposed trade involving lost parking spaces and
assessed valuation of the properties, and stated it was anticipated that the settlement agreement would
be introduced on the July 8  City Council meeting agenda.  Upon inquiry, Mayor Kaysen advisedth
the  City has traded other properties several times in the past and the process is in compliance with
state statute requirements; noted the trade is just one piece of the proposed settlement agreement and
indicated that, upon approval of the settlement agreement by the parties involved, there would be no
further action required regarding the eminent domain filing.  There being no further comments or
input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing.
ORDINANCE - A mending Ordinanc e No. 3951 de fining the terms and conditions  of
employment for non- uniformed e mployees of  the City  of Chey enne.  Re porting for F inance
Committee, Mr. Brown moved to  approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Valdez.
Motion carried.  Vo ting “yes” -- all members of the  governing body with the exception of Ms.
Williams voting “no”. (#3980)
ORDINANCE - Amending  Ordinanc e No. 3952 de fining the terms and c onditions of
employment for sworn officers of the City of Cheyenne Police Department.  Mr. Brown moved to
approve on third and final reading, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Upon inquiry, Mayor Kaysen advised
police officers have the same option to participate in the City’s wellness program as other employees.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#3981)
ORDINANCE - Amending various sections within Chapter 5.68, Body Art Establishments
and Operations, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Cheyenne City Code to provide
for a Body Art Special Event Business License and requirements for participating body artists and
guest body artists.  Gary  Hickman, Chey enne-Laramie County Health Department, advised the
ordinance was prepared at the request of local body artists who would like the opportunity to hold
special events in the community and to also allow non-local guest body artists to provide body art
services in local shops.  Mr. Brown moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.
Upon inquiry  reg arding whether  any  ty pe of r equirement for malpr actice insurance  should be
considered relative to temporary body art services and protection to local customers, Mr. Hickman
stated the ordinance does not require that type of coverage and no disc ussions on the topic have
occurred, noting  that ot her licenses, such as tempor ary food stands, do not have  an insuranc e
requirement.  Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, clarified the ordinance does provide for the applicant for
the special license to serve as the contact person and they must provide a register, containing daily
customer logs, from each body artist participating in the special event that will be kept on file with
the Health Department for communication or contact purposes.  Upon further inquiry, Dan White,
City Attorney, stated wording in the ordinance requires proof of general liability coverage for any
event held on City-owned property, which protects the interests of the City, and that they could look
into professional malpractice insurance to see if it’s even available and what the potential cost might
be.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr.
Allen and Dr. Rinne voting “no”.
ORDINANCE - Amending Chapter 5.08, Business License Fees, Section 5.08.250, Body Art
Establishments and Operations -- Body Artists, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the
Cheyenne City Code to implement a license fee for  Body Art Specia l Events.  Gar y Hickman,
Cheyenne-Laramie County Health Department, advised this is a companion ordinance to the previous
one establishing a license fee for special body art events.  Mr. Brown moved to approve on second
reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Comment followed on the proposed fee amount of $100.00. Ms.
Williams moved to amend the fee amount from $100 to $25.  Motion died due to lack of a second.
Upon inquiry, Mr. Hickman advised the Health Department will have a separate permit fee and Carol
Intlekofer, City Clerk, explained the City’s fee amount is to cover the cost of processing a business
license application including, for this type of license, on-site inspections.  Upon further inquiry, Ms.
Intlekofer verified the applicant, or promoter, of a special event would have to pay any facility rental
fees customarily assessed if , for e xample, the e vent venue would be held within a City -owned
facility, and woul d need to acquire any other re quired City licenses or  permits.  Motion carr ied.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Allen and Dr. Rinne
voting “no”.
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, Zoning
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified Development
Code, changing the zoning classification from LR-1 Low-Density Residential-Established to MUR
Mixed-Use Residential Emphasis for the south ½ of Lots 8 and 9, Block 3, Yellowstone Addition,
Cheyenne, W yoming (l ocated ea st o f a nd ad jacent t o Ye llowstone R oad, b etween De ll R ange
Boulevard and Carroll Avenue).  Ron Pinther, applicant requesting the zone change, read a Memo
dated June 24, 2013 (on file with City Clerk’s office) addressed to the Mayor and City Council
members indicating neighbors have stated they will support a sign variance permitting a low-profile
ground sign, approximately eight feet high and 22 sq. ft., zero to five feet from the right-of-way and
two window decals 8½ x 5½ inches, and requesting the zone change ordinance be postponed until
a final decision is made by the Board of Adjustment pursuant to his intention to submit a request for
a sign variance by the Board.  He reviewed his understanding of meeting dates pursuant to his request
for postponement, noting the Board may make a final decision on his variance request at its August
15  meeting.  Steve Oxley, stating he will be a resident in the zone change request area in the nearth
future, confirmed he had no objec tion to Mr. Pi nther seeking a sign variance instead of a zone
change.  Upon inquiry to Mr. Oxley and Kim Oxley regarding whether yellow (striped) parking space
lines would be re quired for Mr. Pinther’s prope rty, utilized as a business office, as well as other
businesses in the vicinity  along Yellowstone Road, Mr. a nd Ms. Ox ley responded they  had no
knowledge of requirements regarding striped parking lines.  Mr. Allen raised a point of order stating
he did not think it was proper to call upon members of the audience and cross examine them; that
they should be able to make comments on their own if desired.  Mayor Kaysen stated the form of
questioning was a little off- target and r ecognized Matt Ashby , Planning Ser vices Director, who
provided information on the site plan process, which would include defining the location and number
of parking spaces required for the area if the zone change is granted.  He further explained parking
requirements generally for home based businesses.  Dr. Rinne raised a point of order that discussion
should stay on track for the proposed postponement.  Ma yor Kaysen advised comments should
remain germane to the topic before them.  Rosalind Schliske, resident in the vicinity, commented on
Mr. Pinther’s request for postponement vs. a letter Mr. Pinther had circulated to the neighborhood
indicating he would be withdrawing his request for a zone change.  Mr. Allen raised a point of order
inquiring if there was any motion on the floor with Mayor Kaysen verifying no motion had yet been
made and public discussion on the ordinance was being entertained.  The following individuals spoke
in opposition to the proposed zone change: Ms. Schliske, Cindy Paul, Birgette Paul, Mr. Oxley and
Eugene Kn ipping.  T opics d iscussed inc luded: S pot zon ing re quests fo r b usiness a ctivities in
Yellowstone Addition and past attempts for zone changes by the applicant; retaining the residential
integrity of the neighborhood; traffic volume and parking concerns; active neighborhood support to
keep the current zoning classification; concern with the types of business operations that could be
allowed if the zone change were approved, and opposition to the zone change generally.  Birgette
Paul read a letter from neighborhood residents Brian and Kosha Olsen, who were unable to attend
the meeting, expressing their support in keeping the neighborhood primarily residential in nature.
Mr. Pinther provided a handout (on file with City Clerk’s office) containing vicinity traffic counts
and stated if the zone change were approved and at some point he didn’t continue his business at the
current location, he would request the zone be changed back to an LR-1.  He clarified if his request
for a sign variance is approved by the Board of Adjustment, he will withdraw his request for a zone
change.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Allen advised there was no recommendation
on the item from the Committee.  Mr. Allen moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Ms.
Williams.  Mr. Allen moved to postpone to August 12, 2013, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Upon inquiry
from Ms. Oxley and Cindy Paul, Mayor Kaysen provided general information on City Council
meeting procedures regarding common actions that may involve amendments or postponements on
agenda items, a nd revie wed the a nticipated mee ting sc hedule, if the motion to post pone wa s
approved, relative to w hen the ordinance would be back before the governing body and Public
Services Committee as well as the B oard of Adjustment meeting schedule.  Mr. Ashby provided
information on the public notification process involved for Board of Adjustment meetings, and noted
that within the home occupation standards and regulations essentially the allowance for signage is
limited to one sign not more than three sq. ft. in area per home.  He further advised the Board would
be using  revie w cr iteria to make  their dec ision and evalua te the cir cumstances as well as a ny
neighborhood feedback.  Comments followed regarding past established custom of the governing
body concerning postponements of agenda items beyond the next regularly scheduled City Council
meeting vs. wording contained in Robert’s Rules Of Order (RROO) indicating postponement can not
extend past the next regularly scheduled meeting, and taking action on the ordinance at the current
meeting instead of postponing the item.  Mr. Allen raised a point of order questioning whether the
motion to postpone past the next regularly scheduled City Council meeting was in order with Mayor
Kaysen r equesting Dan White, City  Attorney, provide his leg al interpretation of RROO on the
subject.  Mr. White expressed his belief that Mr. Allen’s analysis of RROO was correct and that in
the past City Council has postponed items for longer than the next meeting but RROO is clear that
if a member of the City  Council raises a point of o rder with respect to postponement of an item
beyond the next regularly scheduled meeting then the custom that is currently employed by the City
Council “falls to the ground” and the Council is required to abide by RROO.  Mr. White advised the
chair’s (i.e. mayor’s) role involving this point of order is to decide whether the point is well taken
or not well taken in this particular instance.  He explained if the chair’s conclusion is that the point
is well taken that mea ns the motion to post pone to Aug ust 12   is not in proper orde r andth
theoretically should not be voted on, and if a motion to postpone is to be considered it should only
be for a two week time frame and not to August 12th.  Mayor Kaysen noted the point of order was
well taken and the motion to postpone for more than two weeks was not in order, therefore, there
would be no vote taken on the motion.  Mayor Kaysen announced the main motion to approve on
second reading was now the motion on the floor.  Upon query to Mr. Pinther whether he wished to
request a two week postponement or withdraw his current request for a zone change, Mr. Pinther
advised he would like to withdraw the zone change request and go forward with seeking a s ign
variance.  Mr . Br own ra ised his objection to allowing the re quest for the  zone chang e to be
withdrawn by the applicant at this time and requested a vote on the main motion be taken.  Mayor
Kaysen stated that as an objection had been raised, the main motion was back on the floor.  Upon
inquiry, May or Ka ysen advised tha t WY DOT has maintenance  authority  for this portion of
Yellowstone Road (state highway) and determines any allowable on-street parking.  Main motion to
approve on second reading failed.  Voting “no” -- all members of the governing body.
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, Zoning
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified Development
Code, changing the zoning classification from CB Community Business to HI Heavy Industrial for
Lot 3, Block 2, Campstool Addition, Second Filing , Cheyenne, Wyoming (located north of and
adjacent to Hu tchins Drive, west of Ca mpstool Road).  Mr . Allen moved to appr ove on sec ond
reading, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Upon inquiry regarding EPA requirements for proposed use as
an impound and truck washing facility, Steve Borin, property owner, clarified any user must comply
with all requirements but advised this may be a moot point as the proposed property user may not
proceed with development plans.  He provided information on potential other uses of the property
and advised he is still seeking the zone change.  Comment followed on potential impact beyond the
property line and fence screening contingent upon the type of property use.  Motion carried.  Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception of Ms. Broyles voting “no”.
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, Zoning

Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.

Understanding with the Board of County Commissioners of Laramie County, Wyoming to submit

other items for the agenda at Finance Committee and provisions of Title 2 of City Code had been
met.  Mr. Brown moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Chief Brian Kozak, Police Department,
advised this was reviewed under other items for the agenda at Finance Committee due to the grant
application deadline of July 9, 2013.  He explained they are requesting $31,260.00 and, if the grant
request is approved, the money would be used to purchase a “use of force” simulator for the City, and

Software Subscription Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Special Projects/Parking
Division, and T-2 Systems, Inc., Indianapolis, Indiana, for a parking enforcement software system
with support maintenance.  Bob Bradshaw, Special Projects Director, provided a summary of the
purpose of and need to acquire the software system, stating the current software program is housed
on  the Divi sion of I nformation Technology’s mainframe computer for whic h operating system
support will no longer be provided in September and that the current software (written in-house) is
approximately 25 years old.  He advised the system will also provide for 2 handheld devices with
printer capability to be used by parking enforcement with information from the devices entered into
the computer software program.  Laura Lears, T-2 Systems, advised she was available to answer any
questions regarding the softwar e.  Mr. B rown moved to a pprove in the amount of $58,295.76,
seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Bradshaw advised the new software program with the
handheld devices will be an obvious improvement over the current obsolete program; explained that
funding for the system, including cost of initial maintenance was approved through a prior budget
re-appropriation; advised of maintenance and program costs anticipated for and budgeted within the
current budget, and reviewed numerous other entities that are utilizing the T-2 software system.  He
noted expenses to implement the system are anticipated to be lower than initially estimated.  Upon
further inquiry, Mr. Bradshaw cited his concern with regard to any delay in implementing the system
by the September date when the maintenance for the mainframe will no longe r be available, and
provided information generally on the handheld devices relative to issuanc e of paper tickets and
downloading of informa tion into t he softwa re pr ogram, noting  the cha nge will not affe ct the
Municipal Court process; lack of on-line payment capability, at least initially, due to security issues;
training, and approved funding for the system.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the
governing body with the exception of Ms. Williams voting “no”. (#6022)

Re-Appointments of Da nny Glick, J eff Lamm, Rick Robinson and Gay  VanHorn to the
Laramie County Community Juvenile Services Joint Powers Board; Dick O’Gara and John Sundahl
to the Downtown Development Authority Board; Mary Bienz, Dean Meyer, Scott Thomas and Robin

Appointments of Christopher Humphrey to the Laramie County Community Juvenile Services

Mr. Allen moved to approve, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Mo tion carried.  Vo ting “yes” -- all

Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held July 8, 2013 at 6:00 p.m. in City
Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for a tra nsfer of owne rship and
transfer of location for a re tail liquor license applica tion filed with the City  Clerk’s Of fice for
Safeway Stores 46, Inc. d/b/a Safeway Store #2975, 305 Cole Shopping Center, Cheyenne, Wyoming
(License currently held by Lambousis Revocable Trust, 112 West 17  Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming).th
Under other business, Ms. Broyles advised at the June 10, 2013 City Council meeting the
motion to amend the City of Cheyenne’s sanitation fee schedule ordinance failed on 3  and finalrd
reading and, having voted on the pre vailing side and pursuant to Chapter 2.04 of City Code, she
moved to rec onsider the vote ta ken on the or dinance.  Mr. B rown seconded the motion.  May or
Kaysen requested Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, read the entitlement of the ordinance for the record,
which was read as follows:  An Ordina nce -- 3  Reading -- Ame nding the City of Chey enne’srd
Sanitation Fee  Schedule.  Ms. B royles explained that after r eviewing additional information
involving the ordinance and notes attached to the fee schedule that provide for clarification regarding
policy and procedures, she was requesting support for the motion to rec onsider.  Vicki Nemecek,
Assistant Public Works Director, provided an explanation of amendments that were approved prior
to the final vote at the June 10  meeting.  Motion to reconsider carried.  Voting “yes” – all membersth
of the governing body.  Mayor Kaysen advised the motion now on the floor was the main motion to
approve the ordinance on third and final reading as twice amended at the June 10  meeting.  Ms.th
Broyles moved to amend by substitute dated June 17, 2013, referencing an attached memo from Jim
Elias, Director of Public Works, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Ms. Nemecek reviewed proposed changes
contained in the substitute fee schedule and notes and also the two amendments that were approved
at the last meeting included in the substitute.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Nemecek provided information on
billing procedures involving multiple businesses within one building and relationship to removing
the last sentence in Note 2 pursuant to legal staff advice; cardboard dumpster fee change, and existing
Note 3, which is not proposed for change.  Motion to amend by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” --
all members of the governing body.  Comment followed on Note 3 which reads: “If material is left
outside of containe rs (dumpsters)  requiring  manual pickup, the ow ner of  the pro perty may be
assessed an additional cleanup fee for manpower and equipment time at a rate of $25.00 per each 5
minute period or portion thereof.”  Upon inquiry, Jim Elias, Public Works Director, advised typically
the cleanup time is less than 5 minutes.  He added careful consideration is given in assessing a fee
over the 5 minute time frame and if it is a one-time situation a fee isn’t charged, but if it’s a repeat
situation then the fee is charged.  Ms. Broyles moved to strike the words “or portion thereof” from
Note 3.  Motion died due to lac k of a second.  Upon inquiry regarding Notes 12 and 3, Mr. Elias
advised the container placement fee is $5 (Note 12), and explained unknown circumstances affecting
manpower time and ver ified documentation is recorded by the driver  (Note 3) .  Main motion as
amended carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception of Ms.
Williams voting “no”. (#3982)
There being  no further  business to come bef ore the  g overning body , the meeting  was
officially adjourned at 8:08 p.m.
 Submitted by,
  
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.

 July 9, 2013
