MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2013-07-08 | CLIP_ID: 383 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 383

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 July 8, 2013
The Go verning B ody of the C ity of  Che yenne me t in  re gular s ession on  thi s d ate
beginning at 6:00 p.m. in C ouncil Chambers.  Pre sent were: MAYOR - Richa rd L. Kaysen;
COUNCIL MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne,
Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and Ms. Williams.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff,
City Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There  will be no
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support
by two other membe rs is rec eived.  Any  item removed f rom the Consent Ag enda w ill be
considered in its normal sequence  on the agenda.)  Mr. B rown  moved to a pprove Consent
Agenda items, sec onded by Mr. Allen.  Mo tion carried.  Voting  “yes” -- all member s of the
governing body. 
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on June 24, 2013.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
 
Mr. Brown and Mr. Valdez each declared a conflict of interest (business interest in
vouchers payable to Ameri-Tech HVAC Services and KRAE-AM respectively) and vacated
Council Chambers.  Mr. Allen moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded
by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present in
Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown and Mr. Valdez returned
to Council Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive comments and input for a transfer of
ownership and transfer of location for a retail l iquor license application filed with the City
Clerk’s Office for Safeway Stores 46, Inc. d/b/a Safeway Store #2975, 305 C ole S hopping
Center, Cheyenne, Wyoming (License currently held by Lambousis Revocable Trust, 112 West
17  Str eet, C heyenne, Wy oming).  John  Sun dahl, attorney re presenting Sa feway, s poke inth
support of the tra nsfer; discussed Safew ay’s major remodeling plans for the Cole Shopping
Center pr operty; Safewa y’s commitment and contribut ion to the community ; benef its of
promoting economic development within the area, and stated that Safeway is seeking approval
for the license to provide alcohol package store products as a customer convenience.  He advised
the area for the retail package store would be approximately 2,000 sq. ft. with a separate outside
entrance (no inside entrance to the Safeway grocery store portion), and there would be no drive-
up window or bar environment.  He referenced and discussed previous information that had been
provided to me mbers of the governing body outlining affiliated benefits if the license was
approved, and distributed additional copies of the information (on file with City Clerk’s office).
Mr. Sundahl further commented on the “ parked” status of the c urrent license, noting it is not
providing any sales tax revenue benefits due to the status, and reasons why the applicant believes
approval of the transfer would not affect other liquor licensees in the area.  Jeff Fergot, design
manager and architect with Safeway Stores, stated an approved planned unit development (PUD)
zoning amendment to allow this type of property use was achieved in 2012 and that Safeway has
purchased the property.  He provided information on the re-development site plan and anticipated
new retail buildings and operations, as well as rehabilitation of some existing buildings, for the
property; cooperative work with the City on the new r ound-about adjacent to a po rtion of the
property; access/egress points within the lot, specifically noting a better designed delivery truck
route flow; dimensions and intende d uses of the pr oposed package store pr emises; Safeway
alcohol training program, including the company’s employee policy pertaining to alcohol sales,
and secur ity measure s.  During  his comments, Mr. Fe rgot displayed maps of the  proposed
development site, which includes the package store.  Mr. Sundahl added that employees working
in the package store will go through TIPS training as well as Safeway’s alcohol training program.
Tom Montoya, Chief of  Enforcement, Department of Re venue, Wyoming Liquor Divi sion,
expressed that the Liquor Division holds no position with regard to the application, and that he
was present to provide information per Mr. Sundahl’s request.  Mr. Montoya advised he was not
aware of any Wyoming town in which a package store operation affiliated with a grocery store
environment had devalued other liquor outlets in the area, and submitted hand-outs consisting
of a state-wide census report compilation of all currently approved liquor licenses and type of
license and a listing of grocery stores with liquor licenses.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Montoya advised
Wyoming law doesn’t allow bulk purchases by a licensee;  that the Liquor Division does not offer
quantity discounts, and that all licensees must buy malt beverages from a local distributor.  Jim
Weaver, realtor, advised he is affiliated with Safeway’s leasing efforts involving those areas that
will be renovated, and reviewed possible types of future businesses that may be interested in
locating on the prope rty, stating potential tenants are looking at the package store as a useful
convenience and that it will help with revitalization of the area.  John Lambousis, Lambousis
Revocable Trust (holder of the current liquor license), spoke in favor of the transfer and presented
reasons why he believes the proposed liquor store will not pose a competitive threat to other
package liquor stores in the City.  Upon inquiry, Terry Kunz, Safeway real estate representative,
advised although the package store would be a separate space, it will function and be treated as
any other store department, such as the bakery and deli departments.  Upon inquiry whether a
new Safeway store would be built if the transfer of the liquor license was not approved, Mr.
Fergot advised that would be a senior management decision and not his to make.  Ma jor Dorr
spoke in opposition of the transfe r, commenting on his disappointment re garding closure of
Safeway’s former store site on Pioneer  Ave. a nd expressing c oncerns regarding setting of  a
precedent that would allow alcohol to be sold in grocery stores and that, in the future, it may be
requested to expand the size of the liquor store.  Mr. Lambousis stated diversification of business
interests by many companies has evolved over the years and it is no longer unique to have liquor
and food sold at the same site or within the same shopping complex, as evidenced in other towns
in Wyoming.  Alberta LeGrande spoke in opposition to the transfer of ownership, citing concerns
with a package store in close vicinity of schools and the V.A. hospital and her belief there were
enough places already available to purchase alcohol.  Eric B urke commented on whether the
license mig ht b etter benefit another b usiness w hich w ould e ncourage mor e “ night l ife” in
Cheyenne.  John J ohnson, owner of  Old Chicag o restaurant, advised he  holds a reta il liquor
license in Casper and stated when big box stores in Casper acquired a liquor license it did have
an effect on other licensees.  Comments followed on past policy decisions by members of the
governing body to promote more restaurant-type environments involving retail liquor licenses;
antiquated state  laws involving li quor licensing ; sales conc erns r elated to big  box  store
environments; that the applica tion involves a transfe r request and not a lic ense the City has
available, and “parked” status of the current license.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Lambousis provided
information on his “parked” license as provided by state law and, upon inquiry, Carol Intlekofer,
City Clerk, advised if, in the future, a request is made to enlarge the dispensing room, it will
come before the governing body for approval.  There being no further comments or input and
purpose of the hea ring having been me t, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing and re ferred the
application to Finance Committee.
ORDINANCE - A mending various sec tions w ithin Chapter 5.68, B ody Art

City Code to provide for  a B ody Art Specia l Event Busine ss License and requirements for
participating body artists and guest body artists.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Brown
moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Allen, Mr. Cook and Dr.
Rinne voting “no”. (#3983)

of the Cheyenne City Code to implement a license fee for Body Art Special Events.  Mr. Brown
moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Comment followed in
reference to a letter governing body members had received (on file with City Clerk’s office) and
support for regulating and licensing this type of business activity.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes”
-- all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Allen, Mr. Cook and Dr. Rinne
voting “no”. (#3984)

(located north of and adjacent to Hutchins Drive, west of Campstool Road).  Reporting for Public

Shanor.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#3985)

Laramie County, Wyoming (located at the southwest corner of the intersection of Bluegrass

by Mr. Shanor.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#3986)
ORDINANCE - Pursua nt to Section 2.2.1, Z oning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2,
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified
Development C ode, c hanging the  zon ing classification f rom H I He avy I ndustrial to  CB
Community Business f or Lot 3, Block 1, Chey enne Business Parkway, Cheyenne, Wyoming
(located west of and adjacent to Progress Circle, north of Campstool Road.  Mr. Allen moved to
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Comment followed regarding a letter that
was re ceived by  the Deve lopment Office  from Va nDiest Supply  Co. in oppo sition to the
proposed z oning c hange, a nd r equest w as m ade t hat a  c opy b e p rovided t o C ity C ouncil.
Brandon Cammarata, Planning and Development Director, advised staff will provide a copy of
the letter and will also provide information of this type to Council members in the future.  Motion
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
ORDINANCE - A mending the Chey enne U nified De velopment Code (UD C), as
approved by Ordinance No. 3943 on January 23, 2012, to adopt amendments identified as high
priority quick fixes from the 2013 annua l r eview.  Albert Simpson discussed his personal
experience involving buildings and properties he has restored or developed over the years under
past as well as the present codes and reviewed his concerns with the current UDC.  His comments
included PlanCheyenne and UDC development process concerns; costs to developers; submittal
and content of two freedom of information (FOI) requests (re: how much money has been spent
to develop PlanCheyenne and the UDC), and Mayor’s committee formed to review elements of
the UDC after Planning Commission’s initial review of the final draft in 2010.  Upon inquiry,
Mr. Simpson provided information relative to a past proposed development involving Maverick’s
convenience store.  M ayor Kaysen provided information on composition of the Critical Path
Committee involved in reviewing the UDC prior to its initial approval in 2012, and verified the
two freedom of information requests, submitted by Mr. Simpson at the meeting, were both dated
July 4, 2013.  Frank Cole stated that changes contained in the proposed ordinance doesn’t address
widths of driveways outside of curbs where two streets intersect at an angle and situations
affiliated with narrow  lot frontag es and lots on cur ves, and e xpressed that these ne ed to be
addressed in the code.  Paul Frauendienst advised he had served as a member of the Critical Path
Committee and various committees through compilation of the UDC and spoke in favor of the
ordinance, s tating t he a nnual r eview p rocess i s w orking e xactly as it s hould.  D oug S elby,
architect, expressed concerns with visual and quality architecture and his opinion that input on
the UDC by local architects was not being listened to.  H e stated his biggest issue was with
components of Article 6 of the UDC, specifically referencing color and material requirements and
design and shape of structures; commented that the UDC needs to have more flexibility and that
it goes too far in controlling the appearance of structures and landscaping designs, and advised
that some of the provisions, such as alternate designs, result in additional costs to property
owners.  He stated that staff review and the approval process is cumbersome and he, and some
others, would like to see Article 6 removed.  Mr. Allen moved to approve on second reading,
seconded by  Mr. B rown.  Mr. A llen advised conc erns we re r aised a t the Public Services
Committee meeting regarding changes proposed in Section 10 of the ordinance and moved to
divide the question to allow for separate discussion and vote on Section 10 of the ordinance,
seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body
with the exception of Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles and Mr. Valdez voting “no”.  Discussion included
the following topics relative to Section 10 of the ordinance: Concern with regulations on those
trying to develop downtown properties; lack of certain provisions pertaining to the downtown
Central Business District (CBD) zone; new construction vs. additions to existing bui ldings;
historic nature of the downtown and providing protections or guidelines for development; Section
10 effect pertaining only to new construction with no requirement compelling the rehabilitation
or remodeling of existing buildings to meet the proposed new requirements; proposed ordinance
being the result of the first UDC annual review process and that the quick fixes do not include
other changes being considered; lack of site a nd building design standards for the CBD zone
compared to similar standards for some other zone types; relationship of Section 10 to Article
6, and revitalizing downtown involving a recent study and consultant recommendation.  Upon
inquiry during discussion, Brandon Cammarata, Planning and Development Director, provided
information regarding CBD provisions contained in past codes, current UDC code and proposed
Section 10 changes; interpretation of what the w ord “addition” means (floor area; height of a
building), a nd quick fixes proposed t hrough the annua l review  proce ss, including
recommendation by the Planning Commission, with ot her changes still being considered.  He
confirmed the CBD had min imal design standards in the previous codes prior to t he UDC.
Pursuant to a procedural point of order raised by Mr. Brown, Dan White, City Attorney, stated
the main motion was to approve the ordinance as written and the motion was seconded; after the
motion to di vide carried the motion (as aff iliated with the making  of the main mot ion) now
before the governing body was to vote on approval of Section 10.  Motion to approve Section 10
failed (due to a tie v ote).  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Dr. Rinne and
Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and Ms. Williams.
Main motion to approve the ordinance, containing Sections 1 through 9 and 11 through 14, on
second reading carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.

Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Casper, Wyoming and the City of
Cheyenne, Wyoming for Law Enforcement Extraterritorial Assistance -- r eferred to Financ e
Committee.  Mr. Brown advised that due to the governing body approval deadline of July 8, 2013
for the 2013 Cheyenne Frontier Days celebration, the item was reviewed under other items for
the agenda at the July 1  Finance Committee meeting and provisions of Title 2 of City Code hadst
been met.  Mr. Brown moved to ap prove, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Chief Brian Kozak, Police
Department, explained the MOU will allow for combined resources involving assistance from
Casper police officers during Cheyenne Frontier Days and p rovides the a bility for Cheyenne
police officers, in return, to assist the City of Casper if requested.  He advised Casper officers will
utilize their own cars and will be assisting with DUI enforcement not only at Frontier Park but
on the stree ts as well; that there  would be no cost to the Ci ty; that office rs from the City  of
Laramie will also be assisting during the event, and noted grant funding assisting with expenses
being utilized by the other municipalities.  Upon inquiry, he advised both Casper and Laramie
officers will have the same authority as Cheyenne police officers have, and that wording relative
to the MOU’s term duration follows state law.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of
the governing body.  (#6032)

Under other business:
Pete Laybourn stated that accompanying documentation for agenda items included on the
City’s website is helpful and applauded Council members for their efforts in the challenges they
face as e lected officials.  He  discussed the importanc e of a scertaining the life e xpectancy of
streets relative to repairs and reconstruction; commented on infrastructure funding, resources and
planning for the future, and expressed concerns with the quality of concrete and asphalt products.
He requested Council look at history as to why some streets and curbs and gutters are failing as
correlated to c hanges in product mate rials and planning  on how to deal with future  street
infrastructure to provide for longer life expectancy.
Appreciation was extended to a City Engineer staff member for assistance involving a
sewer/drainage issue; question was posed regarding whether Mr. Herrick’s sanitation concerns
had been addressed; comment was made regarding contractors on construction projects not
cleaning up de bris, and it was noted that the Police De partment’s 4  annual Ne ighborhoodth
Night-Out event was scheduled for Tuesday evening, July 9 .  During comments, Mayor Kaysenth
advised the sanitation matter is still under on-going discussion, and advised staff will follow-up
with the construction project debris concern.
There being no further business to come bef ore the governing body, the meeting was
officially adjourned at 8:32 p.m.
 Submitted by,
  
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 July 22, 2013
The Governing B ody o f t he C ity o f C heyenne m et i n r egular s ession o n t his da te
beginning at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present we re: MAYOR - Richard L . Kaysen;
COUNCIL MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne (arriving at 6:04
p.m.), Mr. Shanor, Mr. V aldez and Ms. W illiams.  Absent: Ms. Broyles.  Also present: Carol
Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of
allegiance was recited.
Following explanation of action take n to change the order of the a genda at the recent
Public Services Committee meeting, Mr. Allen moved to suspend the rules to change the order
of the a genda to br ing f orward ag enda item #20 ( Resolution determining c onditions for
annexation exist for Swan Ranch II) for action prior to a genda item #9 ( Ordinance regarding
annexation for Swan Ranch II), seconded by Ms. Williams.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body present.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to
be routine items by the governing body and will be ena cted by one motion.  There will be no
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support
by two other membe rs is rec eived.  Any  item remo ved from the Consent Ag enda w ill be
considered in its normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Allen, and pursuant to
support received by Ms. Williams and Mr. Cook, agenda item #24 was removed from the Consent
Agenda.  Mr. Brown moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Allen.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on July 8, 2013.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
 
Mr. Brown and Mr. Valdez each declared a conflict o f interest (business interest in
vouchers payable to Ameri-Tech HVAC Services and radio station KYOY-FM respectively) and
vacated Council Chambers. (City Clerk note: Dr. Rinne joined the mee ting in progress)  Mr.
Allen moved to appr ove payment of vouche rs as presented, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion
carried.  Vo ting “yes” -- all m embers of  the governing body present in Council Ch ambers.
Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown and Mr. Valdez returned to Council Chambers.
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Z oning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2,
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified
Development C ode, c hanging th e zoning c lassification f rom H I He avy I ndustrial to  CB
Community Business f or Lot 3, Block 1, Chey enne Business Parkway, Cheyenne, Wyoming
(located west of and adjacent to Progress Circle, north of Campstool Road).  Cortney Thompson,
representative of Green House Data Center, spoke in support of the zone change, advising they
are pursuing the change in order to expand their facility and service operations and providing
information on growth of the company.  He stated the expansion affiliated with the zone change
request involves two vacant lots adjacent to the current facility site and that the company is
purchasing the lots from  Cheyenne LEADS.  He e xplained communication efforts by Green
House Data with Van Diest Supply Company, a neighboring company, who has indicated its
objection to the zone change.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Allen moved to
approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body present. (#3987)
ORDINANCE - A mending the Chey enne U nified De velopment Code (UD C), as
approved by Ordinance No. 3943 on January 23, 2012, to adopt amendments identified as high
priority quick fixes from th e 2013 annual r eview.  B rad Harrington, Joe Hardy  and Alber t
Simpson commented in opposition to the UDC ge nerally citing c oncerns such as re gulatory
control and authority affecting private property rights; certain development aspects and design
level s tandards contained in the UDC, and that the UDC is resulting in increased costs t o
developers and property owners.  Request was made to repeal the UDC and reinstate prior codes.
M. Lee Hasenauer, Laramie County Commissioner, referenced a petition he had submitted at a
recent Public Services Committee meeting, noting it had been signed by individuals opposed to
the UDC, and submitted additional petition pages (on file with City Clerk’s office); commented
in opposition to the UDC generally, and requested the UDC be repealed.  Mr. Allen moved to
approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Upon inquiry, it was verified the
correct ordinance version before the governing body was one that was dated July 16, 2013 (City
Clerk n ote: date of City  Attorney stamp) re flecting that Sec tion 10 wording  contained in a
previous ordinance version had been removed due to an approved amendment at the July 8, 2013
Council meeting.  Comments followed in support of the quick fix ch anges contained in the
proposed ordinance and that additional changes should be forthcoming.  Motion carried.  Voting
“yes” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Mr. Cook, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting
“no” -- Mr. Luna, Mr. Valdez and Ms. Williams. (#3988)
 RESOLUTION - Determining that conditions for annexation required by Wyo. Stat. §15-
1-402 exist f or a parcel of land being situated in por tions of Sections 14, 15, 22, 23, 27,
Township 13 North, Range 67 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming, to be known
as the Swan Ranch II Annexation (located northwest of the intersection of Clear Creek Parkway
and Tundra Drive).  Mr. Allen moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Mr. Allen, seconded by
Mr. Cook, moved to amend with the following recommendation:  In order to meet the required
statutory criteria for annexation including findings of the annexation being for the protection of
health, safety, and welfare, staff recommends that the annexation be conditioned on dedication
of land for a future fire station.  That the governing body explore options to fund constructing,
equipping, and staffing the fire station, and direct staff to procure a study of the costs of new
development for the purpose of making policy decisions in the future about paying for capital
investments necessitated by development (i.e., road improvements, fire stations, etc.) and with
due consideration and credit given to the applicant for such dedication based in whole or in part
of the fair market value of said land.  Comments followed that additional information is being
worked on through discussions between the administration and the developer; that purpose of the
amendment may not be needed due to the topics involved in discussions, and that the governing
body should “ stay on track” with the  purpose of the resolution regarding statutory cr iteria.
Motion to amend failed.  Voting “no” -- all members of the governing body present.  Mr. Allen
moved to postpone to August 12, 2013, seconded by Mr. Brown.  M. Lee Hasenauer, Laramie
County Commissioner, questioned whether the “201" boundary had ever been set aside so land,
such as the remote location of this parcel, wouldn’t be subject to annexation requirements with
Mayor Ka ysen re sponding tha t he was not a ware o f any situation invol ving tha t scena rio.
Discussion included support of the postponement, construction of a proposed new fire station on
the property to be annexed and other developments and fire station locations.  During comments
involving a potential new fire station and other station sites in the community, Mr. Brown raised
a point of order that discussion should remain germane to the motion on the floor with Mayor
Kaysen ruling the point was well taken and advising there will be an opportunity to a ddress
questions on the purpose of a fire station in the area at a later time but that discussion should
pertain to the motion to postpone.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of
the governing body present.
ORDINANCE - Annexing to the City  of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a parcel of land being
situated in portions of Sections 14, 15, 22, 23, 27, Township 13 North, Range 67 West of the 6th
P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming, to be know n as the Swan Ra nch II Annexation (located
northwest of the intersection of Clear Creek Parkway and Tundra Drive).  Mr. Allen moved to
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Mr. Allen, seconded by Ms. Williams,
moved to amend with the following recommendation: In order to meet the required statutory
criteria for annexation including findings of the annexation being for the protection of health,
safety, and welfare, staff recommends that the annexation be conditioned on dedication of land
for a future fire station.  That the governing body explore options to fund constructing, equipping,
and staffing the fire station, and direct staff to procure a study of the costs of new development
for the purpose of making policy decisions in the future about paying for capital investments
necessitated by  development ( i.e., roa d improvements, fire  stations, etc.) a nd with due
consideration and credit given to the applicant for such dedication based in whole or in part of
the fair market value of said land.  Comments followed in non-support of the amendment for the
reasons presented during discussion on the previous agenda item.  Motion to a mend failed.
Voting “no” -- all members of the governing body present.  Upon inquiry, Jim Flesher, Planning
Department, expressed his opinion that he did not see any value in postponing the item at this
time as it is still on second reading.  Following further comment, main motion carried.  Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body present.

RESOLUTION - C reating the Employee Investment S tudy Implementation Team to
consider alternative means of implementing the Employee Investment Study conducted by the
Mercer Group, LLC.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Brown moved to adopt, seconded
by Mr. Valdez.  Mr. Brown move d to amend by  substitute, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  It was
advised the substitute adds City Treasurer Lois Huff to the team as she was inadvertently omitted
on the initial resolution.  Motion to amend by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of
the g overning bo dy pr esent.  Mr . B rown mov ed to  a mend by  a dding a n a dditional f ifth
“Whereas” clause to read as follows: “Whereas, a final plan must be submitted to the governing
body for approval no later than January 1, 2014, and implementation process must begin within
the FY 2015 budget,” seconded by Mr. Allen.  It was noted the amendment would provide for
a specific time line in which the team would present the governing body with alternatives and
options for funding.  Concern was expressed that since the governing body had not yet received
the final salary study from the consultant that the amendment would commit implementation of
the recommendations prior to receipt and review.  Discussion included support for a time frame
in which the tea m would submit its recommendations; that implementation may  need to be
accomplished in stages, and that the governing body will approve the final plan.  Mayor Kaysen
provided an update on the consultant’s progress with the salary pay range portion of the study,
noting the employee 2.25% COLA (cost of living adjustment) approved with the FY2014 budget
has been incorporated into the ranges; reviewed components of the overall study, which includes
revised job descriptions and staffing analysis in addition to a proposed compensation pay plan,
and staff participation in the study process.  He noted a work session with the consultant and City
Council is planned for August 7  and, upon request, advised when information is received fromth
the consultant it will be shared with department heads first and then shared with City Council
members prior to the work session.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of
the governing body present with the exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no”.  Main motion as twice
amended carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present. (#5508)

Transfer of ownership and transfer of location for a retail liquor license application filed
with the City Clerk's Office for Safeway Stores 46, Inc. d/b/a Safeway Store #2975, 305 Cole
Shopping Center, Cheyenne, Wyoming (License currently held by Lambousis Revocable Trust,
112 West 17  Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming).  Major Dorr, Alberta LeGrande, Ed Clark and Janth
Nelson Schroll spoke in opposition to the license transfer citing availability of liquor outlets
currently in the area/community; and expressing concerns involving potential expansion of the
dimensions of the proposed package store in the future; proximity of the proposed site to schools;
minors in possession of alcohol; Safeway corporate holdings not having a direct relationship with
Wyoming and profits not remaining at the local level, and setting a p recedent in awarding a
license tha t would be af filiated with a g rocery store envir onment.  J ohn Sundahl, attorney
representing the applicant, commented on Safeway’s long-standing community support and its
financial investment in re-developing the site, which will promote economic development for the
Cole Sh opping Ce nter a rea.  H e n oted Sa feway’s c ontributions to  lo cal e mployment, the
community’s tax base and org anizational and prog ram contributions an d sponsorships, and
pointed out that no objection was raised by the neighborhood during the public hearing nor the
previous Board of Adjustment (zoning appr oval) process.  He advised Saf eway operates as
competitively as other stores providing similar services and that the corporation wishes to provide
a convenient shopping experience for its customers.  He explained the proposed 2,000 sq. ft.
package store will have an outside entrance, noting if there was any request to change dispensing
room dimensions in the future it would require governing body approval, and urged support for
approval of the application.  Pat Crank, attorney representing Maury Brown, spoke in opposition
to the transfer stating approval would be a significant policy shift for the community and City
Council in awarding the license to a “big box” store.  He discussed historical importance of local
licensees and noted their commitments to and investments in the community; unfair business
pricing advantage of a big box store over “mom and pop” operations, citing provisions of  Wyo.
Stat. 40-4-107 involving unlawful competition, and potential for future size expansion or change
in entry access to the proposed package store.  He encouraged support that liquor licenses be held
by local business persons and urged that the application be denied.  Jim Weaver, #1 Commercial
Properties, CCM (Certified Commercial Investment Manager), stated he had been retained by
Safeway to assist with re-development efforts for the Cole Shopping Center by finding businesses
to lease pr operty on the site, and spoke in support of  awarding the license  to Saf eway.  He
advised that elements involving site analysis and criteria are utilized by companies in determining
store locations for various kinds of developments, whether it was for a grocery store, restaurant
or a big box business that moves into a community.  He discussed the current 36 retail-type liquor
licenses currently approved by City Council; differing operational aspects involved and average
dispensing room size of other licensees; Safeway’s convenience store intent for its customers;
Safeway’s financial philosophy affiliated with each of its store departments and the corporation’s
estimated $30,000,000 to be expended in improveme nts benefitting the City , and Safe way’s
willingness to be r estricted to the 2,000 sq. f t. dispensing r oom.  During  his co mments, Mr.
Weaver r eferred to  tw o ma ps he  dis played depicting the  loc ation o f o ther r etail l icenses,
including two county licensees, and those representing operations (i.e. package store) the same
as the ty pe being  reque sted by  Safew ay. Kevi n Georges commente d on a re cent incide nt
involving a current retail licensee that, due to violations, resulted in City Council taking action
to require closure of the business for a certain time frame, and questioned if the license transfer
was approved and at some point there was a violation of law and Council wished to penalize the
licensee in a similar manner, would the entire grocery store be shut down.  Upon request, Dan
White, City Attorney, advised he and Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, had discussed this topic and,
following review of statutes and City Code, it was their conclusion that, in the event Safeway
were to violate any liquor state statutes or provisions of City Code and the Council decided to
impose any closure action, only the dispensing room would be shut down (i.e. package store) not
the entire grocery store.  Mr. White clarified the entire building of the licensee involved in the
incident resulting in a temporary business closure, as referenced by Mr. Georges, was designated
as the dispensing room and, therefore, that was why both the package store and bar areas were
involved in the temporar y closure.  Mr. G eorges spoke in opposition to the transfer  and
commented on his belief that approving the license transfer would not help with economic
development of the shopping center site, noting currently vacant buildings and closed businesses
on the property.  He discussed concerns with losing local control, big box stores holding a liquor
license, and setting a precedence.  Eddie Georges, D.T.’s liquor licensee, spoke in opposition to
the transfer, commenting on loss leader pricing strategies; compliance responsibility involving
sales to minors and support for the principles of business competition generally, and advocated
having liquor licenses remain with “locals”.  Christine Fitzgerald, area resident within the vicinity
of the proposed license site, spoke in support of the transfer, stating the package store and new
grocery store will benefit revitalization of the area.  Following a 10 minute recess, Mayor Kaysen
reconvened the meeting at 9:00 p.m.  Mr. Brown reported there was no recommendation on the
item from Finance Committee.  Mr. Brown moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Allen.  M r.
Brown stated, pursuant to questions he had been personally asked recently, that he did not have
a business relationship with the applicant and, therefore, he did not have a conflict of interest.
Discussion topics included:   Aspects of competition; local control; state law r estricting the
availability of these ty pes of liquor lice nses; license be ing an existing license and not a ne w
license available through the City; Safeway’s proven community track record; whether Safeway
would continue to build its new store if the application was not approved; conditioning that the
2,000 sq. ft. size of the dispensing room be a future license requirement; allowing the current
licensee to have the opportunity of selling its property interest in the license to a willing buyer;
fairness concerns; retail liquor licenses being a valuable asset of the holder due to restrictions
placed on the limited number that can be issued per state statutes; best use of these types of liquor
licenses in the City; past utilization of the license when it was operated as the former Mayflower
establishment vs proposed license use as a package store; best use of the lice nse and whether
approval of the transfer will assist with economic development of the area, and support for the
existing license holder to operate under the license.  During discussion comments were made in
support as we ll as non-support of the lice nse transfer and, upon inquiry , Mr. Sundahl stated
Safeway ha s ma de a  fi nancial c ommitment t o th e pr operty as well a s a n in vestment i n
development plan ning, ad vising tha t Sa feway ha s p urchased th e sit e, a nd pla ns to s tay in
Cheyenne.  Motion to approve failed.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Brown and Mr. Shanor.  Voting “no”
-- Mr. Allen, Mr. Cook, Mr.  Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Valdez, Ms. Williams and Mayor Kaysen.
[CA] Annual Report to the C ity of Chey enne regarding capital improve ments made a t the
Airport Golf Course as required by Lease Agreement -- referred to Finance Committee.
Announcement was made of the Re-appointment of Jane M. France to the Community
Action of Laramie County, Inc. Board of Directors to be confirmed August 12, 2013.
Under other business, Mayor Kaysen advised there was another item for the agenda that
involved timeliness and reque sted the City Clerk re ad the e ntitlement of the item.  Carol
Intlekofer, City Clerk, read the entitlement as follows:  “Contract between the City of Cheyenne,
Police Department, and Wyoming Division of Criminal Investigation for task force officers to
serve as members of a joint drug enforcement team.”  Mayor Kaysen referred the item to Finance
Committee.
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was
officially adjourned at 9:18 p.m.
 Submitted by,
  
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 August 6, 2013

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2013-08-12 | CLIP_ID: 389 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 389

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 August 12, 2013
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  P resent were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen (arriving at 6:05 p.m.), Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna,
Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and Ms. Williams.   Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk;
Lois Huff, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Mr. Shanor moved to suspend the rules to change the order of the agenda to bring forward
agenda item #18 (Resolution determining conditions for annexation exist for Swan Ranch II) for
action prior to agenda item #7 (Ordinance regarding annexation for Swan Ranch II), seconded by
Mr. Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to
be routine items by  the governing body and will be e nacted by one motion.  There  will be no
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support
by two other members is re ceived.  Any  item removed f rom the Consent Ag enda w ill be
considered in its normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Shanor, and pursuant to
support received by Mr. Brown and Dr. Rinne, agenda item #20 was removed from the Consent
Agenda.  Mr . Brown moved to approve re maining Consent Ag enda items, sec onded by Mr.
Shanor.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on July 22, 2013.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
 
Mr. Valdez declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to KYOY-
FM) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. B rown moved to a pprove payment of vouchers as
presented, sec onded by Mr. Shano r.  Motion carr ied.  Voting  “yes” - - all member s of the
governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Valdez
returned to Council Chambers. (City Clerk note: Mr. Allen joined the meeting in progress).
 RESOLUTION - Determining that conditions for annexation required by Wyo. Stat. §15-
1-402 exist for a par cel of  l and being situated in portions of Sec tions 14, 15, 22, 23, 27,
Township 13 North, Range 67 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming, to be known
as the Swan Ranch II Annexation (located northwest of the intersection of Clear Creek Parkway
and Tundra Drive) (Postponed from July 22, 2013).   Reporting for Public Services Committee,
Mr. Allen moved to adopt, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Comments followed regarding non-support
of the annexation due to location of the prope rty, emergency access concerns, proposed zone
classification (light industrial/PUD), future costs to the City relative to an anticipated new fire
station within the area, maintenance costs of City areas, and a proposed amended and restated
Development Agreement introduced on the meeting agenda.  Mr. Allen moved to postpone for
two wee ks (to Aug. 26, 2013), seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Brad Emmons, AVI, agent for the
applicant, referenced the current Development Agreement, entered into approximately 2 years
ago, noting the a greement states that fut ure developments must come bef ore the  City  for
approval.  He stated it is understood thing s have c hanged since  the ini tial Deve lopment
Agreement and, therefore, a revision to the agreement is proposed.  Scott Sutherland, Granite
Peak Development, advised they have been working for the past two years to move toward the
development process, and expressed concern with delay if the postponement were approved as
it may affect discussions currently being held with compan ies interested in locating upon the
property.  Comments followed in support of postponing the item to allow for further discussion
on concerns that have been raised and for Council members to have time to review the proposed
amended and restated Development Agreement at the next Finance Committee meeting.  Upon
inquiry, Mr. Sutherland confirmed that before any sale of parcels can be in place with interested
companies, the platting process, whic h is in conjunction with the annexation action, must be
completed first, and Mr. Emmons advised additional hard copies of supporting materials included
with the ag enda item pa cket would be  provided to  Council members.  Motion to postpone
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
ORDINANCE - Annexing to the City  of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a parcel of land being
situated in portions of Sections 14, 15, 22, 23, 27, Township 13 North, Range 67 West of the 6th
P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming, to be know n as the Swan Ranc h II Annexation (located
northwest of the intersection of Clear Creek Parkway and Tundra Drive).  Mr. Allen moved to
approve on third and final r eading, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Mr. Allen moved to amend by
substitute, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Upon inquiry, Jim Flesher, Planning Department, advised
the substitute amends Section #5 by inserting a provision that the ordinance will not become
effective until the amended and restated Development Agreement between the City and property
owners is entered into (and filed).  Matt Ashby, Planning Services Director, advised in light of
the previous agenda item be ing postponed, he sugg ested this item also be def erred to allow
related agenda items be approved at the same time.  Upon inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney,
stated he believed the amendment was proper to move forward.  Comments followed in support
of the amendment.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the g overning
body.  Mr. Allen moved to postpone for two weeks (to Aug. 26, 2013), seconded by Mr. Shanor.
Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
ORDINANCE - Pur suant to Section 2.2.1, Z oning Map Amendment, 2.2.2, Planned

Established, and Sec tion 5.1.3, Official Z oning Map, of the Chey enne Unified Development
Code, changing the zon ing classification from LI, Light Industrial, and PUD, Pl anned Unit

Lot 1, Block 2, Swan Ranch Rail Park, F irst Filing, Lots 1 through 4, a nd 6, Bloc k 1, Swan
Ranch Rail Park, Fourth Filing, all of Swan Ranch Rail Park, Fifth Filing, all of Swan Ranch Rail
Park, Seventh Filing, a parcel of land situated in a portion of the north half of Section 27, T.13N.,
R.67W., 6th P.M., and portions of Sections 14, 15, 22, and 23, T.13N., R.67W., 6th P.M. to be
known as Swan Ranc h Rail P ark, Eighth Filing, Che yenne, Wyoming ( located e ast of and
adjacent to Clear Creek Parkway and north of and adjacent to Tundra Drive).  Mr. Allen moved

a clerical error contained at the end of the title by changing “... (located east of and adjacent to
Clear Creek Parkway ...)” to “... (located west of ...)”.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” --
all members of the governing body.  Comments followed regarding postponing the item as it was
affiliated with the previous items that had be en postponed, and that wording in the ordinanc e
provides that if the or dinance was a pproved it would not be e ffective u ntil the annexation
ordinance and final plat were approved and filed.  Main motion as amended c arried.  Voting
“yes” -- all member s of the governing body with the exception of Mr. B rown voting “no”.
(#3989)

ORDINANCE - P ursuant to Section 2.2.1, Z oning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2,
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from AR Agricultural Residential to LI
Light Industrial for L ots 1-3, Bloc k 1, Polk Additi on, Cheyenne, Wyoming ( located a t the
northeast corner of the interse ction of E. Pershing  Boulevard and Polk Avenue).  Mr . Allen
moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  It was noted that County
Planning staff had attended a Public Services Committee meeting expressing buffering concerns
and sugg esting a diffe rent zone cla ssification, and following  discussion with staff it was
determined the proposed zone chang e ty pe wa s appropr iate for  the deve lopment and ar ea

members of the governing body. (#3993)
ORDINANCE - Amending various sections of Title 8, Health and Safety, Chapter 8.44,
Solid Waste, of the Cheyenne City Code, pertaining to the general provisions of the Solid Waste
Code.  Providing comments on the ordinance topic were:  Jeff Tray, Aspen Real Estate, owner
of business property involving tenants; Dick O’Gara, Board President, Downtown Development
Authority; Tim Bieligk, downtown business tenant; Phil Herrick, downtown business property
owner; Julie Gliem, Cheyenne Landlords Association; Karlena Johnson, co-owner, The Kat’s
Meow, downtown business tenant; Glen Garrett, downtown business and office building owner,
and Major Dorr.  Topics discussed included: Concerns with the current rate structure relative to
increases in rates and resulting financial increase to tenants; multiple businesses housed in one
commercial building; occurrence of solid waste pick-ups, rates for those operating businesses out
of their homes vs. commercial districts; rate inequities; support for the proposed ordinance;
inappropriate use of assigned dumpsters by unauthorized persons; support for recycling efforts;
current fee structure and financial hardship on small businesses; generation of minimal waste
relative to container size and fees and concerns affiliated with unauthorized dumping.  During
comments and in response to inquiries, Mayor Kaysen advised, like most businesses, the City’s
cost of providing services increases with proposed rate increases reviewed by the governing body,
and Jim Elias, Director of Public Works, provided information on current solid waste provisions,
including service fees, container sizes, pick-up schedule and code enforcement involvement for
unauthorized container users.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Brown moved to approve
on second reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Elias pro vided information on
procedures available (i.e. contact Sanitation office) with regard to vacant properties or owners
who wish to request temporary suspension of services. Discussion followed and included support
for a fee study involving the entire sanitation ra te system; loss of revenue  to the City  if the
proposed ordinance was approved and concerns/options on how to make up the budget revenue
deficit amount; comments in support as well as non-support of the  rate change; number of
commercial accounts that may be affected by the change; “pay as you throw” programs; January
1, 2014 ordinance effective date, if approved; inequities in rate structure; fair and justifiable laws
and policies; feasibility of implementing the rate change on a trial (one year) basis; effect of the
rate chang e; delaying a ny rate  chang es until an overa ll system fee study is conducted a nd
anticipated time frame in which to conduct a  study, and legal requirement to have a balanced
budget.  During discussion, it was clarified if the ordinance were approved the estimated amount
of lost reve nue would be a pproximately $3 25,000 annually , and Mr. Elias a dvised of the
estimated number of commercial containers currently being utilized.  Mayor Kaysen stated a
proposed resolution, also on the meeting agenda, would provide for looking at the entire structure
of sanitation services, including fees and efficiencies, and recommendations.  Motion carried.
Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and Ms. Williams.
Voting “no” -- Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Dr. Rinne and Mayor Kaysen.

[CA] RESOLUTION - Authorizing the Submittal of an Application to the Wy oming Water
Development Commission for grant funding for engineering services and other administrative
costs for Phase III of the Southern Water Transmission Main Project in the amount of $1,206,000
and authorizing the Mayor, City Clerk and Director of the Board of Public Utilities to execute
the application and all related documents.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5511)
RESOLUTION - A uthorizing the  City  sta ff to conduct a  stu dy of  the  so lid wa ste
collection and disposal system, conduct a rate study and review the feasibility of converting the
solid waste system to an enterprise fund.  Commenting in favor of the resolution were:  Dick
O’Gara, Board President, Downtown Development Authority; Jeff Tray and Tim Bieligk.  Topics
presented included: I ncentives to re duce waste and “pay as y ou throw” programs to reduce
landfill space, support for a rate study, and current rate inequities which may exist.  Mr. Bieligk
commented that a study would prevent the executive branch of City government from making
private deals regarding sanitation fees and he stated his belief that such deals had been made.  Mr.
Brown reported there was no recommendation on the item from Finance Committee.  Mr. Brown
moved to adopt, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Up on inquiry requesting an explanation of his
previous comment regarding private deals, Mr. Bieligk stated one of the points he was hinting
at was that a study would assist in preventing any private deals being negotiated between private
property owners and [Ci ty] ex ecutives, and that fee  structures and ordinances be f ollowed.
Discussion followed pursuant to further inqu iry to Mr. Bieligk on his implied alleg ation of a
private de al be ing ne gotiated.  Ma yor Ka ysen r elinquished d uties o f the chair to  Cou ncil
President Rinne to enable him to participate in discussion.  Following comments which involved
further inquiries, Mr. B ieligk admitted that he was “ alleging hearsay”.  President Rinne then
requested Mr. Bieligk to submit any written documentation involving his implied allegation to
the City Attorney by the close of business on Friday (August 30, 2013).  Discussion followed in
support of the p roposed study and the components and purpose of the study, information to
Council members during the study process, and estimated “ballpark” cost for the study.  Upon
inquiry, J im Elias , Public Works Director, a dvised the cost of the  study is estimated to be
approximately $30,000-$50,000 with funding from the solid waste fund.  Motion carried.  Voting
“yes” - all mem bers of the g overning body with the exception of Mr. Va ldez voting “no” .
Following announcement of the vote, President Rinne returned duties of the Chair back to Mayor
Kaysen. (#5512)
Following a 10 minute recess, Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at 8:20 pm.  
[CA] RESOLUTION - Approving Outside User Water/Sewer Services, by User’s Agreement,
with Longhaul Enterprises, LLC, Lot 3, Block 1, Swan Ranch Rail Park, 4  Filing, Laramieth
County, Wyoming.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5513)
RESOLUTION - Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Swan
Ranch Rail Park, Eighth Filing, a Replat of Swan Ranch Rail Park, Sixth Filing, and portions of
Sections 14 , 15, 22, 23 and 27, T.13N., R.67W., 6th P.M ., Chey enne, Wy oming ( located
northwest of the intersection of Tundra and Clear Creek Parkway).  Mr. Allen moved to adopt,
seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Mr. Allen moved to amend to include sta ff recommendations #1
through 3, seconde d by Mr. B rown.  Staff  reco mmendations were recited for the r ecord as
follows:  1) Enter into a revised development agreement addressing future road improvements
that may be necessary depending on the amount of traffic generated within the development; 2)
Dedicate to the City Lot 18, Block 2, for a future fire station location or enter into an agreement
with the City regarding dedication of land for a fire station; 3) The Governing Body endorse a
study of the costs of new development for making policy decisions in the future that are definitive
about who pays for capital investments necessitated by development (i.e., road improvements,
fire station, etc.).  Buck Holmes, Laramie County Commissioner, questioned the effect on the
developer if the land was not annexed and it was advised the plat would not become effective if
the a nnexation is n ot a pproved.  B rad Emm ons, AVI, agent f or the  a pplicant, a dvised if
annexation was not approved, they would like to proceed with the project through the County
platting process.  Scott Sutherland, Granite Peak Development, requested the plat be approved
to enable them to move forward with communication efforts with parties interested in developing
on the site.  Comment was made r elative to staff  rec ommendation # 3 and whether it wa s
applicable to the resolution.  Dr. Rinne moved to divide the question to allow for a separate vote
on staff recommendation #3, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Mo tion carried.  Vo ting “yes” – a ll
members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Allen, Mr.  Luna and Ms. Williams
voting “no”.  Discussion on staff recommendation #3 followed.  Matt Ashby, Planning Services
Director, clarified that staff had identified this recommendation as being an important study to
conduct, which was confirmed by the Planning Commission to i nclude as a recommendation.
During further discussion and, upon inquiry, Mr. Ashby advised he did not foresee this type of
recommendation being proposed on a regular basis but that it was associated enough with the
conversations on this proposed plat to request the study; provided information on the estimated
time frame in which to conduct the study ; anticipated study costs relative to components that
would be studied and identification of a funding source for the study by City Council as funding
is not within Planning’s current budget; pending development actions that could be impacted if
plat approval was delayed, and beneficial information on how to address growth related issues
that can be obtained as the result of a study.  Upon inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney, advised
his belief that the recommendation should probably be acted upon as a separate resolution and
not included with the final plat action as it is n ot usually associated with approval of a plat.
Discussion continued and included verification that the re-zoning ordinance for the property had
been approved earlier in the meeting .  Mr. Ashby  suggested that c ost of t he study could be
recouped through future fees, and discussed a possible time frame for a separate resolution to be
introduced and approved.  Motion to approve the inclusion of staf f recommendation #3 in the
previous motion to amend failed.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Brown. Voting “no” -- Mr. Allen, Ms.
Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. L una, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez, Ms. Williams and Mayor
Kaysen.  Discussion on staff recommendations #1 and 2 included a request by Mr. Emmons that
the resolution be postponed for 2 weeks due the development potentially changing to a county
plat vs. city plat process if the annexation ordinance was not approved.  Dr. Rinne raised a point
of order as to the motion on the floor with Mayor Kaysen clarifying that discussion was currently
being entertained on the motion to amend which now includes staff recommendations #1 and 2.
Motion to  a mend to i nclude sta ff reco mmendations #1  a nd 2 c arried.  Vo ting “ yes” --  a ll
members of the g overning body with the exception of Mr. B rown voting “no”.  Mr. Brown
moved to  po stpone for 2 weeks ( to A ug. 2 6, 20 13), se conded b y Ms.  Broyles.  Mo tion to
postpone carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
