MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2013-08-26 | CLIP_ID: 391 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 391

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 August 26, 2013
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  P resent were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor,
Mr. V aldez an d M s. Willi ams.  Al so pr esent: Ca rol I ntlekofer, City  Cle rk; L ois Hu ff, Ci ty
Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
Dr. Rinne moved to suspend the rules to change the order of the agenda to bring forward
agenda item #16 (Resolution determining conditions for annexation exist for Swan Ranch II) for
action prior to agenda item #8 (Ordinance regarding annexation for Swan Ranch II), seconded by
Mr. Valdez.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
Mr. Allen moved to suspend the rules to change the order of the agenda to bring forward
agenda item #22 (Resolution authorizing an amended and restated Development Agreement for
the “Swan R anch Development Area ”) f or ac tion prior to ag enda item #16 ( Resolution
determining conditions for annexation exist for Swan Ranch II), seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the g overning body with the exception of Mr. B rown
voting “no”.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to
be routine items by  the governing body and will be ena cted by one motion.  There  will be no
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support
by two othe r members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be
considered in its normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Brown, and pursuant to
support received by Dr. Rinne and Mr. Allen, agenda items #22 and #30(g) were removed from
the Consent Agenda.  Upon request by Dr. Rinne, and pursuant to support received by Ms. Broyles
and Mr. Shanor, agenda item #18 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon request by Mr.
Allen, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Shanor and Mr. Cook, agenda item #29(b) was
removed from the Consent Ag enda.  Mr. Allen moved to appr ove remaining Consent Agenda
items, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing
body.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on August 12, 2013.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
 
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Ameri-
Tech HVAC) and vacated Council C hambers.  Mr. Valde z moved to approve pa yment of
vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of
the governing body present in Council C hambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr.
Brown returned to Council Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to r eceive public comments associated with
determining whether compliance of annexation conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exist
for a parcel of land be ing situated in a portion of the South Half of  Section 20, Township 14
North, Range 66 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming, to be known as the Terre
Pont Annexation (located at the northeast corner of the intersection of Frontier Mall Drive and
Prairie Ave nue).  Jim Fleshe r, Planning  Service s Depa rtment, revie wed r equirements and
compliance of conditions contained in W yo. Stat. §15-1-402, including  specific information
indicating compliance with Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-402(a)(i) through (vi).  Upon inquiry  regarding
possible extension of Rue Terre, Mr. Flesher clarified no City cost estimate has yet been done
regarding an extension of the stree t.  During comments on extension of Rue Terr e for future
consideration, Rob  Ge ringer, City  En gineer, a dvised e ngineering sta ff c an w ork o n a  c ost
estimate.  Mark Butler, Inberg-Miller Engineering, firm affiliated with proposed development
of the prope rty, revie wed c ost estimates involving Rue Terre  aff iliated with the proposed
development on the property.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the
hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public meeting for the purpose of receiving public comments on
the submission of a 5307 Federal Grant to fund transportation services by Cheyenne Transit for
Fiscal Years 2013/2014.  J oe Dougherty, Transit Director, provided information on the g rant
request, which is in the amount of $1,110,189, and reviewed information contained in his memo
dated August 26  to City Council.  He stated they are requesting more in capital assistance andth
maintenance in orde r to purcha se 3 re placement buses, and r eviewed the nee d for f ederal
operating assistance, and money utilized from other funds and grants for program services.  Upon
inquiry, Mr. Dougherty provided information on the weekday and Saturday hours for fixed route
bus services, noting it was hard to find the perfect mix of weekend hours based upon available
funding.  Shawn Ajdari questioned whether alternate fuel types have been considered for vehicle
operational cost savings and related his observation of a transit program in operation in another
city and state that uses natural gas.  Mr. Dougherty advised compressed natural gas works better
at sea level vs. hig h altitudes, explaining some problems involve limited vehicle fue l storage
capacity and a longer time in which to fill tanks, and stated he would check on the program Mr.
Ajdari mentioned.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the meeting  having
been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the public meeting.
 
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive public comments regarding the intent
of the City of Cheyenne to submit an application for funding to the Wyoming Business Council’s
Business Ready Community, Downtown Development Grant Program to construct an elevated
walkway over Pioneer Avenue to the Jack R. Spiker downtown parking garage to provide access
to parking for a national hotel franchise in Cheyenne’s downtown.  Cindy Schneider, Grants
Manager, advised the grant and loan request is in conjunction with a proposed Hilton hotel to be
built near Pioneer Ave. and 17  St. (across the street west of the parking structure) and that theth
hotel project will facilitate downtown economic development.  S he stated the grant request
amount would be $562,500 with a $187,500 loan and there would be no out of pocket cost to the
City.  She explained the grant and loan will fund construction of the walkway that will be owned
by the City and leased to the hotel. Anja Bendel, Director of Business Development, Cheyenne
LEADS, reviewed public, sales tax and economic development benefits involved in the proposed
hotel project, noting hotel plans call for approximately 122 rooms and that the hotel would be
responsible for walkway operational and maintenance expenses during the term of the walkway
lease.  She advised the walkway is envisioned to extend between the parking garage and hotel
lobby/meeting rooms area and can be used by others.  James Perot McNeil commented on his
family history involving ownership and leasing of property.  Lea Bain, Larry Atwell and Shawn
Ajdari spoke in support of the application for funding, noting benefits of economic development
at no cost to taxpayers; investment in downtown; lack of downtown parking; potential for future
extension of access to the garage to benefit other downtown areas, and suggestion that a master
plan be conducted.  Upon inquiry, Brian Weinmaster, affiliated with the proposed hotel project,
advised of H ilton st andards relative to the numbe r of parking  space s that are  require d
(preliminary estimate is 120 spaces).  Dick O’Gara, Board President, Downtown Development
Authority, advised informal support for the project has been expressed through DDA Committees
and that he a nticipated formal support would be provide d at the next Boar d meeting.  Upon
inquiry regarding any potential future costs involving improvements to the garage, Mayor Kaysen
advised nothing in detail has yet been discussed in that regard.  A lso in response to inquiry,
Mayor Kaysen provided inf ormation on a pa st proposal for a  skywalk connection from the
parking structure to another building that involved a different project.  Randy Bruins, Cheyenne
LEADS, commented that the past skywalk proposal involved different grant program rules and
a different type purpose, and stated the proposed City application for grant and loan funding
through the Wyoming Business Council’s program is the first step in the process, and that it must
still be vetted by the Business Council and State Loan and Investment Boards and involves more
steps throughout the application review process.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Bruins advised the loan is
part of the grant process and that the availability of parking spaces through the elevated walkway
connectivity is a  c ritical c omponent t o th e ho tel be ing a ble to b e built unde r f ranchise
requirements.  Rob Uehran, Spirit Hospitality, verified it is a Hilton franchise requirement to
have the connectivity for parking and explained elements involved in a franchise relationship.
Upon inquiry whether there was any constitutionality issue with the City investing in this type
of funding and loan request, Dan White, City Attorney, advised research had been conducted and
the City would be acting within its statutory authority.  There being no further comments or input
and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the public hearing.
 RESOLUTION - Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk of the City of Cheyenne to enter
into an amended and restated Development Agreement with Granite Peak Development, LP,
Clear Creek Land Company, LLC, and Swan Ranch, LLC to define the rights and obligations of
the parties relating to the annexation and future economic development of the land generally
described as the “Swan Ranch Development Area”.  Mr. Brown moved to adopt, seconded by
Mr. Valdez.  Upo n inquiry, Matt Ashby , Planning Ser vices Director, advised the ag reement
provides for an option by the City to purchase land for a fire station but if the pump station is
paid for from grant funds, then the developer would be willing to donate the land.  He stated he
was not aware of any funding yet set aside to build and staff the station.  Comment was made
regarding any interest in the “downtown hole”  by the deve loper of this prope rty, and it was
advised the current developer was perhaps looking for more acreage with industrial-type uses.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception of Ms.
Williams voting “no”. (#5520)
 RESOLUTION - Determining that conditions for annexation required by Wyo. Stat. §15-
1-402 exist for a par cel of  land being  situated in portions of Sections 14, 15, 22, 23, 27,
Township 13 North, Range 67 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming, to be known
as the Swan Ranch II Annexation (located northwest of the intersection of Clear Creek Parkway
and Tundra Drive) (Postponed from 08/12/13).  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Dr.
Rinne moved to adopt, seconde d by  Mr. Allen.  Questions followed re garding emer gency
response fire se rvices for the c hemical plant loca ted in the ar ea; Roll ing Hills residential
subdivision, Walmart Distribution Center and NCAR.  In response, Chief James Martin, Fire and
Rescue Department, stated the areas are all served by Laramie County Fire Districts and that the
City responds only if they are called upon for mutual aid assistance.  Upon inquiry  regarding
anticipated c osts associated with build ing a new fire station, Chief Mar tin advised the
approximate cost may be  4-5 million dollars.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of
the governing body with the exception of Mr. Brown and Ms. Williams voting “no”. (#5515)
ORDINANCE - Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a parcel of land be ing
situated in portions of Sections 14, 15, 22, 23, 27, Township 13 North, Range 67 West of the 6th
P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming, to be known a s the Swan Ranch I I Annexation (located
northwest of the  intersec tion of Clear Cre ek Parkw ay and Tundra  Drive)  (Postponed from
08/12/13).  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Shanor.
Discussion topics included: Annexation providing City growth and benefits; vision for growth,
and concern with reduced property and sales tax revenue base affiliated with industrial uses vs
retail or residential uses.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
(#3994)
ORDINANCE - Amending various sections of Title 8, Health and Safety, Chapter 8.44,
Solid Waste, of the Cheyenne City Code, pertaining to the general provisions of the Solid Waste
Code.  Pr oviding comments on the ordinance topic were: Phil Herrick, downtown business
property owner; Joan Ghani, General Manager, Frontier Mall and Frontier Square; Dick O’Gara,
Board President, Dow ntown Deve lopment Authority; L ea B ain, business consultant; Sam
Galeotos, do wntown p roperty ow ner; a nd Jef f T ray, A spen Re al E state, o wner o f b usiness
property involving tenants.  Topics discussed included:  Current rate structure and financial effect
on small business property owners and tenants; negative effect of proposed ordinance on owners
of larger properties involving multiple business tenants housed in one commercial building;
responsibility of property owners for retail tenants’ solid waste charges; rate inequities; proposed
ordinance benefitting small businesses; current rate structure being unfavorable to those trying
to bring businesses to the downtown area; rates for those operating businesses out of their homes
vs. commercial districts; waste amount and containers vs. dumpsters; uniform enforcement, and
support for r ecycling, composting a nd “pay as you throw” programs.  Reporting  for F inance
Committee, Mr. Brown advised there was no recommendation from the Committee on the item.
Mr. Valdez moved to approve on third and f inal reading, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Discussion
topics included: Rate inequities; effect of proposed ordinance on mall and strip-mall properties;
pending independent study of sanitation rate structure, and definition of “building” and wording
in current City code.  Upon inquiry regarding clarification of current code regarding buildings
in which one or more businesses operate, Dan White, City Attorney, read current language of
Section 8.44.010 (D) of City code as follows: “It is assumed that each residence and business in
the city produces solid waste.  Therefore, each residence and business must subscribe to and pay
for city solid waste services.  An owner(s) may terminate solid waste services if the residence or
business is vacant, unused, or there have been changes made which affect the use or occupancy
of the property, by filing a written request with the solid waste division to terminate, suspend, or
change services. An affidavit form is available at the transfer station. This form when signed and
notarized, will meet this requireme nt. Adjustments will be made upon receipt of a completed
form by the solid waste division. Owner(s) must also notify the solid waste division immediately
of any changes to the property after the affidavit is signed. If notification is not made, the solid
waste division is authorized, at its discretion, to charge for the entire period back to the change
date specified in the original affidavit. Customers bear the burden to ensure correct billing for
services provided. Warranted adjustments for past billing errors will be limited to no more than
sixty (60) days from customer’s notification of the error to the solid waste division.”  Comments
followed on interpretation of proposed ordinance language relative to charges for services for
buildings with numerous businesses; concern with ordinance effect pertaining to lost revenue
(estimated $166,000 during c urrent fiscal year; $325,000 for following  year) and options for
consideration in recouping lost revenue (ex: rate increases, reduced services); rate structure being
a long standing issue, and opportunity to further review/revise ordinance provisions based upon
results of the rate study.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend Section 2 of the proposed ordinance by
striking the words “against the owner(s)” in the 3  sentence contained in Section 8.44.010 (D),rd
seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Speaking in favor of the amendment were:  Ms. Ghani, Mr. Herrick,
Mr. Galeotos and Ms. Ba in.  J im Elias, Publ ic Works Director, expressed concern that the
proposed amendment would make the ordinance almost unenforceable for fee collection purposes
as it would not be clear who would ultimately be responsible for the bill.  Mr. Tray suggested the
dumpster could be removed for non-payment.  Discussion included comments in support as well
as non-support of  the proposed am endment and intent of the ame ndment with reg ard to fe e
assessment vs. payment of bill responsibility.  Upon inquiry concerning sending a bill “to the
building” per other proposed wording in Section D, Mr. White advised Section E of current City
code perhaps addresses the matter and read the following wording from existing Section E: “...
Charges for the collection of garbage and refuse must be paid by the owner or occupant of every
premise(s) from which garbage and refuse is collected.”  Mr. White further advised that sanitation
fees have to be charged against someone -- a person or legal entity who can make payment, not
a building.  Further comments followed on discretionary authority provided public works staff
in determining who to bill, and that the ordina nce proposes no cha nge to Section E.  Upon
request, Mr.  White read exis ting Sec tion E in it s entirety  as follows:  “...Char ges for  the
collection of garbage and refuse must be paid by the owner or occupant of every premise(s) from
which garbage and refuse is collected.  These charges will be included in water and sewer bills
issued by the Board of Public Utilities.”  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” - Mr. Allen, Mr.
Cook, Mr. Luna, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and Ms. Williams.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Brown Ms.
Broyles, Dr. Rinne and Mayor Kaysen.  Discussion followed and included the following topics:
Ordinance change effect “for a few”;  forthcoming overall sanitation rate study results; unknown
and potential City budget costs involving fire arbitration and employee salary study relative to
loss of sanitation revenue; timing of the ordinance as a current issue to change fee assessments
to be based upon a per dumpster and pickup basis and addressing other topics later, and invoicing
for services.  Upon inquiry , Mr. White advised it is som ewhat difficult to predict all of the
resulting implications that mi ght occur with respec t to the ordinance , and re iterated wording
contained in Section E regarding charges for collection, commenting that bills will still be sent
to owners or occupants of premises in the City but this is a complicated issue.  Co mments
followed on the effective da te of the or dinance (i.e. January  1, 2014); possible ordinance
change(s) by the effective date if receipt of the pending solid waste study is completed by the end
of the year, and waiting for study results before acting on the proposed ordinance.  Upon inquiry
by Mayor Kaysen to members of the governing body and there being no objection voiced, Mayor
Kaysen raised a question regarding wording of Section E (current city code) relative to the phrase
“owner or occupant” and the scenario of whether the City could collect for sanitation charges
from a pr operty owner if a lesse e of the  property owner, who has re sponsibility for pa yment
through agreement with the owner, failed to make payment, and requested Mr. White provide a
legal interpretation.  Mr. White responded that it appears under the ordinance the City could
collect from the owner if the occupant, for whatever reason, refused to pay or did not pay.
Motion to approve as amended carried.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Mr.
Shanor, Mr. Valdez and Ms. Williams.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Dr. Rinne and
Mayor Kaysen. (#3995)
Following a ten minute recess, Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at 8:15 pm. 
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning  Map Amendme nt, Section 5.1.2,
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified
Development C ode, c hanging the  zon ing c lassification f rom MU B Mixe d-Use B usiness
Emphasis to P Public for a portion of Lot 1, Block 2, and ½ of vacated Vosler Place, Plaza Del
Range, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the northwest cor ner of the intersec tion of Sheridan
Street and Ridg e Road).  D r. Rinne moved to appr ove on sec ond reading, seconded by Mr.
Shanor.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. 
ORDINANCE - Pursua nt to Section 2.2.1, Zoning  Map Amendm ent, Section 5.1.2,
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified
Development Code, chang ing t he z oning c lassification fr om A-1 Ag ricultural a nd Rural
Residential (County) to MUR Mixed-Use Residential  Emphasis for Snyder Plaza, Cheyenne,
Wyoming (located east of and adjacent to Snyder Avenue, north of W. Allison Road).  Dr. Rinne
moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Comments followed reg arding
potential retail business activities at the site; neig hborhood notification re quirements of the
proposed zone change; traffic and safety concerns; zone change requirements, and whether a site
plan, in conjunction with a re lated pre liminary plat item on  the meeting ag enda, ha d been
submitted.  In response to inquiry during comments, Brett Walker, Development Office, advised
a site plan had not yet been submitted and isn’t normally received as part of a zone change
procedure, and Matt Ashby, Planning Services Director, clarified land annexed into the City
sometimes retains its county zone type until the property is developed.  Shawn Ajdari, owner and
developer of the property, verified the requested zone change is t o MUR which has specific
requirements on what types of development can be placed upon the property.  Brad Emmons,
AVI Engineering, agent for the applicant, reviewed development types, including applicable
architectural desi gn standards, a llowed within a MUR zone and pursuant to the Unif ied
Development Code (UDC), and stated a full architectural design will not begin until the zone
type is confirmed.  Continuing discussion included the following topics: Whether the zone
change type was appropriate for the ar ea; notification radius require ments; traffic study and
traffic volume in the vicinity ; uses by  right for a MU R z one pursuant to ad ministrative or
conditional use review; letter received from area resident, and proposed use of property.  During
discussion, Mr. Ashby provided information on the process utilized in reviewing uses by right
for a MUR zone pursuant to provisions of Article 2 of the UDC; Mr. Emmons advised the
developer is looking at providing small neighborhood-type business services, and Mr. Walker
confirmed an error with a map coordinate connected with the preliminary plat as well as a
corrected legal description will be provided throug h the final plat proc ess.  Motion carr ied.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception of Ms. Williams voting
“no”.
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2,
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified
Development C ode, c hanging the  zon ing c lassification f rom A-1 and  MU  (C ounty) t o CB
Community Business for a parcel of land situated in a portion of the South Half of Section 20,
T.14N., R.66W., 6th P.M., Cheyenne, Wyoming, to be known as Lots 1 and 2, Block 1, Terre
Pont ( located a t th e no rtheast co rner of the int ersection o f Frontier M all D rive a nd Pr airie
Avenue).  Dr. Rinne move d to approve  on second r eading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
ORDINANCE - A mending, cre ating, deleting  and re -creating various cha pters and
sections within Title 15, Buildings and Construction, of the Cheyenne City Code relating to
General Provisions, and including Adoption of the International Building Code and other Related
Codes, 2012 Edition; The National Electrical Code, 2011 Edition; and the International Energy
Conservation Code, 2009 Edition.  Joe Patterson, Joe Patterson Construction, advised he has
served on several Building Code Review Committees and commented on the 2009 International
Energy Conservation Code (I ECC), indicating that version wa s more consistent with cur rent
practices and provides more options for builder s.  Dick O’Gara, Board President, Downtown
Development Authority, stated the Board has not formally endorsed the proposed codes, citing
DDA’s interest in codes benefitting historic and existing downtown buildings, and requested the
ordinance be postponed to allow the Board time to receive additional information.  Speaking in
favor of the ordinance were Randy Byers, local architect and member of Code R eview
Committee; Brian Casey, owner, Mister.B’s Heating and Cooling, and member of Code Review
Committee, and Delmar Stone.  Their comments included support for the IECC as a means to
protect the safety and welfare of residents by setting a minimum standard and helping reduce
energy consumption; Code Review Committee membership composition and its role in reviewing
and recommending proposed codes; benefits and components of the proposed 2009 IECC edition
vs. 2012 edition; need to update the codes, and opposition to postponement.  Dr. Rinne moved
to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Dr. Rinne moved to divide the question
to allow for separate discussion and vote on the International Energy Conservation Code, 2009
Edition, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Ms. Broyles,
Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Mr. Valdez and Ms. Williams.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Brown, Dr. Rinne, Mr.
Shanor and Mayor Kaysen.  Following comments on the IECC, Mr. Shanor moved to postpone
consideration of the 2009 I ECC to S eptember 9, 2013, seconde d by Mr. L una.  Discussion
followed on the purpose of postponement; possibility of holding a City Council work session to
receive further staff information, and whether postponement was needed as the ordinance was
on 2 , not 3 ,  reading.  Motion to postpone failed.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Luna, Mr. Shanor, Mr.nd rd
Valdez and Ms. Williams.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Dr.
Rinne, and Mayor Kaysen.   M ain motion, as divided, to approve the IECC, 2009 Edition,
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Luna and
Mr. Shanor voting “no”.  Comments followed in support of scheduling a Council work session
on the ordinance topic.  Main motion, as divided, to approve the remaining codes enumerated in
the ordinance carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception of
Mr. Shanor voting “no”.

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2013-09-09 | CLIP_ID: 393 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 393

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 September 9, 2013
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  P resent were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor,
Mr. V aldez an d M s. Willi ams.  Al so pr esent: Ca rol I ntlekofer, City  Cle rk; L ois Hu ff, Ci ty
Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
Mr. Allen moved to suspend the rules to change the order of the agenda to bring forward
agenda item #16 (Resolution determining conditions for anne xation exist for Te rre Pont) for
action immediately prior to agenda item #11 (Ordinance regarding annexation for Terre Pont),
seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to
be routine items by  the governing body and will be e nacted by one motion.  There  will be no
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support
by two other members is re ceived.  Any  item removed f rom the Consent Ag enda w ill be
considered in its normal sequence on the agenda.).  Mr. Allen moved to approve, seconded by Mr.
Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on August 26, 2013.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
 
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Ameri-
Tech HV AC) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Valdez moved to approve payment of
vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of
the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr.
Brown returned to Council Chambers.
ORDINANCE - Pursua nt to Section 2.2.1, Zoning  Map Am endment, Section 5.1.2,
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified
Development C ode, c hanging the  zon ing c lassification f rom MU B Mixe d-Use B usiness
Emphasis to P Public for a portion of Lot 1, Block 2, and ½ of vacated Vosler Place, Plaza Del
Range, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the northwest cor ner of the intersec tion of Sheridan
Street and Ridge Road).  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Allen moved to approve
on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members
of the governing body. (#3996)
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5 .1.2,
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified
Development Code, chang ing the  zoning classific ation from  A-1 Agr icultural and Rura l
Residential (County) to MUR Mixed-Use Residential  Emphasis for Snyder Plaza, Cheyenne,
Wyoming (located east of and a djacent to Snyder Avenue, north of W. Alli son Road).  Brad
Emmons, A.V.I. Engineering, agent for the applicant, advised they would like to postpone the
item for two weeks to allow for additional public notification and comment, and to hold a public
open house.  Mr. Allen moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Shanor.
Mr. Allen moved to postpone for  two wee ks (to Sept. 23, 2013), second ed by Mr. Shanor.
Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5 .1.2,
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified
Development C ode, c hanging the  zon ing c lassification f rom A -1 and MU  (C ounty) t o CB
Community Business for a parcel of land situated in a portion of the South Half of Section 20,
T.14N., R.66W., 6th P.M., Cheyenne, Wyoming, to be known as Lots 1 and 2, Block 1, Terre
Pont ( located a t th e no rtheast c orner o f t he int ersection of Frontier M all D rive a nd Pr airie
Avenue).  Mr. Allen move d to approve  on third and final r eading, seconded by Mr. Shanor.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#3997)
ORDINANCE - A mending, cre ating, deleting  and re -creating various c hapters and
sections within Title 15, Buildings and Construction, of the Cheyenne City Code relating to
General Provisions, and including Adoption of the International Building Code and other Related
Codes, 2012 Edition; The National Electrical Code, 2011 Edition; and the International Energy
Conservation Code, 2009 Edition.  M r. Allen moved to approve on third and final reading,
seconded by Ms. Williams.  Mr. Allen moved to amend Section 11 to read as follows:  “This
ordinance shall be in full for ce and effect upon approval and publication, but not sooner than
November 1, 2013", se conded by Mr. Shanor.  Upon inquiry  by Paul Frauendienst regarding
purpose of the amendme nt, it was advised that the e ffective date of Nove mber 1  had bee nst
inadvertently excluded from the ordinance.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “ yes” --  a ll
members of the governing body.  D iscussion followed on Section 15.08.020, Amendments,
paragraph B. pertaining to deleting and re-creating Section 102.4.1 wording and which code, the
International Building Code (IBC) or International Fire Code (IFC), would prevail if a conflict
occurred be tween the  two codes.  B ruce Wilson, Chief B uilding Of ficial, ve rified the
International Energy Conservation Code would not be excluded in the proposed change as it is
included in the I BC, and tha t intent of the wording is to clear up a ny conflict with fire  and
building codes.  Upon inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney, explained they wanted to make sure
that the fire code would stand alone as a maintenance code; whereas the building code applies
to new construction, remodels and alterations.  He stated the change will ensure that the IBC
would only supercede if  the re was a  c onflict, a nd, if  the re was a  c onflict, h e a dvised th at,
generally speaking, then the fire  code of ficial, building code of ficial and the prope rty owner
would discuss the matter to work out any areas involved in the conflict.  Upon further inquiry,
Mr. White advised the proposed wording does represent a City policy change.  Further discussion
followed on interaction between fire and building codes.  Upon inquiry, Chief Jim Martin, Fire
and Rescue Department, advised the propose d change was drawn up by  the Fir e Prevention
Division and that, in the past, if there was a conflict between the codes staff attempted to work
out the differences.  Mr. Shanor moved to strike paragraph B. listed under Section 2., 15.08.020,
Amendments, so that the language from the IBC 2012 code (i.e. Section 102.4.1) would remain
unchanged, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Discussion followed on the current policy if conflicts arose
between the IBC and IFC; objectivity; forthcoming ordinance to update the IFC, and whether
other Wyoming communities had made a  simil ar change as pr oposed in para graph B.  Mr.
Wilson, upon inquiry, advised he wasn’t aware of other communities in Wyoming making a
similar amendment to the IBC code, and stated for clarification that during the building permit
process that would be the ruling process but after completion of the building the fire code, which
is a maintenance document,  will be in effect.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. B rown, Ms. B royles and May or
Kaysen voting “no”.  Main motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
(#3998)
RESOLUTION - Determining that conditions for annexation required by Wyo. Stat. §15-
1-402 exist for a parcel of land being situated in a portion of Section 20, Township 14 North,
Range 66 West of the 6th P .M., Laramie County, Wyoming, to be know n as the Te rre Pont
annexation (located northeast of the intersection of Frontier Mall Drive and Prairie Avenue).  Mr.
Allen moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members
of the governing body. (#5524)
ORDINANCE - Annexing to the C ity of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a parcel of land be ing
situated in a portion of Section 20, Township 14 North, Range 66 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie
County, Wyoming, to be known as the Terre Pont annexation (located northeast of the
intersection of Frontier Mall Drive and Prairie Avenue).  Mr. Allen moved to approve on second
reading, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing
body.
ORDINANCE - Pursua nt to Sections 1.1.6 and 2.4.1 of the Chey enne U nified
Development Code, amending the landscaping requirements under Unified Development Code
Sections 6.3.4a, 6 .3.4b, and 6.3.7e.  B randon Cammarata, Planning and Development office,
explained staff prepared a development report on the proposed change, which pertains to a text
amendment to re duce landscaping requirements, and provided a  brief history on landsca ping
provisions that have been in place since 1992.  Commenting on the proposed ordinance were:
Jan Nelson-Schroll; Mark Madsen; Boyd Wiggam, Wyoming Liberty Group; Barbara Brennan,
Harmony Hearth and Home; Brian Tyrrell; M. Lee Hasenauer, Laramie County Commissioner;
Nancy Loomis; Patrice Gapen; Major Dorr; Jean Vetter and Albert Simpson.  Comments were
made in s upport as well as non-support of the ordinance and included the following topics:
Community impact concerns if amount of landscaping currently required was reduced; effecting
a cha nge for  a small g roup of pe ople (i.e. small businesses) ; support for p roviding more
flexibility and better solutions; economic, social and property value benefits involving trees and
landscaping; reducing landscaping requirements to support wise water use; Unified Development
Code (UDC) requirements and financial effect on small businesses; applying for variances;
allowing business owners to ha ve more  of a de cision; Chey enne a s an urba n landsca pe
environment and implementing more conservation, irrigation and alternative measures to reduce
water run-off; concerns with treating new (big) development the same as small business, multi-
use or non-residential properties; uniform application of the UDC; difficulty of growing trees in
Cheyenne’s environment and saf ely maintaining la rger tre es; sug gestion that the term
“redevelopment” be defined; concern with reducing landscaping requirements “across the board”;
support for small businesses, and reducing the landscaping amount to reduce high water uses.
Mr. Allen moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Comment followed
on new development vs. redevelopment.  Mr. Shanor moved to amend by adding a sentence to
Section 10 which would immediately precede the current sentence that starts with “ ... That the
City Planning Office is ...”; said sentence to read “The amendments contained in Sections 6, 7,
8 and 9 herein are only applicable to UDC Section 6.3.2 (b), (c) and (d), the rest of 6.3.2 remains
unaltered.”.  Motion was seconded by Mr. Allen.  It was noted the proposed amendment would
focus only on re- development.  Ms. Ve tter questioned whether 6.3.2 (d)  would prese nt any
drainage run-off concerns for engineering with Rob Geringer, City Engineer, advising he would
need to review further.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing
body with the exception of Mr. B rown, Ms. Bro yles and Ma yor Kaysen voting “no”.  U pon
inquiry Ms. Brennan provided information regarding redevelopment of the site where they have
relocated their business and explained, even with a reduction to the amount of required trees,
sidewalk and pavement would still have to be removed.  Discussion topics included: UDC review
and amendment process, “quick fixes” and other amendments; business owner responsibilities;
Planning Commission involvement in amendments; whether the proposed ordinance will “fix”
small business owners’ concerns; landscaping requirements as compared to other similar regional
communities; landscaping (example: trees and shrubs) a s a community enhancement or non-
enhancement; redevelopment scenarios; tree point methodology and providing more flexibility,
and comments in support and non-support of the pr oposed ordinance.  Upon inquiry  during
discussion, Mr. Cammarata provided information on the UDC annua l review process, noting
there were a number of topics to manage and prioritize during this first annual review.  He stated
the priority was to get the “quick fixes” done; then the few items dealing with in-fill, and work
on proposals to take to the Pl anning Commission for review and recommendations to then
present to the governing body.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” - all members
of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles and Mayor Kaysen.
ORDINANCE - Amending the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (UDC), to allow,

Mixed-Use Business Emphasis and the NB Neighborhood Business zone districts and to allow,

district -- referred to Public Services Committee (sponsor -- Mr. Allen).

seconded by Mr. Allen.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the City Council with
the exception of Ms. Williams voting “no”.  Mayor Kaysen did not vote (Mayoral appointment).
Announcement was made of the Appointment of Tricia S. Crilly to the Cheyenne Housing
Authority Board to be confirmed September 23, 2013. 
Under other business, comment was expressed that anyone in the community wishing to
provide input on the landscaping ordinance discussed earlier in the meeting could contact City
Council members.  Succe ss of Fr eedom’s Edge Brewery event he ld over the w eekend that
monetarily benefitted local non-profit organizations was noted.
There being no further business to come b efore the governing body, the meeting was
officially adjourned at 8:24 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 
  
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2013-09-23 | CLIP_ID: 395 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 395

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 September 23, 2013
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  P resent were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez
and Ms. Williams.  Absent: Mr. Allen.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City
Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to
be routine items by the governing body and will be ena cted by one motion.  There  will be no
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support
by two  other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be
considered in its normal sequence on the agenda.)  Mr. Brown moved to approve, seconded by Mr.
Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on September 9, 2013.  Motion
(per Consent Agenda) carried.
 
Mr. Valdez moved to approve  payment of vouche rs as pre sented, sec onded by Mr.
Shanor.  Upon inquiry regarding a voucher payable to Concrete Specialists, Rick Parish, Parks
and Recreation Director, advised services by the contractor involved Greenway path repairs and
provided information on the annual Greenway maintenance assessment and repair program.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, S ection 5.1.2,
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified
Development Code, cha nging the  zoning classific ation from A- 1 Ag ricultural a nd Rural
Residential (County) to MUR Mixed-Use Residential  Emphasis for Snyder Plaza, Cheyenne,
Wyoming (located east of and adjacent to Snyder Avenue, north of W. Allison Road) (postponed
from Sept. 9, 2013).  Brandon Cammarata, Planning and Development Office, advised a petition
opposing the zone change had been signed by neighbors in the vicinity and submitted on this
date.  He stated, pursuant to Uniform Development Code (UDC) requirements, the signatures
must be verified and recommended postponement of the item to the October 14  City Councilth
meeting to allow time for verification.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Shanor
advised there was no recommendation on the item from Committee.  M r. Shanor moved to
approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Mr. Shanor moved to postpone to
October 14, 2013, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Ms. Williams noted that she had submitted the protest
petition to the Ci ty Clerk, and e xpressed appre ciation to the developer  and ag ent for
communication efforts with the neighborhood.  Upon inquiry regarding the petition process, Dan
White, City Attorney, recited wording contained in Wyo. Stat. Section 15-1-603 and stated it
would need to be determined whether the petition met statutory requirements and, if so, then it
would require a minimum of eight affirmative votes by members of the governing body for the
ordinance to pass on fina l reading rather than the usual six vot es.  Upon fur ther inquiry, Mr.
White stated petition signers must be owners of property within a designated area in order to be
counted, a nd revie wed generally what the ve rification process would e ntail.  Upon inquiry
regarding whether notification to additional prope rty owners that had re cently occurred was
invalid relative to the Planning Commission process, Mr. White advised he would like time to
review what ha d been be fore the Planning Commission as well as state statute and UDC
provisions in order to respond to the inquiry.  Mr. White further clarified there did not seem to
be a time limit in which petition signatures could be submitted but that the UDC states a protest
petition should be filed in sufficient time for City staff to verify the petition.  Motion to postpone
carried.  Vo ting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.  Un der other business,
Amber Ash, Ward I constituent, spoke in support of the ordinance and proposed development
plans for the site.
ORDINANCE - Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a parcel of land be ing
situated in a portion of Section 20, Township 14 North, Range 66 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie
County, Wy oming, to be  known as t he Te rre Pont annexation (located northea st of the
intersection of Frontier Mall Drive and Prairie Avenue).  Mr. Shanor  moved to approve on third
and final reading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body present. (#3999)
ORDINANCE - Pursua nt to Sections  1.1.6 and 2.4.1 of the  Chey enne U nified
Development Code, amending the landscaping requirements under Unified Development Code
Sections 6.3.4a, 6.3.4b, and 6.3.7e .  Commenting on the proposed ordinance were: Lea Bain,
John Volk, Dave  Teubne r, Na ncy L oomis, Mark Ellison and Jan Nelson-Schroll.  Topics
discussed included the following:  Comments in support as well as non-support of the ordinance;
growing and maintaining liveable trees in Cheyenne’s climate; current landscaping requirements
and modification of same which would benefit various types of property owners/developers;
financial and maintenance concerns involving landscape installation and grounds care, including
City-owned p arks, g reenway pa ths a nd de tention po nd a reas; s upport fo r comm unity
beautification efforts; community appearance as a factor in attracting and retaining businesses
and employees; taking a progressive stance regarding landscaping relative to developments and
re-developments; primary purpose of the proposed ordinance; landscaping assisting with run-off
surface w ater and conse rvation/retention measures; providing  for f lexibility reg arding
requirements fo r s mall b usinesses o r d etermining on  a case-by-case ba sis un der c ertain
conditions; wise and efficient water use; water/pollutant discharges into storm sewers and storm
water m anagement; tr ees p roviding e conomic, e nvironmental a nd e nergy e fficient be nefits;
remaining competitive with other are a communities’ landsc aping requirements; annual UDC
review process; xeriscaping, and water re-use.  In response to comments involving maintenance
of City p arks, greenways and dete ntion ponds, Ri ck Parish, Parks a nd Recreation Director,
provided information on public areas maintained by Parks and Recreation; tree survival rate, and
water re-use and use of raw (untreated) water in certain areas.  Mr. Shanor moved to approve on
third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Discussion followed regarding landscaping costs
associated with development and re-development of properties, and landscaping requirements
and effect on large business vs. small business developments.  Mr. Shanor  moved to amend by
substitute ordina nce da ted September  13, 2013, seconde d by  Dr. Ri nne.  I t was noted the
proposed substitute provides clarification on the intent of an amendment that was approved at
the September 9  City Council meeting.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all membersth
of the governing body present.  Further discussion followed on the purpose of the or dinance,
which is to reduce the amount of required liveable landscaping which would financially help
small businesses; providing for flexibility; encouraging “in-fill” developments, and taking a
wider look at development issues.  Main motion as amended by substitute failed.  Voting “yes” --
Mr. Luna, Mr. Shanor and Ms. Williams.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook,
Dr. Rinne, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen voting “no”.
ORDINANCE - Amending the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (UDC), to allow,
after conditional discretionary review, Service Uses, Animal Care - Hospital/Clinic in the MUB
Mixed-Use Business Emphasis and the NB Neighborhood Business zone districts and to allow,
as a use permitted by right, Service Uses, Animal Care - Kennel in the LI Light Industrial zone
district.  L uke Meyer, representative for Black Dog Animal Rescue; Troy  Thompson, local
veterinarian; Brittany Steinman, owner of  local dog  day care and pe t hotel business, and Dr.
Tiffany Hea ley, local vete rinarian, offe red c omments i n support of the or dinance.  Topics
presented included: Accessible veterinary care services; proposed zone changes being appropriate
and providing for location options for animal care and kennel services, and minimum noise levels
generated by animal care facilities.  Mr. Shanor moved to approve on second reading, seconded
by Mr. C ook.  Co mments f ollowed in  f avor o f th e o rdinance.  U pon in quiry c oncerning
conditional discretionar y revie w, B randon Cammar ata, Planning  and De velopment offic e,
provided information on approvals required through the Board of Adjustment for certain types
of requested uses and clarified the ordinance addresses two uses, one being veterinary clinics and
the other pertaining to uses involving kennels, which would change kennel uses now allowed
only in CB and Agricultural zones to include Light Industrial zones.  Motion carried.  V oting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
ORDINANCE - Pursua nt to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2,
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from NB Neighborhood Business to MR-
2 Medium Density Residential - Developing for Lots 15 and 16, Block 17, Crest Ridge, 4  Filing,th
Cheyenne, Wyoming (located south of and adjacent to Storey Boulevard, between Canyon and
Ridge Roads).  Shelley Hood, adjacent property owner, spoke in opposition  to the proposed zone
change, expressing concerns with the type of development that is planned for the site (i.e. Multi-
family residential 4-plexes) and effect on the neighborhood relative to n oise and increase in
vehicle/traffic volume.  M r. Shanor moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Ms.
Broyles.  It was noted several letters of concern and opposition had been submitted from residents
in the vicinity, and that more in-depth review by City Council regarding proposed development
plans could occur during the platting process.  Upon inquiry, Brett Walker, Development Office,
clarified the current zone classification of NB has been in place since the original subdivision was
determined in 1996 and that the sole access into the property will be off Storey Blvd.  He advised,
based upon proposed development plans, a site plan review and conditional use approval will be
needed, and a re-platting action may come before the governing body for approval.  Upon further
inquiry, Mr. Walk er provided infor mation on development desig n that would not require
approval by the governing body.  Casey Palma, Steil Surveying, agent for Leaning Tree Homes,
the applicant, advised they have been refining the site plan and are close to having it completed,
and that a neighborhood meeting is scheduled for September 27  and notice of the meeting hasth
been mailed to vicinity property owners.  Ms. H ood advised she ha d not rec eived a meeting
notice and requested the agent reschedule the meeting to allow more  notification time to the
neighborhood.  Mr. Palma stated the meeting was preliminary in nature and if the project moves
forward additional notification of meetings relative to the site plan will be provided.  Mr. Palma
was requested to submit a copy of the upcoming meeting notice to members of the g overning
body.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Palma provided information on the “radius” area used in conjunction
with notification to property owners of the proposed zone change and utilization of property
ownership and mapping data through the County Assessor’s GIS system.  Brandon Cammarata,
Planning and Development office, explained the 140 ft. radius is a minimum distance, as set forth
in the UDC, and excludes streets and alleys.  Question was raised on the number of apartment
complexes that are located within each of the City’s Wards and concern was expressed that some
complexes have resulted in higher police calls for services.  In response to the question on the
number o f a partment c omplexes, Ma yor Ka ysen advised h e wo uld dis cuss th e ma tter w ith
Planning staff.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present with
the exception of Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna and Ms. Williams voting “no”.

Street, consisting  of 6,098 squar e fe et and ha ving a n appra ised value of  $6 ,098.00, to J ina
Morandin Costello, Jeanie M. Grogan, K. Tippy Costello, and Kyle W. Breeden.
Pursuant to comments by Amber Ash (refer to above agenda item on re-zoning ordinance
for Snyder Plaza), Mayor Kaysen noted the ordinance would be reviewed again at the October 8th
Public Services Committee meeting and the October 14  regular meeting of the governing bodyth
(meetings open to the public).  Ms. Wil liams commented on pro- growth and re ferenced the
protest petition she had filed relative to proposed retail business activity concerns involving the
site.
There being no fur ther business to come be fore the g overning body, the meeting  was
officially adjourned at 8:15 p.m.

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2013-10-14 | CLIP_ID: 397 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 397

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 October 14, 2013
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  P resent were: MAYOR - R ichard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor
and Ms. Williams.  Absent: Mr. Valdez.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff,
City Treasurer, and John Ridge, Deputy City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to
be routine items by  the governing body and will be ena cted by one motion.  There  will be no
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support
by two other membe rs is rec eived.  Any item removed f rom the Consent Ag enda w ill be
considered in its normal sequence on the agenda.)   Ms. Broyles  moved to approve, seconded by
Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on September 23, 2013.  Motion
(per Consent Agenda) carried.
 
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Ameri-
Tech HVAC Services) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Allen moved to approve payment
of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Luna.   Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members
of the governing body present in Council Chambers.   Following announcement of the vote, Mr.
Brown returned to Council Chambers.
Public hearing to  receive public comment on a proposed trade of real property owned by
the City of Cheyenne, being a parcel of land located generally east of Missile Drive and south of
the right-of-way of West 22  Street, consisting of 6,098 squa re feet and having an appraisednd
value of $6,098.00, to Jina Morandin Costello, Jeanie M. Grogan, K. Tippy Costello, and Kyle
W. Breeden in exchange for certain  real property generally located east of Missile Drive and
north of the right-of-way of West 22  Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming, having appraised value ofnd
$43,560.00.  Pete Laybourn spoke in support of the proposed trade of property which is affiliated
with a City drainage project in the vicinity.  Mayor Kaysen noted that Agenda I tem #22
(Resolution regarding the trade of properties) is related to the public hearing topic and further
input from the public could be m ade at the Octobe r 21  Finance Committee meeting.  Therest
being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen
closed the public hearing.
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5 .1.2,
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified
Development Code, chang ing the  zoning classific ation from  A-1 Agr icultural and Rura l
Residential (County) to MUR Mixed-Use Residential  Emphasis for Snyder Plaza, Cheyenne,
Wyoming (located east of and adjacent to Snyder Avenue, north of W. Allison Road) (postponed
from Sept. 23, 2013).  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Allen moved to withdraw
the item from the agenda, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members
of the governing body present.
  
ORDINANCE - Amending the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (UDC), to allow,
after conditional discretionary review, Service Uses, Animal Care - Hospital/Clinic in the MUB
Mixed-Use Business Emphasis and the NB Neighborhood Business zone districts and to allow,
as a use permitted by right, Service Uses, Animal Care - Kennel in the LI Light Industrial zone
district.  Troy Thompson, veterinarian, voiced his support for the ordinance.  Mr. Allen moved
to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” --
all members of the governing body present. (#4000)
ORDINANCE - Pursua nt t o Section 2.2.1, Z oning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2,
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from NB Neighborhood Business to MR-
2 Medium Density Residential - Developing for Lots 15 and 16, Block 17, Crest Ridge, 4  Filing,th
Cheyenne, Wyoming (located south of and adjacent to Storey Boulevard, between Canyon and
Ridge Roads).  John Bonifer, Steil Surveying, agent for Leaning Tree Homes, requested a two
week postponement to October 28, 2013.  Roger Alexander and Debbie Hall, vicinity property
owners, spoke in oppo sition to the zone chang e.  The y expressed conc erns with the ty pe of
development (multi-family residential 4- plexes) planned for  the site; maintaining  the cur rent
predominantly single residential home environment in the area, and effect on neighborhood with
regard to noise, increased vehicular traffic and potential for increase in crime.  Mr. Allen reported
there was no recommendation on the item from Public Services Committee.  Mr. Allen  moved
to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Mr. Allen moved to postpone
to Oct. 28, 2013, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Upon inquiry , Mr. Bonifer clarified the developer
requested the postponement.  Discussion included the  ap proximate number  of apa rtment
complexes located within each of the City Ward districts and the apparent need for affordable
housing.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present
with the exception of Mr. Luna voting “no”.
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Sections 1.1.6 & 2.4.1 of the Cheyenne Unified Development
Code, Repealing and Re-enacting Unified Development Code Section 2.4.2, Annexations.  Mr.
Allen reported there was no recommendation on the item from Public Services Committee.  Mr.
Allen moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Discussion involved the
purpose of the ordinance, which is to simplify the annexation process by more closely following
state statute provisions, and re move redundancy from the c urrent process.  Motion carrie d.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.  
ORDINANCE - Repealing Chapter 13.20, Wastewater and Disposal, in its entirety and
re-creating Chapter 13.20, Wastewater Treatment and Disposal, of Title 13, Public Services, of
the City of Cheyenne City Code.  Mr. Allen moved to approve on second reading, seconded by
Mr. Shanor.  Comments followed regarding the purpose of the ordinance, which is to bring City
Code provisions up to date with curr ent EPA and DEQ  regulations and requirements.  Upon
inquiry, J im Hughe s, Water Recla mation Manag er, B oard of  Public Utili ties, advised the
ordinance was pre pared pursuant to 2005 re visions to t he EPA’s na tional treatment program
which changed streamlining and pre-treatment rules, noting the update should make it easier for
BOPU staff and community industrial users to understand, and clarified the program has not been
updated for about 20 years.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body
present.
ORDINANCE - Amending Section 9.16.070(A), sale of tobacco to persons under the age
of eighteen years prohibited, of Chapter 9.16, Offenses by or against minors, of Chapter 9, Public
Peace and Welfare, of the Cheyenne City Code, pertaining to electronic cigarettes.  Officer John
Gay, Cheyenne Police Department (CPD), advised CPD supports the proposed ordinance change
and c ited b enefits o f in corporating th e s tate s tatute r estriction w ithin mu nicipal c ourt’s
jurisdiction for officer efficiency purposes; to provide the ability to verify re-occuring offenses;
to have a pre-determined bond/fine schedule, and for any juvenile assistance programs that may
be available through municipal court.  Amy Schicketanz, owner, Straight Line Vapors, and Lisa
Ammons, Wyoming Prevention Management office, each spoke in support of the ordinance to
restrict sales to minors.  E ach discussed and pr ovided data a nd statistical information on e-
cigarettes as well as regular cigarette tobacco products.  Ms. Schicketanz comments included the
following topics: E-cigarettes as a helpful tool for those who are trying to quit smoking; lack of
data reflecting that kids become addicted to e-cigarettes; her opposition to any potential future
ordinance involving e-cigarettes as a tobacco product and the City’s current smoking in public
places regulations; nicotene and e-cigarette (cartridge(s)) ingredients and chemicals, and review
of enforcement measures prior to the ordinance being implemented.  In support of her comments,
Ms. Schicketanz submitted a packet of data on e-cigarettes (on file with City Clerk’s office) to
members of the governing body.  Ms. Ammons’ comments included composition of e-cigarettes
and how they’re used (inhaled); court rulings determining e-cigarettes as tobacco products;
similar laws in other states, and lack of regulation of the products by the FDA.  Mr. Allen moved
to approve on second reading, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Upon inquiry, John Ridge, Deputy City
Attorney, clarified if a citation is written as an offense against city code rather than state statute
then the fin e would go to the City.  Clarification was made that the ordinance would not be
outlawing e-cigarettes but is aimed only at restricting sales of e-cigarettes to minors.  Discussion
included the following topics:   “Mirroring” state statute; preventing youth from gaining access
to these devic es; whether fines a ssessed to be r eceived by the City  was ove rshadowing the
purpose of the ordinance, and procedural benefits for police officers to be able to cite offenders
into municipal court vs. circ uit court.  Motion carr ied.  Voting  “yes” -- all member s of the
governing body present with the exception of Mr. Luna voting “no”.
ORDINANCE - Amending , cre ating, deleting  and re -creating various cha pters and
Sections within Titl e 8, He alth and Safe ty, of the Chey enne City Code rela ting to g eneral
provisions pertaining to Fire Protection and Regulations, and adoption of the International Fire
Code, 2012 Edition, and certain codes  and practices promulgated by the National Fire Protection
Association.  Mr. Shanor declared a conflict of interest (property business interest) and vacated
Council Chambers.  Gl en Garrett, architect, advised he was asked to attend a D DA design
meeting wherein the B uilding and Fire Departments discussed the new pr oposed codes and
amendments from the 2006 Code to the 2012 Code.  He reviewed and discussed the following
three items of concern: Retroactivity; concurrence (interdepartmental communication between
Building and Fire staff), and undefined terms.  His comments included local authority vs. State
Fire Marshall and state statute regulations and restrictions; effect on historic buildings that may
be remodeled or renovated; what constitutes a “distinct hazard”, and concern with enforcement
and complianc e.  Mr. Allen move d to ap prove on second rea ding, se conded by Mr. L una.
Discussion included the following topics: Ordinance providing some positive changes with
regard to day cares and disposal of f ireworks; existing structures and conditions; omissi on of
chief bui lding of ficial a uthority; di scretion o f f ire c hief o r d esignated f ire c ode of ficial;
opportunity for appeal of decisions involving interpretation of the codes, and inspection process
and procedures utili zed by the fire  department.  During  discussion and u pon inquiry, Mark
Norford, Division Chief, Cheyenne Fire and Rescue Department, provided information on the
fire c ode off icial; appea l proce ss throug h the Fir e Code B oard of  Appea ls and member
composition of the Board; examples of a distinct hazard scenario as well as what might constitute
an “egregious” violation, and how often inspections are conducted.  Mr. Garrett stated the fire
code official is defined in the fire code and it could be the fire chief or their designee.  Motion
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers with
the exception of Ms. Williams voting “no”.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Shanor
returned to Council Chambers.
ORDINANCE - Amending various sections contained in Chapters 8.20, 8.24, 8.28 and
8.32 within Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Cheyenne City Code relating to General Provisions
pertaining to Fire Protection and Regulations.  Mr. Allen moved to approve on second reading,
seconded by Mr. Cook.  It was noted the or dinance includes the topics of public display s of
fireworks and underground storage of tanks.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body present.
ORDINANCE - Amending Chapter 2.32, Fire Department, Section 2.32.020, Department
Personnel, and Cha pter 2.84, Misce llaneous B oards a nd Commi ssions, Section 2.84.050,
International Fire Code Boa rd of Appeal s, of Title 2, Administration and Personnel, of  the
Cheyenne City Code pertaining to the Fire and Rescue Department.  Mr. Allen moved to approve
on second reading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  It was advised the ordinance is a bookkeeping clean-
up to Cit y Code sections involving F ire De partment divis ion names and ti tles, and the
International Fire Code.  Motion carri ed.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body
present.

carried (amount not to exceed $126,000.00). 
Announcement was made of the Re-appointment of Earl B. Kincheloe to the Cheyenne
Housing Authority Board, to be confirmed October 28, 2013. 
Announcement was made of a public hearing to be held October 28, 2013, at 6:00 p.m.,
in City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, to hear public comments
regarding whether the Mayor and the City Clerk of the City of Cheyenne should be authorized
to file an application for grant and/or loan funds under the Wyoming Business Council Business
Ready Community Grant and Loan Program for funding for the construction of a rail spur within
the Swan Ranch Industrial Park, located generally west of Interstate 25 and south of Interstate
80, Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming.  The public is invited to attend and participate. 
Under other business:
Pete Laybourn commented on landscaping requirements and areas along Lincolnway that
are in need up maintenance  and upkeep, such as mowing  and weeding, graffiti removal, etc.;
spoke in support of the improvements made in Holliday Park relative to the Greenway underpass,
and requested City Council review the Keep America Beautiful program, commenting on the City
of Casper’s beautification program.
Major Dorr commented favorably on the water drainage project currently going on in the
west part of Cheyenne, and questioned whether there had been any discussions with potentially
interested businesses regarding opening a grocery store on the west side.  Mayor Kaysen advised
that the City, through various representatives, have talked with major grocery stores asking them
to look at Cheyenne, specifically the area defined, with no success to date.  Du ring further
comments, Mayor Kaysen stated discussions had also been held about a cooperative effort (i.e.
potential to form a co-op) when the Safeway Store closed on Pioneer.  Pete Laybourn advised he
was involved in a pa st effort on how to cre ate a co-op in Chey enne and in the meeting s and
discussions, they learned it was a very challenging endeavor and cost-wise a co-op could not
compete financially with other major grocery/food stores.
Mr. Shanor questioned the public comment process under other business at City Council
meetings as it pertains to items on the meeting agenda that had been previously discussed or acted
upon earlier in the meeting, and requested City Attorney staff research the topic.
There being no further business to come bef ore the governing body, the meeting was
officially adjourned at 8:06  p.m.
 Submitted by,
 
  
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
