RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 October 28, 2013
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  P resent were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez and
Ms. Williams.  Absent: Ms. Broyles.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, City
Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to
be routine items by the governing body and will be ena cted by one motion.  There  will be no
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support
by two  other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be
considered in its normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Shanor, and pursuant to
support received by Mr. Allen and Mr. Cook, Agenda Item #18 was removed from the Consent
Agenda.  Upon request by Dr. Rinne, a nd pursuant to support rece ived by Mr. Allen and Mr .
Cook, Agenda Item #24(a) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Allen  moved to approve
remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on October 14, 2013.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
 
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Ameri-
Tech HVAC Services) and vacated Council Chambers.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve payment
of vouchers as pre sented, seconded by Mr. L una.  Comment followed re garding a vouche r
payable to TruGreen Commercial, contract services for grounds aeration/seed work, due to labor
shortage.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present in Council
Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown returned to Council Chambers.
Public hearing to receive public comments regarding whether the Mayor and the City
Clerk of the City of Cheyenne should be authorized to file an application for grant and/or loan
funds under  the Wy oming Business Council Business Ready  Communi ty Gra nt and L oan
Program for funding for the construction of a rail spur within the Swan Ranch Industrial Park,
located generally west of Interstate 25 and south of Interstate 80, Cheyenne, Laramie County,
Wyoming.  Ma yor Ka ysen noted that Ag enda I tem #21 (Resolution regar ding the gra nt
application) is re lated to the public hearing  topic and is being  rec ommended (by  Financ e
Committee) for postponement.  Due to the anticipated postponement of the resolution, Mayor
Kaysen queried whether there was any objection by City Council members to resc hedule the
public hearing to Tuesday, November 12, 2013 at 6:00 p.m.  There  being no objection raised,
Mayor Ka ysen ann ounced the hear ing w as being  canc elled and w ould be re scheduled for
November 12 , and noted an announcement of the rescheduled hearing was included on theth
meeting agenda (Agenda Item #28 (d)).
ORDINANCE - Pursua nt to Section  2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2,
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from NB Neighborhood Business to MR-
2 Medium Density Residential - Developing for Lots 15 and 16, Block 17, Crest Ridge, 4  Filing,th
Cheyenne, Wyoming (located south of and adjacent to Storey Boulevard, between Canyon and
Ridge Roads) (Postponed from October 14, 2013).  Brett Walker, Development Office, advised
pursuant to a pr otest peti tion that had bee n filed opposing  the zone chang e, it had bee n
determined the petition met Wyoming Statute §15-1-603 requirements that at least 20% of
eligible property owners (within the boundary area) had signed the petition, therefore, approval
of the ordina nce would require an affirmative vote by  at least 3/4ths of all member s of the
governing body.  Roger Alexander, Tom Hood , John Nichols, Shelley Hood, Elisa Sanders, Kyle
Lovell and De bbie Hall, re sidents in the vicinity , spoke in opposition to t he proposed zone
change.  Comments included the following topics: Developer’s offer to sell the property to the
neighbors; prop osed construction of multi-family residential units in a  predominantly single
residential home e nvironment; proper ty values; quality  of life c oncerns i nvolving potential
increase in noise, traffic, safety, density and crime; renters not having a property investment in
the neighborhood, and number of multi-residential (apartment) units already in existence in the
area.  J. Volk, local real estate agent, advised of the community’s need for mid-range rental units
and his belief that the proposed zone change is applicable for current times relative to progressive
growth and development of the City.  Boyd Wiggam, Wyoming Liberty Group, commented on
difficulty in finding rental properties in the community; applicable zoning for the property and
recommended zoning f or the a rea contained in PlanCheyenne, and property rights and equa l
protection for the owner of the property proposed to be re-zoned.  Casey Palma, Steil Surveying
Services, agent for a pplicant, Leaning Tree H omes, advised the a pplicant is a loca lly-owned
company and discussed meetings that have been held with the neighborhood to listen to concerns
and developer’s willingness to continue to listen to residents during the development project.  Mr.
Palma commented on criteria contained in PlanCheyenne and the Unified Development Code
(UDC) and meeting compliance with codes, a nd that acc ess into the proposed development
would be o ff Storey Blvd., noting a proposed lay-out of the development is available.  Donn
Edmunds commented on wha t is the appr opriate a rea to place a partments in Chey enne.
Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Allen  moved to appr ove on third and f inal
reading, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Discussion topics included: Comments in support as well
as non-support of the requested zone change; appropriate locations for multi-plex residential
units; developer working with residents on issues of concern; best interests of the community;
the UDC; focusing on agenda item topic of re-zoning and not the specific type of development
for the property, which will involve a plat and possible variance by the Board of Adjustment;
disapproval of a past zone change request involving property off Yellowstone Road; whether the
proposed zone change is appropriate for the property owner’s intended use, and uses by right
allowed unde r a MR-2 zone cla ssification.  Upon inquiry , Mr. Walker provide d g eneral
information regarding density allowed per the lot size, noting setback and frontage requirements
would be reviewed; potential variance required by the Board of Adjustment, and site plan review
process.  M otion fa iled ( City Cle rk no te: Pu rsuant t o th e pr otest p etition a nd sta te sta tute
provision, minimum of eight affirmative votes required for final passage).  Voting “yes” -- Mr.
Allen, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez, Ms. Williams and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” – Mr. Brown, Mr.
Cook, Mr. Luna and Dr. Rinne.
ORDINANCE - Pursuant to Sections 1.1.6 & 2.4.1 of the Cheyenne Unified Development
Code, Repealing and Re-enacting Unified Development Code Section 2.4.2, Annexations.  Mr.
Allen moved to appr ove on third and f inal reading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion carrie d.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present. (#4001)
ORDINANCE - Repealing Chapter 13.20, Wastewater and Disposal, in its entirety and
re-creating Chapter 13.20, Wastewater Treatment and Disposal, of Title 13, Public Services, of
the C ity o f C heyenne C ity C ode.  M r. Al len m oved t o ap prove o n t hird an d fi nal reading,
seconded by Ms. Williams.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body
present. (#4002)
ORDINANCE - Amending Section 9.16.070(A), sale of tobacco to persons under the age
of eighteen years prohibited, of Chapter 9.16, Offenses by or against minors, of Chapter 9, Public
Peace and Welfare, of the Cheyenne City Code, pertaining to electronic cigarettes.  Officer John
Gay, Cheyenne Police Department, provided information, pursuant to comments made at the last
meeting, on the ability to enforce the ordinance based on nicotine information contained on e-
cigarette cartridges, and commented that enforcement options for violators may include use of
diversion programs, such as community service.  Mr. Allen moved to approve on third and final
reading, seconded by Ms. Williams.  Upon inquiry, Officer Gay confirmed sales of an e-cigarette
itself t o minors, u nder t he o rdinance, wo uld n ot b e p rohibited, b ut an y af filiated ca rtridge
containing nicotine fluid would make it illegal, and stated the definition of tobacco contained in
the ordinance matches the definition in state law.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members
of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Allen, Mr. Cook and Mr. Luna voting
“no”. (#4003)
ORDINANCE - A mending, cre ating, deleting  and re -creating various cha pters and
Sections within Titl e 8, He alth and Safe ty, of the Chey enne City Code rela ting to ge neral
provisions pertaining to Fire Protection and Regulations, and adoption of the International Fire
Code, 2012 Edition, and certain codes  and practices promulgated by the National Fire Protection
Association.  Mr. Shanor declared a conflict of interest (property business interest) and vacated
Council Chambers.  Chief James Martin, Fire  and Rescue Department, spoke in support of a
proposed substitute ordinance; stated his belief the ordinance meets the state’s intent that codes
can not be less restrictive than those adopted by the state, and read a statement of support for the
ordinance he had re ceived from the Dir ector of Planning  Services, who overse es the City ’s
Building Department.  Glen Garrett, local architect and downtown property owner, advised he
and oth er d owntown pr operty ow ners a ssisted t he Do wntown D evelopment A uthority wi th
funding to hire a code consultant to review and discuss concerns regarding the proposed updated
code version.  Mr. Garrett’s comments included the following topics of concern: Retroactive
enforcement and potential effect on owners of historic buildings; uncertainty of the meaning of
the phrases “distinct hazard(s)” and buildings “not legally in existence at the time of adoption of
this code”; interpretation and enforcement issues affiliated with both Building and Fire codes;
recent adoption of updated building codes, and State Fire Marshall’s role involving code versions
and compliance with the state’s adopted version.  He explained reasons why he felt concurrence
of cod e interpretations (i.e. Building  Codes and F ire Codes) be tween de signated Fir e and
Building staff was needed, and suggested the structure and processes of the Building Code Board
of Appeals and Fire Code Board of Appeals be reviewed.  Mr. Allen reported there was no
recommendation on the item from Public Services Committee.  Mr. Allen moved to approve on
third and final r eading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Mr. Allen m oved to amend by  substitut e,
seconded by Mr. Luna.  It was advised the  substitute amends ordina nce Section 8.12.030 by
inserting new wording for section B., amending Section 102.1 of the I nternational Fire Code
(IFC), by adding subsection 5 that will read:  “In making a determination whether a particular
structure, facility or condition constitutes a distinct hazard, the fire code official shall follow the
guidelines and procedures specified for Section 102.1.4 which appear on page 1-8 of the official
commentary to the 2012 Edition of the International Fire Code.”  Mr. Garrett stated the official
commentary information is “obscure” and not easily available to the public, expressing that the
commentary may contain subjective language, and questioned whether the page reference meant
pages 1 throug h 8 or a s ingle page numbered 1-8.  Upon inquiry , Mark Nor ford, Chief of
Prevention, Fire a nd Rescue D epartment, clarifie d pag e 1-8 r eferenced in the propose d
amendment means page 1 “dash” 8 (single page) and that a copy of the page will be provided to
the City Clerk’s office.  Mr. Norford noted  correspondence dated October 24, 2013 (on file with
City Clerk’s office) that he had submitted to members of the governing body that included the
commentary wording pertaining to Item 4 of Section 102.1, as contained on page 1-8, and read
the commentary wording.  Comment followed in support of the proposed substitute.  Motion to
amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.
Discussion topics included the phrase buildings “not legally in existence”; current buildings
under construction; building codes vs. fire codes, and concern with removing the retroactivity
wording that is included in curr ent code.  During  discussion and upon inquiry , Mr. Norf ord
provided information regarding current buildings under construction, which will be inspected
under current existing code, and historic buildings relative to being legally in existence, and
advised building and fire codes are designed to w ork together and neither code can waive
requirements of the other.  Mr. Allen moved to amend, seconded by Mr. Cook, ordinance Section
8.12.030 (B), amending Section 102.1 of the IFC, by inserting the following  subsection: 4.
Existing structures, facilities and conditions which, in the opinion of the fire code official, and
with concurrence of the chief building official or his designee, constitute a distinct hazard to life
or property.  Upon request, Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, reiterated the wording of the proposed
amendment.  Mr. Norford advised the department believes the proposed amendment would be
less restrictive than the code adopted by the state and could create a situation which, if
concurrence was not reac hed, could a llow a distinct hazard to rema in in place, whic h may
increase a hazard to the public.  He noted the ordinance proposed by the Fire Department was
vetted through the building of ficial a nd had bee n ag reed upon by  both Fire  and B uilding
departments.  Speaking in support of the amendment were Bob Clary and Mr. Garrett, noting it
would provide c oncurrence through cooperative efforts between the two  departments.  Mr.
Norford noted there  is a specif ic appeals process available to t he public to re dress any code
interpretation concerns which involves both a Building Code Board of Appeals and a Fire Code
Board of Appeals, and, with regard to the proposed amendment, there are many potential distinct
hazards that may need to be addressed through consultation with other individuals that fall
outside of the chief building official’s authority or expertise.  Upon request, Ms. Intlekofer read
the wording of the proposed amendment on the floor.  Upon request for clarification, Mr. Norford
confirmed his previous statement that it was the opinion of F ire Department staff tha t the
proposed amendment could cause a situation that would be less restrictive than the code version
adopted by the state.  Motion to amend failed (tie vote).  Voting “yes” – Mr. Allen, Mr. Cook,
Mr. Luna and Ms. Williams.  Voti ng “no” – Mr . Brown, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Val dez and Mayor
Kaysen.  Main motion as amended failed (City Clerk note: Ordinance on final reading requires
minimum affirmative vote (6 votes) of all members of the governing body to pass).  Voting “yes”
-- Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” – Mr. Cook, Mr.
Valdez and Ms. Williams.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Shanor returned to Council
Chambers.  Unde r other  busine ss later in the mee ting, Mr. Cook, who had voted on the
prevailing side on the motion to approve on final reading as amended, moved to reconsider the
vote on the ordinance (Agenda Item #12), seconded by Mr. Allen.  Mr. Shanor declared a conflict
of int erest ( property bu siness i nterest) a nd vacated Co uncil Ch ambers.  U pon r equest, Ms.
Intlekofer reviewed criteria involving a motion to reconsi der and read the entitlement of the
ordinance proposed to be reconsidered into the record.  Motion to reconsider carried.  Voting
“yes” – all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following comment
that it has been the practice of City Council in the past that when an item has been brought up and
approved for r econsideration under other business the item has be en postponed to allow for
public awareness of the action, Dr. Rinne moved to postpone the item to November 12, 2013,
seconded by Mr. Valde z.  Mot ion to postpone carr ied.  Voting “yes” – all member s of the
governing body present in Council Chambers with the exception of Ms. Williams voting “no”.
Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Shanor returned to Council Chambers.
Following a 10 minute recess, Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at 8:20 p.m.

RESOLUTION - Requiring the officers described in Section 2.08.010(A) of the Cheyenne
City Code, other than fire chief, to submit departmental estimates of all proposed expenditures
at least five months prior to the expiration of any budget approved pursuant to W.S. §16-4-111.
Reporting for F inance Committee, Mr. B rown moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Mr.
Shanor moved to amend, seconded by Mr. Allen, as follows:  (1) Change phrase contained in
entitlement language from “ ... to submit departmental estimates of all proposed expenditures ...”
to read “... to submit a narr ative of the anticipated budget, strategic goals and objectives and
departmental priorities for the upcoming fiscal year ...”; (2) Change phrase contained in the 5th
Whereas clause from “... benefit from receiving individual departmental estimates of all proposed
expenditures ...” to re ad “... be nefit from receiving a nar rative of the depa rtmental goals and
objectives ...”; (3) Change phrase contained in the 6  Whereas clause from “... the r eceipt ofth
individual departmental estimates of all proposed expenditures ...” to read “... the receipt of the
narrative described above ...“; (4) Striking the  first Now, ther efore, be it re solved clause and
replacing in its entirety with the following : “Now, therefore, be it re solved by the Governing
Body of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, that each officer described in Section 2.08.010(A) of
the Cheyenne City Code, other than the fire chief, shall submit their narrative of the anticipated
budget, strategic goals and objectives, and department priorities for the upcoming fiscal year.”,
and (5) Striking in its entirety the last resolved clause which begins with “... Be it further resolved
that each departmental estimate ...”.  Comment followed that the intent of the amendment is to
give Cou ncil m embers a  sn apshot of  wh ere de partment h eads a re in t he bu dget pr ocess i n
February and provide  more tra nsparency.  Mr.  Brown offered a friendly amendment to the
proposed wording change to the Now, therefore, be it resolved clause (re:. (4) above) to include
the following words at the end of the sentence “... to the City Council”.  Mr. Shanor, as maker
of the motion to a mend, concurred with inclusion of the friendly amendment wording in h is
motion.  Mr. Allen raised a  point of order concerning clarification of the friendly amendment
relative to t he status of the initial motion to amend, and, following verification, voiced his
concurrence, as the second to the motion to amend, with inclusion of the friendly amendment
wording.  Upon quer y and ther e being no objection ra ised by City Council members, May or
Kaysen questioned whether there needed to be a specified time frame for submission to the City
Council relative to wo rding, including the friendly amendment, to be c ontained in the “Now,
therefore, be it resolved” clause.  Mr. Shanor verified the time frame outlined in the resolution
indicating “at least five (5) months prior to the expiration of any budget approved pursuant to
W.S. § 16-4-111" would apply.  Motion to amend, including the friendly amendment wording,
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.  Comment was voiced as
to  t he necessity of the resolution.  Main motion, as amended, carried.  Voting “yes” -- a ll
members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Br own and Mayor Kaysen
voting “no”.  (#5533)
 
[CA]  RESOLUTION - Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk of the City of Cheyenne to enter
into an agreement for the trade of real property owned by the City of Cheyenne being a parcel of

Cheyenne, Wyoming, to Jina Morandin Costello, Jeanie M. Grogan, K. Tippy Costello, and Kyle

RESOLUTION - A cknowledging re ceipt o f ‘ The C ity o f C heyenne, W yoming,
Compensation and Classification Study, Final Report,’ dated October 1, 2013, prepared by the
Mercer Group, Inc., and referring said study to the ‘Employee Investment Study Implementation
Team’ created by Resolution No. 5508.”  Dr . Rinne reported the Committee of the Who le
recommendation w as to  a dopt t he re solution wi th n ew l anguage “ Acknowledging re ceipt”
(replacing the previous wording of “Approving”) and moved to so adopt, seconded by Mr. Allen.
Mr. Shanor moved to amend the last paragraph that begins with “Be it further resolved ...“ by
adding the words “all or some of” to be contained in the following phrase “... a plan containing
alternative means of implementing all or some of the recommendations of said study ...”, and to
change the date contained in the paragraph from January 1, 2014 to February 1, 2014, seconded
by Mr. Luna.  Comment followed on the intent of the amendment, which is to provide the team
with more direction and allow more time for the team to provide its recommendations.  Motion
to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.  Carol Intlekofer,
City Clerk, expressed her belief that the entitlement as listed on t he agenda was incorrect and
should instead read a “Resolution Approving ...” to match the document as initially submitted
on the October 14  agenda.  Ms. Intlekofer explained the approved recommendation action at theth
Committee of the Whole meeting involved an amendment by substitute that changed the word
“approving” to “a cknowledging receipt of”, and offered suggestion that perhaps a motion to
amend by substitute may be in order.  Dr. Rinne confirmed the recommending action taken at the
Committee meeting was to adopt the resolution as amended by substitute which replaced the
original language “approving” with “acknowledging receipt of” and also corrected some clerical
errors, and moved to amend by substitute.  Ms. Intlekofer requested verification as to the correct
version of the substitute, referencing the attorney’s stamp date of October 23, 2013, and it was
confirmed the substitute resolution was the docume nt that was acted upon at the Committee
meeting.  Mr. Allen raised a point of order inquiring whether there was a main motion on the
floor with Mayor Kaysen advising there was a motion on the floor to amend by substitute (City
Clerk note:  Motion to amend by substitute was made, however, no second to the motion was
received.)  Procedural discussion followed regarding the substitute resolution, intent of the main
motion made by Dr. Rinne to adopt pursuant to recommending action taken at the Committee
meeting which included changing the language of “approving” to “acknowledging receipt of”
(and providing for clerical corrections), and relationship of the substitute to Mr. Shanor’s motion
to amend that had been approved.  Upon inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney, advised procedurally
the substitute resolution should have bee n approved prior to the motion to amend (i.e. Mr.
Shanor’s motion), and expressed that approval of the substitute will not alter the changes in the
first approved amendment (i.e. Mr. Shanor’s motion).  It was confirmed the agenda wording for
the item was incorrect and, as no dissent was voiced, that all of the members of the governing
body present were aware of and in agreement that the main motion to adopt, as explained by Dr.
Rinne regarding the Committee’s recommendation, pertained to the resolution as amended and
recommended at t he Committee of the Whole meeting .  Upon inquiry  and with no objection
raised by those City Council members present, Mayor Kaysen stated the main motion on the floor
was to adopt the r esolution which has the lang uage of “acknowledging receipt of” and which
incorporates Mr. S hanor’s amendment.  Motion car ried.  Voting  “yes” -- all member s of the
governing body present. (#5535)

Building, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming.

whether compliance of annexation conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exist for a parcel

whether compliance of annexation conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exist for a parcel

Annexation (located at 3904 Whitney Road approximately 200 feet west of the northwest corner
of the intersection of Whitney Road and Countryside Avenue).
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held Nove mber 12, 2013, at 6:00
p.m., in C ity Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, to hear public
comments regarding whether the Mayor and the City Clerk of the City of Cheyenne should be
authorized to file an application for grant and/or loan funds under the Wyoming Business Council
Business Ready Community Grant and Loan Program for funding for the construction of a rail
spur within the Swan Ranch Industrial Park, located generally west of Interstate 25 and south of
Interstate 80, Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming.
Under other business, motion to reconsider Agenda Item #12 carried - refer to minutes
section above (re: Ordinance pertaining to Title 8 of City Code regarding proposed International
Fire Code, 2012 Edition, adoption, etc.).
There being no further business to come bef ore the governing body, the meeting was
officially adjourned at 9:06  p.m.

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 November 12, 2013
Due to the Veterans Day holiday, the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne instead
convened in regular session on this date beginning at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present
were: MA YOR -  Ric hard L. K aysen; COUN CIL ME MBERS - M r. Al len, Mr . Brown, M s.
Broyles, Mr. Cook, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor , Mr. Valdez and Ms. Williams.  Absent: Mr. Luna.
Also pr esent: Ca rol I ntlekofer, City  Cle rk; L ois Huf f, City  Tr easurer, a nd Da n Wh ite, Ci ty
Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to
be routine items by  the governing body and will be ena cted by one motion.  There  will be no
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support
by two other membe rs is rec eived.  Any item removed f rom the Consent Ag enda w ill be
considered in its normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Allen, and pursuant to
support received by Dr. Rinne and Mr. Shanor, Agenda Item #20  was removed from the Consent
Agenda.  Mr. Allen  moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Brown.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on October 28, 2013.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Ameri-
Tech HVAC Services) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Valdez  moved to approve payment
of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members
of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr.
Brown returned to Council Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive public comments regarding whether the
Mayor and the City Clerk of the City of Cheyenne should be authorized to file an application for
grant and/or loan funds under the Wyoming Business Council Business Ready Community Grant
and Loan Program for funding for the construction of a rail spur within the Swan Ranch Industrial
Park, lo cated g enerally we st o f Interstate 25  a nd so uth o f Interstate 80 , Ch eyenne, Laramie
County, Wyoming.  Scott Sutherland, Granite Peak Development, provided a brief overview of
the initial Swan Ranch Industrial Park project, and information on the components of the proposed
grant a nd/or loan re quest involving t he rail spur c onnection and a ssociated de velopment
agreement.  He stated due to rising costs for the project, they are looking at the grant and loan
program to help off -set expenses.  He  advised e ven though the City  will be  the “venue” for
applying for the loan, the only indebtedness that may occur will be Granite Peak’s responsibility.
Mr. Sutherland a dvised of the a pplication and appr oval proc ess and provide d additional
information contained in the development agreement concerning Granite Peak’s responsibilities,
noting that Agenda Item #18 (Resolution) is affiliated with moving the application forward.  There
being no further comments or input and purpose of the public hearing having been met, Mayor
Kaysen closed the hearing.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive public comments on the following topic:
To determine whether compliance of annexation conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402
exist for a pa rcel of land being  situated in a portion of the Nor thwest Quarter of Sec tion 13,
Township 13 North, Range 67 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming to be known as
the CST Annexation (located at the northwest corner of the intersection of West College Drive
and Southwest Drive).  Jim Flesher, Planning and Development office, reviewed requirements and
compliance of conditions contained in Wy o. Stat. §15-1 -402, including specific information
indicating compliance with Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402(a)(i) through (vi).   Mr. F lesher advised the
parcel is proposed to be part of a redevelopment (expansion) of an existing truck stop business.
Upon inquiry, Mr. Flesher provided information on the site location relative to where the animal
shelter is located.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the public hearing
having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive public comments on the following topic:
To determine whether compliance of annexation conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402
exist for a pa rcel of land being  situated in a portion of the N ortheast Quarter of Section 26,
Township 14 North, Range 66 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming to be known as
the Ko ritnik Annexation (located at 3904 Whitney Road approximately 200 feet west of the
northwest corner of the intersection of Whitney Road and Countryside Avenue).  Jim Flesher,
Planning and Development office, reviewed requirements and compliance of conditions contained
in Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402, including specific information indicating compliance with Wyo. Stat.
§15-1-402(a)(i) through  (vi).  Upon inquiry , Mr. Fle sher c onfirmed the pe tition requesting
annexation was submitted by the property owner; that the parcel is approximately one acre in size,
and that the type of proposed property development action has not yet been received.  There being
no further comments or input and purpose of the public hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen
closed the hearing.
ORDINANCE - A mending, creating, deleting  and re -creating various cha pters and
Sections within Title 8, Hea lth and Safety , of the Chey enne City  Code rela ting to g eneral
provisions pertaining to Fire Protection and Regulations, and adoption of the International Fire
Code, 2012 Edition, and certain codes  and practices promulgated by the National Fire Protection
Association (postponed from Oct. 28, 2013).  Mr. Shanor declared a conflict of interest (property
business interest) and vacated Council Chambers.  Chief Jim Martin, Fire and Rescue Department,
spoke in favor of the ordinance.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Brown moved to
approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Mr. Brown moved to amend Section
4 by replacing the current language with the following: “That this ordinance shall be in full force
and effect upon its passage and publication.”, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Motion to amend carried.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Comments were
expressed regarding retroactivity concerns, and the need to have the updated fire code in place.
Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present in
Council Ch ambers.  F ollowing a nnouncement o f t he vo te, M r. Sha nor r eturned to  Cou ncil
Chambers. (#4004)
ORDINANCE - Amending various sections contained in Chapters 8.20, 8.24, 8.28 and
8.32 within Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Cheyenne City Code relating to General Provisions
pertaining to Fire Protection and Regulations (postponed from Oct. 28, 2013).  Mark N orford,
Division Chief of Prevention, Fire and Rescue Department, spoke in favor of the ordinance and
explained changes contained in a proposed substitute ordinance.  Mr. Brown moved to approve
on third and final reading, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Mr. Brown moved to amend by substitute
(dated Oct. 22, 2013), seconded by Ms. Broyles.  It was advised the substitute provides for clerical
corrections and does not change the substance of the ordinance.  Motion to amend by substitute
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.  Mr. Brown moved to amend
Section 6  of  the  su bstitute by re placing the  c urrent l anguage wi th t he fo llowing: “ That th is
ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its passage and publication.”, seconded by Ms.
Broyles.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
Main motion as amended by substitute, as amended, carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body present. (#4005)
ORDINANCE - Amending Chapter 2.32, Fire Department, Section 2.32.020, Department
Personnel, and Cha pter 2.84, Miscellaneous B oards a nd Commi ssions, S ection 2.84.050,
International Fire Code B oard of  Appea ls, of Title 2, Administration and Personnel, of the
Cheyenne City Code pertai ning to the Fire and Rescue D epartment (postponed from Oc t. 28,
2013).  Mark Norford, Cheyenne F ire a nd Rescue, e xplained the or dinance will “clea n-up”
language and clarify the department’s organizational structure.  Mr. Brown moved to approve on
third and final r eading, seconde d by Mr. Cook.  Mr. Br own moved to amen d Section 2 by
replacing the current language with the following: “That this ordinance shall be in full force and
effect upon its passage and publication.”, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Motion to amend carried.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.  Main motion as amended carried.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present. (#4006)
ORDINANCE - Amending the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (UDC), as approved
by Ordinance No. 3943 on J anuary 23, 2012, to adopt amendments identified a s Election and
Ideological Sign Amendments from 2013 Annual Review.  Mark Butler commented on reasonable
restrictions with regard to election campaign signs and posed a scenario relative to neighborhood
disputes involving ideological signs.  Tim Kingston spoke in favor of the ordinance as reviewed,
discussed and amended at the Public Services Committee meeting; reviewed a past court decision
involving the City and election signs relative to time limitations; current sign provisions contained
in the UDC, and commented on freedom of speech relative to election as well as ideological signs.
Boyd Wiggam, Wyoming Liberty Group, spoke in favor of the ordinance, referencing suggestions
for c hanges to the propose d ordinance that he had pr eviously submitt ed to g overning body
members, and protecting individual liberty and first amendment rights.  Mr. Brown moved to
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Mr. Brown moved to amend language in
Section 7 (Residential District Sign Allowances) by removing “No single sign shall be more than
6 square feet” and replacing it with “No single sign shall be more than 16 square feet”, seconded
by Ms. Wi lliams.  P ursuant to a point of inquiry  raised by  Mr. Shanor, it  was confirmed the
amendment recommended by Public Services Committee was to change 6 square feet to 16 square
feet (4x4 ft in size).  Discussion topics included: Comments in support as well as non-support of
the proposed amendment; placement of signs generally throughout the community and City Code
restrictions contained in Chapter 8.52.; large free-standing signs and placement of same; concern
with l oosening re gulations; si gn r egulations a pplicable to e lection a nd id eological si gns vs .
commercial advertising signs; large signs obstructing vehicle operators’ sight lines and potential
liability; size restrictions; enforcement concerns; public right-of-way areas vs. private property
owner’s decision to allow sign placements, and difference between election signs vs. ideological
signs.  Dur ing disc ussion and upon inquiry , Dan White, City  Attorney , advised, without
conducting further review, it would appear that the proposed ordinance changes to the UDC would
not apply to commercial signs, and that without specific facts involving an incident he could not
respond to the inquiry raised concerning determination of liability fault generally if a vehicular
accident occurred due to driver distrac tion while viewing a sig n.  Motion to amend car ried.
Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Mr. Shanor, Mr. Valdez, Ms. Williams and Mayor Kaysen.
Voting “no” -- Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook and Dr. Rinne.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend by deleting the
3  b ullet p oint i n S ections 7  an d 9 , ad dressing el ection s igns (Residential Di strict S ignrd
Allowances and Non-Residential District Sign Allowances respectively), that reads “No time
limitations”, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Mr. King ston spoke in  opposition to the amendment
stating his belief that keeping the wording was “key” and that citizens should be able to choose
the length of time for sign placements.  Discussion followed on signage time and size limitations;
current UDC restrictions; election sign placement restriction near polling places as required by
state law; homeowner associations and covenant restrictions, and private property owners’ and
election candidates’ responsibilities.  During discussion and upon inquiry, Brandon Cammarata,
Planning and Development Director, explained current provisions contained in the UDC relative
to election signs being treated as temporary signs, and that the proposed ordinance would change
the omit the reference to temporary signs, and provided information on allowable signs during the
last general election year, noting that regulation of sign placements near an election polling place
was outside of the UDC as it was more strictly regulated by state statute.  Mr. White confirmed
that the restriction of election signs near polling places is a state law.  Motion to amend f ailed.
Voting “yes” -- Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook and Dr. Rinne.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Mr.
Shanor, Mr. Valdez, Ms. Williams and Mayor Kaysen.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend by striking
Sections 8 and 10 which would remove new category sections dealing with ideological signs,
seconded by Mr. Allen.  Mr. Wiggam and Mr. Kingston spoke in opposition to the amendment,
citing freedom of speech concerns.  Buck Holmes, Laramie County Commissioner, questioned
whether the recent Laramie County Community College bond election signs would be considered
to be ideological signs with Mr. White advising under the proposed ordinance, those types of bond
election s igns wo uld be  c onsidered e lection s igns.  D iscussion c ontinued o n id eological vs .
election signs; types of messages/content that may be conveyed on ideological signs; enforcement
concerns; freedom of speech, and whether the City’s sign restrictions have been legally challenged
since the previous court case as initially referenced by Mr. Kingston.  Upon inquiry, Mr. White
referenced the past court case against the City, as noted previously by Mr. Kingston, and stated
when he reviewed the proposed ordinance he felt the UDC changes would be consistent with the
judgment that had been entered against the City in that case.  He further advised if the pending
amendment carried it would be necessary for him to go back and again review the court order and
analyze the City’s position.  Motion to amend failed.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Ms. Broyles and
Dr. Rinne.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Brown, Mr. Cook, Mr. Sh anor, Mr. Valdez, Ms. Williams and
Mayor Kaysen.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Brown, Mr. Shanor, Mr.
Valdez, Ms. Williams and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Allen, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook and
Dr. Rinne.

RESOLUTION - Authorizing the May or and the  City Clerk to sign a  final plat and
development agreement for Terre Pont Subdivision, a portion of the S1/2 of Section 20, T.14N.,
R.66W., 6th P.M., C heyenne, Wyoming (located at the nor theast corner of the intersection of
Prairie Avenue and F rontier Mall Drive).  Ma rk Butler, Inberg Miller Engineering, advised a
revised agreement has been prepared and it works toward the City’s goal of future completion of
Rue Terre and storm sewer work.  Mr. Brown moved to adopt with staff  recommendation #1,
seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Mr. B rown moved to a mend by substitute (dated Nov. 12, 2013),
seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Upon inquiry, Jim Flesher, Planning and Development office, explained
the substitute, involving changes to resolution and the agreement, now titled a memorandum of
understanding (MOU), simplifies development for the completion of Rue Terre and also changes
language for income tax purposes contained in the MOU.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes”
-- all members of the governing body present.  Main motion as  amended by substitute carried.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present. (#5536 RES/#6091 CON)
RESOLUTION - A uthorizing submission of an application to the Wyoming Business
Council, Business Ready Community Grant and Loan Program for funding for the construction
of a rail spur within the Swan Ranch Industrial Park.  Mr. Brown moved to adopt, seconded by
Mr. Shanor.  Mr. Brown moved to amend by substitute resolution and revised affiliated Project
Development and Administration Agreement (dated Nov. 12, 2013), seconded by Ms. Broyles.
Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.  Mr. Shanor
moved to postpone to November 25, 2013, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Scott Sutherland, Granite Peak
Development, expressed his concerns with a postpone ment, noting the upc oming deadline for
submitting the application to the Wyoming Business Council (WBC), funding availability risk,
and concern with a delay  affecting clients they  are working with who are  affiliated with the
project.  Anya Bendel, Business Development Director, Cheyenne LEADS, voiced support for the
project and reiterated the urgency in approving the resolution as soon as possible due to WBC
funding (biennium) ava ilability concerns.  Comments followed reg arding the purpose of
postponement, which is to provide City  Council members more time t o review provisions
contained in the revised agreement, which was received on this date; item being previously vetted
through City staff, attorneys and parties involved, and concern with effect on the project due to
a delay.  Upon inquiry regarding funding of the ra il spur proposal, May or Kaysen provided
information relative to the City acting as the “conduit” for the loan, which the City, as well as the
County, has done for other types of economic development purposes and which is allowed under
state law; information on the application re commendation and approval process involving the
WBC and State Land and Investment Board, and project funding and protections to the City.  In
response to inquiry  conce rning a def ault loan pay ment scena rio, Mr. Sutherland provided
information on what Granite P eak would be p utting up as co llateral for th e lo an and W BC
involvement in any default situation, stating any default will not affect the City.  Co mments
followed on wording in the resolution that authorizes submission of the application and whether
the governing body would be presented with the opportunity to review the loan documents if the
application were approved.  Upon inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney, clarified the resolution, as
currently written, would authorize sig ning of  the loan docume nts in the event the loan w as
approved, however, if the governing body desires to have the loan documents presented for review
prior to signature that could be achieved through a change to the last paragraph of the resolution.
Mr. White further r eviewed sever al p rotections for the City  that have be en wr itten into the
agreement.  Motion to postpone failed.  Voting “no” – all members of the governing body present.
In response to comment on the property’s location, Mr. Sutherland explained that approximately
half of the rail line will be within newly annexed city limits and half will be in the county, noting
that probable future annexation request will be made to extend additional property into the City.
Mr. Shanor moved to amend the last paragraph of the resolution beginning with “Be It Further
Resolved ...” by  inserting the lang uage “after the governing body has authorized execution”,
seconded by Mr. B rown.  I t was noted the p roposed amending language would re sult in t he
following phrase: “ ... that the Mayor, or his designated representative, after the governing body
has authorized execution, is authorized to ex ecute a grant agreement and loan documents ...”.
Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” –  a ll members of the governing body present.  Main
motion as twice amended carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
(#5537 RES/ #6092 CON)
[CA] RESOLUTION - Authorizing the submittal of an Application to the Wyoming State Loan
and Investment Board for a loan from the Wyoming Clean Water State Revolving fund for an
amount not to exceed $1.3 million for engineering costs for the Phase One Southern Se wer
Interceptor Main Project and Authorizing the Mayor, City Clerk and the Director of the Board of
Public Utilities to execute the application and all related loan documents.  Motion (per Consent
Agenda) carried. (#5538)
RESOLUTION - Authorizing the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming to finance Specific Purpose
Sales an d Us e T ax Election p rojects i ncluding b ut n ot l imited t o t he c osts o f t he p lanning,
construction, furnishing, equipping, and acquiring materials for improvement of land, including
the Public Safety Center  and the payment of lease service payments and the costs of the financed
project, and related thereto authorizing the execution and delivery of a Purchase Agreement, as
seller, with U.S. B ank National Association, as buyer, and a lea se with option to purchase , as
lessee, with U.S. Bank National Association, as lessor, with respect to the sale and lease of certain
real property as part of a financing arrangement and ratifying, approving and  confirming previous
actions of officers and members of the Governing Body of the City and employees thereof.  Mr.
Brown moved to adopt, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  During discussion, Lois Huff, City Treasurer,
explained the city has been trying to find a mechanism to fund large Specific Purpose projects,
including the Public Safety Center, prior to the collection of the entire amount of the tax.  She
provided information on proposed financing arrangements for the Public Safety Building which
will involving selling the building to U.S. Bank and leasing it back with financial arrangements
structured as a line of credit.  Staff was commended on the innovative financing mechanism being
proposed.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present. (#5539
RES/#6093 CON)

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2013-11-25 | CLIP_ID: 403 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 403

 RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
 November 25, 2013
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Shanor,
Mr. V aldez an d M s. Willi ams.  Al so pr esent: Ca rol I ntlekofer, City  Cle rk; L ois Hu ff, Ci ty
Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to
be routine items by  the governing body and will be ena cted by one motion.  There  will be no
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support
by two other membe rs is rec eived.  Any  item removed f rom the Co nsent Agenda will be
considered in its normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Shanor, and pursuant to
support received by Mr. Allen and Dr. Rinne, Agenda Item #25 was removed from the Consent
Agenda.  Upon request by Mr. Brown, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Allen and Mr.
Luna, Agenda Items #21 and  #22 were removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon request by Dr.
Rinne, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Allen and Mr. Brown, Agenda Item #26 was
removed from the Consent A genda.  Mr. Allen move d to approve remaining Consent Agenda
items, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing
body.
CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on November 12, 2013.  Motion
(per Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest in voucher payable to Ameri-
Tech HVAC Services) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Valdez moved to approve payment
of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Upon inquiry, Vicki Nemecek, Public Works
Department, clarified a voucher payable to Doug’s Towing was for towing services involving
residents’ vehicles in a scheduled construction work area.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the
vote, Mr. Brown returned to Council Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive comments and input for a transfer of
ownership for a retail liquor license application filed with the City Clerk's Office for Wadsworth
Old Chicago, Inc. d/b/a Old Chicago Restaurant, 1734 Meadowland Drive, Cheyenne, Wyoming
(License currently held by OC Cheyenne, LLC d/b/a Old Chicago Restaurant, 1734 Meadowland
Drive, Cheyenne, Wyoming).  There being no comments and purpose of the public hearing having
been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing and referred the application to Finance Committee.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive comments and input for a transfer of
ownership for a retail liquor license application filed with the City Clerk's Office for High Plains
Entertainment, Inc. d/b/a Redwood Lounge and Package Liquor, 2105 E. Lincolnway, Cheyenne,
Wyoming (License currently held by Wigwam, Inc. d/b/a Redwood Lounge and Package Liquor,
2105 East L incolnway, Cheyenne, Wy oming).  Upon inquiry , Br yan Gr zegorczyk, curr ent
licensee, advised Kevin Lesperance, High Plains Entertainment, Inc., is purchasing the business.
Mr. Lesperance advised he has worked in the liquor retail marketing business and is in the process
of moving to Cheyenne from Texas.  There being no further comments and purpose of the public
hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing and referred the application to Finance
Committee.
ORDINANCE - Amending the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (UDC), as approved
by Ordinance No. 3943 on January  23, 2012, to adopt am endments identified as Elec tion and
Ideological Sign A mendments from 2013 Annua l Rev iew.  Reporting for Public Service s
Committee, Mr. Allen moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Ms. Williams.
Mr. Allen moved to postpone to December 9, 2013, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  It was advised a
postponement will allow time to review additional information relative to proposed amendments.
Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
ORDINANCE - P ursuant to Section 2.2.1, Zoning  Map Amendme nts, Section 5.1.2,
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Unified Development
Code, changing the zoning classification from NR-3 Neighborhood Residential – High Density
District to MR-2 Medium Density Residential – Developing for Lots 1-10, Block 1, and Lots 1-10,
Block 2, Saddle Ridge, Sixth Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the southwest corner of the
intersection of Countryside Avenue and Medicine Man Trail).  Mr. Allen moved to approve on
second reading, seconded by Mr. L una.  Motion carried.  Voting  “yes” -- all member s of the
governing body.
ORDINANCE -  Pursuant to Section 2.2.1, Z oning Map Amendments, Section 5.1.2,
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the Cheyenne Unified
Development Co de, cha nging the  zoning classific ation from A- 1 Ag ricultural a nd Rural
Residential (County) to NR-2 Neighborhood Residential – Medium Density  for a  replat of a
portion of Blocks 80 and 81, formerly known as Interior Heights Addition, now vacated, being
a portion of the NE¼ of Section 7, T.13N., R.66W., 6th P.M., Cheyenne, Wyoming, to be known
as Shiraz Enclave (located east of and adjacent to Snyder Avenue, north of W. Allison Road).  Mr.
Allen moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Upon inquiry, Brad Emmons,
AVI Engineering and agent for the applicant, advised that currently there are no further meetings
with the neighborhood scheduled, and stated a new drainage study has been submitted as well as
an u pdated tr affic study .  H e sta ted a t th e c urrent t ime the  de veloper i s p roposing to b uild
townhouses, not single family homes.  Upon further inquiry, Mr. Emmons provided information
on drainage plans; storm sewer pipe, and possible formation of a home owners association (HOA)
to address maintenance responsibilities.  Shawn Ajdari, de veloper, advised c ovenants will be
drafted that will include homeowner’s responsibilities.  Mo tion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body with the exception of Ms. Williams voting “no”.
ORDINANCE -  Cr eating Cha pter 5 .78, To wing a nd Wrecker R ecovery Op erations,
Amending Chapter 5.04, Sec tion 5.04.03 0, Types of B usinesses, Activities and Eve nts, and
Chapter 5.08, Sec tion 5.08.270, Towing ( Vehicular) of  Title 5, Business L icenses and
Regulations, and Repealing in its entirety Section 10.12.190, Towing Firm Standards, of Chapter
10.12,  Administrative and Enforcement, of Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the Code of the City
of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Chief Brian Kozak, Police Department, provided a brief background on
the purpose of the proposed ordinance and explained the Police Department rotation call list.  He
reviewed calls for service; complaints received from citizens regarding towing charges; lack of
standardization of fees; requests from towing companies who want a “level playing field”; desire
to establish reasonable standards and rates, and meetings the Police Department has held with the
towing c ompanies and a sur vey completed by  the companie s that assisted with drafting  the
ordinance.  He reviewed various elements of the ordinance, including towing standards, response
times, state licenses a nd City  business license r equirements, operator training , storag e y ard
requirements, and time frame for vehicle “holds” relative to tows involving DUI incidents, and
advised the ordinance is based on best practices throughout the country and the towing industry.
Mike Basom, attorney representing Wyoming Professional Tow Association, spoke in opposition
to the ordinance and expressed concerns regarding legal interpretation regarding exemption from
federal r egulation; i nterpretation of  sta te sta tute re lative to a  sta te sa lvage y ard lic ense
requirement, and towing operator training.  Upon inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney, advised there
was no intention in the ordinance to grandfather existing towing firms relative to meeting the state
salvage license  require ments, and conf irmed that, under  the proposed or dinance, towing
companies must have a state salvage yard license to acquire the City business license.  In response
to q uery b y M r. B asom r egarding f requency a nd c ost o f tr aining, Ch ief K ozak p rovided
information on the frequency of Traffic Incident Management System (TIMS) training and who
would be trained, stating the Police Department will provide training at no cost to participants and
that owners/participants attending can the n train others.  Jodi S hea, attorney with Pence a nd
MacMillan, representing I-80 Towing and its affiliate towing companies, requested the ordinance
be tabled for further discussion.  She commented on day to day effects on towing companies the
ordinance will have, including financial concerns; safe, effective and efficient services; fees for
services, and common goals for improvements.  Ms. Shea proposed alternative solutions involving
tabling of the ordinance to allow towers to address issues and enable additional communication
efforts, and developing a committee to evaluate complaints, identify issues and propose solutions
to concerns that have been r aised.  Ms. Shea f urther discussed issues she fe lt need to be r e-
addressed, including fee schedules; requirement of being open four hours on weekends; forms of
payment for services; vehicle “hold” time; salvage license requirement, and personal property
contents within towed vehicles.  Randy  L eBeaumont, Randy’s Towing ; Bob Cairns, Rocky
Mountain Towing; Lawrence Anaya; M. Lee Hasenauer, Laramie County Commissioner; Todd
Brown, T &  T Auto Center, a nd Thomas Mullan, For t Towing an d I-80 Towing, expressed
concerns with the proposed ordinance and resolution setting a fee schedule for those on the Police
Department’s rotation ca ll lis t.  T opics discussed included: Opposition to the ordinanc e;
opposition to affiliated rate setting resolution; financial effect on company operations; differing
fees for certain scenarios involving towing services; uniformity and support for small businesses;
storage lot zoning and c ompliance; cur rent police  rotation list requirements a nd reg ulating
companies through rotation list enforcement; allowing towing companies to work together to
address issues and concerns; support for postponement and formation of a committee to study and
offer solutions to i ssues of conc ern, and upcoming meeting (invitation ex tended to g overning
body, law enforcement and others interested) of local towing companies to revamp the towers’
group and further discuss the proposed ordinance and call list rate resolution.  Speaking in favor
of the ordinance were Jaymi Lambert and Candy Kelch, citing personal experiences with high fees
for towing services, and Russ Fleetwood, M & F Towing, and Kathy Julius, Doug’s Towing, who
noted the ordinance is the result of bad towing practices by a few companies and commented in
support of providing some type of guidelines, standardization, and review of towing fees assessed
by some companies to prevent overcharging for services.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr.
Brown moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Mr. Brown moved to
amend b y s ubstitute, s econded b y M r. V aldez.  U pon in quiry, L t. M ark M unari, Po lice
Department, advised changes contained in the substitute were at the request of towing company
representatives, and re viewed the changes in the propose d substitute that involve insertion of
“Whereas” clauses explaining the purpose of the ordinance,  and mandated training requirement.
Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception
of Mr. Cook and Mr. Luna voting “no”.  Ensuing discussion on the main motion, as amended,
included the f ollowing topics: Support for a ddressing towing se rvices concerns to protec t the
community; elements of the ordinance involving timely response, types of payment allowed, and
salvage y ard r equirements; reg ulation of ce rtain towing  service s; stand ardizing towing f ees
through a reasonable rate structure for those on the rotation call list; current towing companies
being allowed to remain on the rotation list based on complaints that have been received; issue
of towing service fees being discussed at the state level; providing the towing association/group
the opportunity to offer suggestions on issues of concern that have been raised; storage of vehicles
relative to state statute, and working toward solutions through collaboration efforts.  In response
to query d uring d iscussion, Ch ief K ozak a dvised c urrently th ere is  n o a uthority to  e nforce
standards as it is s et through a resolution, not an ordinance; provided information on how the
proposed fee schedule rates (affiliated resolution) were derived and how future rate changes may
be made (through the governing body), and explained the current business license requirement
relative to rates that the applicant has to submit.  Main motion to approve on second reading, as
amended, carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.

(#5541)

[CA] RESOLUTION - Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a County Final Plat for

[CA] RESOLUTION - Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a County Final Plat for
Deerhaven Subdivision, 3rd Filing, a replat of Lot 1, Block 2, Deerhaven Subdivision, 2nd Filing,
Laramie County, Wyoming (located at the southeast corner of the intersection of Montalto Drive

RESOLUTION - Promulgating maximum approved towing rates for tows within the City
of Cheyenne when towing service is requested by the Cheyenne Police Department.  Lt. Mark
Munari, Police Department, explained research conducted regarding rates for different towing
services a round th e c ountry, a nd sta ted th e ma ximum pr oposed r ates f or no rmal tows is a
reasonable a mount, noting that only  those towing  companies w ho wish to be on the Police
Department rotation call list will have to comply with the set rates.  He provided information on
elements involved in different types of tows, and discussed meetings that had been held with
towing representatives to receive input on the proposed new ordinance and affiliated rat e
regulation.  Jodi Sh ea, attorney with Pence and MacMillan, representing I-80 Towing and its
affiliate towing companies; Thomas Mullan, I-80 Towing; Shane Swett, A-1 Recovery, Laramie,
and Bob Cairns, Rocky  Mountain Towing , expressed conc erns with propose d imposit ion of
maximum fees that could be assessed by towing companies for services requested through the
Police Department call list.  Discussion topics included:  Different towing scenarios; regulation
not being necessary; favorable reputation of the majority of towing firms providing local services;
flexibility provided in proposed rates for adjustments due to towing circumstances; review of rates
for different types of tows and equipment involved; current City regulations; insurance and vehicle
owners' option to file claims with insurance c arriers to cove r towing costs; conseque nces and
expenses to towing companies when vehicle owners fail to pay, and current requirements to be
eligible for placement on the Police Department rotation call list.  During comments, Lt. Munari
provided information on fee schedules submitted by towing firms per current City resolution in
place and the difference enforcement-wise between an ordinance and a resolution; reviewed other
communities' towing rates, and stated that the new proposed affiliated towing ordinance will not
force any company to be on the department call list.  Mr. Brown moved to adopt, seconded by Mr.
Valdez.  Mr. Brown moved to postpone to December 9, 2013, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  It was
advised postponement will allow for action on the item to coincide  with the next age nda item
(Resolution repealing towing services standards) and the proposed towing ordinance acted upon
earlier in the meeting.  Pursuant to inquiry by Mr. Mullan regarding the postponement process
Mayor Kaysen advised if the motion to postpone passed, the r esolution, along with the next
agenda item if it also we re approved to be postponed, would be reviewed at the next Finance
Committee meeting, which would be on December 2nd beginning at 11:00 a.m. in meeting Room
122, and verified the meeting is open to the public.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting "yes" --
all members of the governing body.
RESOLUTION - Repealing Resolution No. 2463 and Resolution No. 2616, pertaining to
towing services standards and standards for operations, for those towing firms approved to be on
the Cheyenne Police Department rotation call list, in their entirety.  Mr. Brown moved to adopt,
seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Mr. Brown moved to postpone to December 9, 2013, seconded by Mr.
Valdez.  I t was advised postponement will allow for ac tion on the item to coincide with the
previous agenda item and the proposed towing ordinance.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting
"yes" -- all members of the governing body.
Following a ten minute recess, Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at 8:13 p.m.
RESOLUTION - Requiring the Mayor to submit to the Governing Body a five-year Capital
Improvement Plan that prioritizes expenditures on capital investments by department.  Mr. Brown
reported there was no recommendation on the item from Finance Committee.  Mr. Brown moved
to adopt, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Comment followed on the purpose of the resolution, which
is to identify capital projects, funding, and prioritization of projects as an ongoing plan.  Motion
carried.  Voting "yes" -- all members of the governing body. (#5545)
[CA] RESOLUTION - In Support of the Creation of a Presidential Youth Council that will serve
as the voice of young people across the United States.  Motion (per Consent Ag enda) carried.
(#5546)
RESOLUTION - Adopting the Fox Farm Corridor Plan.  Boyd Wiggam, Wyoming Liberty
Group, expressed his belief that ther e may be a pote ntial property rights issue and sug gested
conditioning approval of the  plan upon additional input being  obtained fr om applicable land
owners with regard to any future improvements, as noted in the plan, at the intersection of Fox
Farm Rd., Morrie Ave. and Avenue C.  Kevin Burke, Holly Frontier, advised if the option of a
roundabout is implemented in the future at the Fox Farm and Avenue C intersection, it will not
allow for certain truck traffic (long trucks with trailers) to navigate through it enroute to/from the
refinery and if that traffic were re-routed to the alternate routes it would affect traffic flows and
delays.  Mr. Burke advised if the intersection were to be controlled, it should be through use of
a lighted intersection.  M. L ee Hasenauer, Laramie County Commissioner and member of the
South S ide Deve lopment Boa rd, advised ne ighbors in the are a ar e ag ainst any  proposed
roundabout, and stated the County Commissioners approved the plan with recommendations and
requested City Council approve with the same recommendations.  Commissioner Hasenauer also
offered suggestions on other portions of the plan involving bike lanes and traffic flow.  Mr. Allen
moved to adopt, sec onded by Mr. Shanor.  Mr. Shanor  moved to amend by  substitute dated
11/25/13, seconded by Mr. Allen.  It was advised the proposed amendment includes 3 conditions,
2 of which are identical to those adopted by the County Commissioners, and would also provide
(condition #3) that the governing body reserves the right to deviate from the plan.  Tom Mason,
MPO Director, advised that Tom Cobb and Brad Emmons, AVI Engineering, assisted with the
corridor plan; clar ified the City  Planning Commission recommende d adoption of the plan a s
presented (without any amendments), and expressed his opinion that condition #3 may  not be
needed as wording in the Resolution's Be It Resolved clause indicates the plan, if adopted, would
be a "guide" for future corridor improvements.  Tom Cobb, AVI, advised the plan does not make
a recommendation for the Avenue C intersection, leaving options open for further evaluation, and
indicated information in the plan on truck routes, as referenced by Mr. Burke, was obtained from
Holly Frontier.  During comments, Mr. Brown raised a point of order that discussion pertain to
the topic affiliated with the motion to amend on the floor with Mayor Kaysen concurring.  Mr.
Wiggam spoke in support of the f irst two conditions in the proposed motion to amend, a nd
Commissioner Hasenauer commented on truck traffic in the corridor affecting small businesses.
Mr. Brown raised another point of order that discussion remain germaine to the motion to amend
with Mayor Kaysen concurring.  Mayor Kaysen reviewed the content of the 3 conditions in the
proposed amendment.  Discussion included the following topics:  Additional time to review the
topics contained in the proposed amendment and procedural review by the City Attorney; oral vs.
written a mendments; in tent o f t he c onditions; s idewalk safety; tr affic vo lume; bi ke la nes;
corridor's location within the City and County, and City and County involvement in determining
corridor improvements.  Upon inquiry regarding the Fox Far m corridor's location, Mr. Mason
stated that of the portion included in the study approximately 70% of the existing corridor is in
the County and 30% is within the City.  Motion to amend failed.  Voting "yes" -- Mr. Allen, Mr.
Cook, Mr. Shanor and Ms. Willi ams.  Voting "no" -- Mr. B rown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Luna, Dr.
Rinne, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Comment followed on conc erns with the r oundabout
option, bike path safety and beautification of the area.  Main motion failed (tie vote).  Voting "yes"
-- Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Cook, Mr. Shanor and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting "no" -- Mr. Allen,
Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Valdez and Ms. Williams.   Under other business later in the meeting,
Dr. Rinne moved to reconsider the action taken on the main motion, seconded by Ms. Broyles.
Upon inquiry, Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, confirmed Dr. Rinne had voted on the prevailing side
and was eligible to make the motion, and read the entitlement of the Resolution for the record.
Buck Holmes, Laramie County Commissioner, expressed support for the motion.  Following brief
discussion on the purpose of reconsideration, motion to reconsider carried.  Voting "yes" -- all
members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Luna and Ms. Williams voting "no."
Ms. Broyles moved to adopt the resolution, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Following comment that past
practice of the  g overning body  h as been to postpone items that have bee n approve d for
reconsideration under other  business, Dr. Rinne moved to postpone to Dec ember 9, 2013,
seconded by Mr. Allen.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting "yes" -- all members of the governing
body with the exception of Mr. Luna voting "no".  Mayor Kaysen advised the item would be back
before the Public Services Committee at its December 3, 2013 meeting which begins at noon in
Room 122.
RESOLUTION - Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk of the City of Cheyenne to enter

acquiring the land for a park.  He noted parks, open spaces and school grounds currently in the

areas, and expressed concern with the cost of purchasing the property.  Mr. Brown moved to
adopt, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  D iscussion included the following topics:  Unavailability to
utilize school grounds at times; recreational needs of the Buffalo Ridge neighborhood and concern
with park amenities as part of detention ponds, and area population density.  Upon inquiry, Rick
Parish, Parks and Recreation Director, provided information on resources relative to operation and
maintenance costs and staffing; PlanCheyenne identifying the need for a neighborhood park in the

of the p ark.  Motion carried.  Voting   "y es" -- a ll members of the g overning body with the
exception of Mr. Luna voting "no". (Res#5547/Cont#6096)
